```
1099	## --scope continuation (0.41.0)
1100	
1101	Continues the foundation laid in 0.40.0 by wiring
1102	`--scope` into the remaining commands.
1103	
1104	**Cycle steps (initial sketch).**
1105	
1106	- **0.41.0-0** — this plan + version bump. Notes only.
1107	- **0.41.0-1** — interlude (unrelated to `--scope`):
1108	  pre-commit checklist requires `--locked` for
1109	  `cargo install --path .`. See subsection below.
1110	- **0.41.0-2** — `vc-x1 sync --scope=…`. Sync currently
1111	  hard-codes `[".", ".claude"]` as the default repo set;
1112	  scope drives that instead. Default-scope resolution
1113	  reads `.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`
1114	  (present + non-empty → `code,bot`, else `code`).
1115	- **0.41.0-3** — `vc-x1 push --scope=…`. Trickier: push's
1116	  state machine assumes dual. Likely splits into a
1117	  single-repo path (skip `commit-claude` + bookmark for
1118	  `.claude` + `finalize-claude`) plus a scope-aware
1119	  preflight summary.
1120	- **0.41.0-4** — `vc-x1 finalize --scope` review.
1121	  Finalize already takes `--repo`; check whether `--scope`
1122	  adds anything or if this step becomes a no-op /
1123	  documentation-only entry.
1124	- **0.41.0 (final)** — cycle close-out + dogfood
1125	  validation on a single-repo project (e.g. apply the
1126	  full sync→edit→push flow against a `--scope=code`
1127	  fixture).
1128	
1129	**Default-scope resolution.** The rule was sketched in
1130	[60] using `app|other|both` vocabulary; restating in the
1131	shipped vocabulary:
1132	
1133	- No `.vc-config.toml` → `--scope=code`.
1134	- `.vc-config.toml` with `other-repo` missing or empty
1135	  → `--scope=code`.
1136	- `.vc-config.toml` with non-empty `other-repo`
1137	  → `--scope=code,bot`.
1138	
1139	**Scope mismatch warnings.** When the resolved scope
1140	disagrees with the working-copy state (e.g. `--scope=code`
1141	in a workspace whose `.vc-config.toml` says dual), emit a
1142	warning so the user can confirm or back out — do not
1143	silently proceed. Fatal vs warn is sub-step decision.
1144	
1145	**Plan is exploratory.** Sub-step boundaries can shift
1146	once each command's call sites are seen. Push in
1147	particular may want its own `-N`-internal state; allow
1148	the cycle to lengthen if needed.
1149	
1150	### 0.41.0-1: pre-commit checklist requires --locked
1151	
1152	Notes-only interlude, unrelated to `--scope`. Hit a
1153	`cargo install --path .` build break on a clean
1154	worktree where `cargo build --release` and
1155	`cargo test --release` both passed.
1156	
1157	Full background — including the upstream cargo issues
1158	and the internals.rust-lang.org design-debate thread —
1159	lives in [notes/cargo-locked-issue.md](cargo-locked-issue.md).
1160	The summary below is enough for the chores log; defer
1161	to that file for the linked references.
1162	
1163	- Multiple `winnow` versions (0.7.x and 1.0.x) in the
1164	  fresh dep graph caused a type mismatch inside
1165	  `gix-object 0.58.0` (its `parse` fn calls
1166	  `gix_actor::signature::decode` whose
1167	  `ErrMode<E>` came from a different `winnow`).
1168	- Root cause: `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock`
1169	  by default and re-resolves from scratch, picking
1170	  the newest semver-compatible version of every
1171	  dep independently. The lockfile happens to pin
1172	  one `winnow` that satisfies all gix-* crates; a
1173	  fresh resolve no longer can. `cargo build` /
1174	  `cargo test` use `Cargo.lock` so they're immune.
1175	- Fix: `cargo install --path . --locked`. Cargo has
1176	  no stable config key or env var to make `--locked`
1177	  the default for `cargo install` (verified against
1178	  the cargo config reference), so the discipline
1179	  has to live in the checklist.
1180	
1181	Edits:
1182	
1183	- `Cargo.toml`: bump to `0.41.0-1`.
1184	- `CLAUDE.md`: pre-commit checklist step 4 requires
1185	  `--locked`, with a two-line reason inline so a
1186	  future reader doesn't re-litigate.
1187	- `notes/chores-06.md`: this subsection + plan
1188	  renumber (sync moves from `-1` to `-2`, push to
1189	  `-3`, finalize to `-4`).
1190	- `notes/cargo-locked-issue.md`: new standalone
1191	  background doc with the failing build output, the
1192	  asymmetry table, and links to the upstream cargo
1193	  issues + community design discussion.
1194
```

```
1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` wraps the parsed list; a missing flag (None at the arg
5	//!   layer) means "caller decides", typically via workspace-state
6	//!   default resolution.
7	//! - See `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope across commands
8	//!   (0.40.0)` for the full rationale and the vocabulary history.
9	
10	use clap::ValueEnum;
11	
12	/// One side of the workspace.
13	///
14	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
15	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
16	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
17	pub enum Side {
18	    Code,
19	    Bot,
20	}
21	
22	/// Parsed `--scope` value: the set of sides the command applies to.
23	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
24	pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>);
25	
26	impl Scope {
27	    /// True when the scope includes the code side.
28	    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {
29	        self.0.contains(&Side::Code)
30	    }
31	
32	    /// True when the scope includes the bot side.
33	    pub fn has_bot(&self) -> bool {
34	        self.0.contains(&Side::Bot)
35	    }
36	
37	    /// Exactly the code side — single-repo operation.
38	    pub fn is_code_only(&self) -> bool {
39	        self.has_code() && !self.has_bot()
40	    }
41	
42	    /// Exactly the bot side — single-repo operation on the bot.
43	    pub fn is_bot_only(&self) -> bool {
44	        !self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
45	    }
46	
47	    /// Both sides — dual-repo operation.
48	    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {
49	        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
50	    }
51	
52	    /// Neither side — empty scope, always an invalid input.
53	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
54	        self.0.is_empty()
55	    }
56	}
57	
58	#[cfg(test)]
59	mod tests {
60	    use super::*;
61	
62	    #[test]
63	    fn code_only() {
64	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code]);
65	        assert!(s.is_code_only());
66	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
67	        assert!(!s.is_both());
68	    }
69	
70	    #[test]
71	    fn bot_only() {
72	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Bot]);
73	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
74	        assert!(s.is_bot_only());
75	        assert!(!s.is_both());
76	    }
77	
78	    #[test]
79	    fn both_code_then_bot() {
80	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]);
81	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
82	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
83	        assert!(s.is_both());
84	    }
85	
86	    #[test]
87	    fn both_bot_then_code() {
88	        // Order doesn't matter — contains-based checks.
89	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code]);
90	        assert!(s.is_both());
91	    }
92	
93	    #[test]
94	    fn empty() {
95	        let s = Scope(vec![]);
96	        assert!(s.is_empty());
97	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
98	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
99	        assert!(!s.is_both());
100	    }
101	}
102
```

```
1	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
2	use std::process::Command;
3	
4	use clap::Args;
5	use log::{LevelFilter, debug, info, warn};
6	
7	use crate::common::run;
8	
9	/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.
10	///
11	/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any
12	/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually
13	/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every
14	/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.
15	///
16	/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
17	/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,
18	/// explicit flags lock in the contract.
19	///
20	/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
21	/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects
22	/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.
23	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
24	pub struct SyncArgs {
25	    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.
26	    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.
27	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "no_check")]
28	    pub check: bool,
29	
30	    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.
31	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "check")]
32	    pub no_check: bool,
33	
34	    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)
35	    #[arg(short, long)]
36	    pub quiet: bool,
37	
38	    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo
39	    #[arg(long, default_value = "main")]
40	    pub bookmark: String,
41	
42	    /// Remote to sync against
43	    #[arg(long, default_value = "origin")]
44	    pub remote: String,
45	
46	    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`]
47	    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
48	    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
49	}
50	
51	/// Default repo set when `-R` isn't given — the canonical dual-repo pair.
52	const DEFAULT_REPOS: [&str; 2] = [".", ".claude"];
53	
54	/// Resolve the caller's `-R` flags into a concrete repo list.
55	///
56	/// Empty input (no `-R` given) falls back to `DEFAULT_REPOS`. Each
57	/// provided value is also split on `,` and trimmed, so
58	/// `-R .,.claude` works identically to `-R . -R .claude`.
59	fn resolve_repos(raw: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
60	    if raw.is_empty() {
61	        return DEFAULT_REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();
62	    }
63	    raw.iter()
64	        .flat_map(|p| {
65	            p.to_string_lossy()
66	                .split(',')
67	                .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
68	                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
69	        })
70	        .collect()
71	}
72	
73	/// Relationship between a local bookmark and its remote counterpart.
74	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
75	pub enum State {
76	    /// Local and remote point at the same commit.
77	    UpToDate,
78	    /// Local is a strict ancestor of remote — fast-forward possible.
79	    Behind { local: String, remote: String },
80	    /// Remote is a strict ancestor of local — nothing to pull in.
81	    Ahead { local: String, remote: String },
82	    /// Neither is an ancestor of the other — needs rebase.
83	    Diverged { local: String, remote: String },
84	    /// The bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart.
85	    NoRemote,
86	}
87	
88	/// Per-repo context accumulated between the snapshot and action phases.
89	#[derive(Debug)]
90	struct RepoCtx {
91	    path: PathBuf,
92	    #[allow(dead_code)]
93	    op_id: String,
94	    state: State,
95	}
96	
97	/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
98	///
99	/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a
100	/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and
101	/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly
102	/// with absolute fixture paths.
103	///
104	/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped
105	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so
106	/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed
107	/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /
108	/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.
109	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
110	    let repos = resolve_repos(&args.repos);
111	    if args.quiet {
112	        let prev = log::max_level();
113	        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);
114	        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);
115	        log::set_max_level(prev);
116	        result
117	    } else {
118	        sync_repos(&repos, args)
119	    }
120	}
121	
122	/// Sync the given repos against their remotes.
123	///
124	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
125	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
126	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
127	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
128	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
129	///
130	/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or
131	/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence
132	/// under parallel `cargo test`.
133	pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
134	    debug!(
135	        "sync: enter (no_check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
136	        args.no_check, args.bookmark, args.remote
137	    );
138	
139	    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any
140	    // fetch/rebase. Implements "Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection"
141	    // — design at:
142	    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design
143	    for repo in repos {
144	        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &args.bookmark)?;
145	    }
146	
147	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
148	    for repo in repos {
149	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
150	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
151	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
152	    }
153	
154	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);
155	
156	    if let Err(e) = &result {
157	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
158	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
159	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
160	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
161	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
162	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
163	            }
164	        }
165	    }
166	
167	    debug!("sync: exit");
168	    result
169	}
170	
171	/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).
172	///
173	/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the
174	/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid
175	/// end state for this command.
176	fn run_plan(
177	    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],
178	    args: &SyncArgs,
179	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
180	    // Phase 1 — fetch + classify silently.
181	    //
182	    // Each repo's fetch stderr is captured (rather than streamed to
183	    // `info!` via `common::run`) so we can decide after classification
184	    // whether to surface it. `jj git fetch`'s routine "Nothing
185	    // changed." chatter is the main thing we're suppressing here —
186	    // if nothing needs action, the user shouldn't see it.
187	    let mut fetched: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
188	    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();
189	    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {
190	        let stderr = fetch_silent(repo, &args.remote)?;
191	        fetched.push((repo.clone(), stderr));
192	        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;
193	        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {
194	            path: repo.clone(),
195	            op_id: op_id.clone(),
196	            state,
197	        });
198	    }
199	
200	    let any_action_needed = ctxs
201	        .iter()
202	        .any(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }));
203	
204	    // Phase 2 — emit status. `--quiet` is enforced globally via the
205	    // log-level clamp in `sync()`, so these `info!` calls are already
206	    // suppressed in scripts; we just shape the output here.
207	    if !any_action_needed {
208	        let n = ctxs.len();
209	        let noun = if n == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
210	        info!("sync: {n} {noun}, all bookmarks up-to-date");
211	    } else {
212	        for (repo, stderr) in &fetched {
213	            info!("{}: fetch {}", repo.display(), args.remote);
214	            for line in stderr.lines() {
215	                info!("{line}");
216	            }
217	        }
218	        for ctx in &ctxs {
219	            log_state(&ctx.path, &ctx.state);
220	        }
221	    }
222	
223	    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).
224	    // `act_on_state` and `ensure_at_on_main` short-circuit when
225	    // `args.no_check` is false, so check mode is a true no-op here.
226	    for ctx in &ctxs {
227	        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;
228	        ensure_at_on_main(&ctx.path, &args.bookmark, args.no_check)?;
229	    }
230	
231	    // Phase 4 — check mode is fatal when action would be needed.
232	    // Apply mode (`--no-check`) ran the action above and is done.
233	    if !args.no_check && any_action_needed {
234	        let n_action = ctxs
235	            .iter()
236	            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))
237	            .count();
238	        let noun = if n_action == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
239	        return Err(format!(
240	            "sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \
241	             resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run"
242	        )
243	        .into());
244	    }
245	    Ok(())
246	}
247	
248	/// Fetch `repo` from `remote` without streaming subprocess output to
249	/// `info!`.
250	///
251	/// Mirrors what `common::run` would do, but returns stderr to the
252	/// caller so the caller can decide whether to surface it (verbose /
253	/// action case) or drop it (clean case). Stdout is dropped — `jj git
254	/// fetch` doesn't use it. Failure carries stderr in the error message.
255	fn fetch_silent(repo: &Path, remote: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
256	    debug!("$ jj git fetch --remote {remote} -R {}", repo.display());
257	    let output = Command::new("jj")
258	        .args(["git", "fetch", "--remote", remote, "-R", &repo_str(repo)])
259	        .output()
260	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj git fetch: {e}"))?;
261	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
262	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
263	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
264	        debug!("  {stdout}");
265	    }
266	    if !output.status.success() {
267	        return Err(format!("jj git fetch -R {} failed: {stderr}", repo.display()).into());
268	    }
269	    Ok(stderr)
270	}
271	
272	/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.
273	///
274	/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote
275	/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still
276	/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is
277	/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl
278	/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a
279	/// stale branch when the remote advanced.
280	///
281	/// Skipped when `main::@` is already non-empty (i.e., `@` is already
282	/// reachable from `bookmark`). On `--no-check`, runs
283	/// `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` which carries all commits between
284	/// the old parent and `@` forward, then checks `conflicts()` — any
285	/// conflict in this step trips the outer revert the same way a
286	/// conflicted `act_on_state` rebase would.
287	fn ensure_at_on_main(
288	    repo: &Path,
289	    bookmark: &str,
290	    apply: bool,
291	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
292	    if revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{bookmark}::@"))? {
293	        return Ok(());
294	    }
295	    info!("{}: rebasing @ onto '{bookmark}'", repo.display());
296	    if apply {
297	        run(
298	            "jj",
299	            &["rebase", "-b", "@", "-d", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str(repo)],
300	            Path::new("."),
301	        )?;
302	        if has_conflicts(repo)? {
303	            return Err(format!(
304	                "{}: rebase of @ onto '{bookmark}' produced conflicts",
305	                repo.display()
306	            )
307	            .into());
308	        }
309	    }
310	    Ok(())
311	}
312	
313	/// Perform the mutation corresponding to `ctx.state` when `--no-check`.
314	///
315	/// - `UpToDate` / `Ahead` / `NoRemote` → no-op (and no output, the state
316	///   was already logged by `log_state`).
317	/// - `Behind` → `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` to fast-forward.
318	/// - `Diverged` → `jj rebase -b <b> -d <b>@<remote>`, then probe
319	///   `conflicts()`. A non-empty result means the rebase produced
320	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
321	///   pre-fetch state.
322	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
323	    let repo = &ctx.path;
324	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", args.bookmark, args.remote);
325	    match &ctx.state {
326	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
327	        State::Behind { .. } => {
328	            if args.no_check {
329	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), args.bookmark);
330	                run(
331	                    "jj",
332	                    &[
333	                        "bookmark",
334	                        "set",
335	                        &args.bookmark,
336	                        "-r",
337	                        &remote_rev,
338	                        "-R",
339	                        &repo_str(repo),
340	                    ],
341	                    Path::new("."),
342	                )?;
343	            }
344	            Ok(())
345	        }
346	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
347	            if args.no_check {
348	                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list
349	                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head
350	                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The
351	                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence
352	                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head
353	                // and freshly-fetched remote head).
354	                let local_head = local
355	                    .split(',')
356	                    .find(|h| *h != remote)
357	                    .unwrap_or(local.as_str());
358	                info!(
359	                    "{}: rebasing {local_head} onto {remote_rev}",
360	                    repo.display()
361	                );
362	                run(
363	                    "jj",
364	                    &[
365	                        "rebase",
366	                        "-b",
367	                        local_head,
368	                        "-d",
369	                        &remote_rev,
370	                        "-R",
371	                        &repo_str(repo),
372	                    ],
373	                    Path::new("."),
374	                )?;
375	                if has_conflicts(repo)? {
376	                    return Err(format!("{}: rebase produced conflicts", repo.display()).into());
377	                }
378	            }
379	            Ok(())
380	        }
381	    }
382	}
383	
384	/// Print a single-line summary of `state` at `info!` level.
385	fn log_state(repo: &Path, state: &State) {
386	    let r = repo.display();
387	    match state {
388	        State::UpToDate => info!("{r}: up-to-date"),
389	        State::NoRemote => info!("{r}: no remote counterpart — skipping"),
390	        State::Ahead { local, remote } => {
391	            info!("{r}: ahead (local {local} > remote {remote}); nothing to sync")
392	        }
393	        State::Behind { local, remote } => {
394	            info!("{r}: behind (local {local} < remote {remote}); would fast-forward")
395	        }
396	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
397	            info!("{r}: diverged (local {local} vs remote {remote}); would rebase")
398	        }
399	    }
400	}
401	
402	/// Return the id of the most recent operation on `repo`.
403	///
404	/// Used as the revert target on failure. Exposed at `pub(crate)` so
405	/// `push` (0.37.0-2+) can reuse the same snapshot pattern for its
406	/// commit-stage rollback without duplicating the jj invocation.
407	pub(crate) fn current_op_id(repo: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
408	    let out = run(
409	        "jj",
410	        &[
411	            "op",
412	            "log",
413	            "--no-graph",
414	            "-n",
415	            "1",
416	            "-T",
417	            "id.short(12)",
418	            "-R",
419	            &repo_str(repo),
420	        ],
421	        Path::new("."),
422	    )?;
423	    Ok(out.trim().to_string())
424	}
425	
426	/// Restore `repo` to the operation identified by `op_id`.
427	///
428	/// Thin wrapper around `jj op restore`. Called during the failure
429	/// revert path — drops the caller's returned stdout. Exposed at
430	/// `pub(crate)` so `push` reuses the same restore call.
431	pub(crate) fn op_restore(repo: &Path, op_id: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
432	    run(
433	        "jj",
434	        &["op", "restore", op_id, "-R", &repo_str(repo)],
435	        Path::new("."),
436	    )?;
437	    Ok(())
438	}
439	
440	/// Classify the relationship between `bookmark` and `bookmark@remote`.
441	///
442	/// Uses `bookmarks(<b>)` rather than the bare name so a conflicted
443	/// bookmark (jj's representation of a diverged fetch) resolves to all
444	/// of its heads instead of erroring. When the set has multiple heads,
445	/// the bookmark is conflicted and the repo is `Diverged` by definition.
446	/// Otherwise we compare the single local head against the single
447	/// remote commit via two revset-ancestry probes.
448	///
449	/// Returns `NoRemote` when `<b>@<remote>` does not resolve — the caller
450	/// logs a skip and moves on.
451	fn classify(
452	    repo: &Path,
453	    bookmark: &str,
454	    remote: &str,
455	) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
456	    let local_heads = local_bookmark_heads(repo, bookmark)?;
457	    let remote_rev = format!("{bookmark}@{remote}");
458	    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {
459	        return Ok(State::NoRemote);
460	    };
461	    if local_heads.is_empty() {
462	        return Err(format!("{}: bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist", repo.display()).into());
463	    }
464	    if local_heads.len() > 1 {
465	        // Conflicted bookmark — jj's shape for post-fetch divergence.
466	        let local = local_heads.join(",");
467	        return Ok(State::Diverged { local, remote });
468	    }
469	    let local = local_heads.into_iter().next().unwrap(); // OK: `len() > 1` arm handled above, `is_empty()` handled above
470	    if local == remote {
471	        return Ok(State::UpToDate);
472	    }
473	    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{local}::{remote_rev}"))?;
474	    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{remote_rev}::{local}"))?;
475	    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {
476	        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },
477	        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },
478	        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },
479	    })
480	}
481	
482	/// Return all commit ids the `bookmark` currently points at.
483	///
484	/// Normally a one-element vector; two or more elements indicate a
485	/// conflicted bookmark (jj's representation of diverged post-fetch
486	/// state, where the local bookmark has multiple heads).
487	fn local_bookmark_heads(
488	    repo: &Path,
489	    bookmark: &str,
490	) -> Result<Vec<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
491	    let out = run(
492	        "jj",
493	        &[
494	            "log",
495	            "-r",
496	            &format!("bookmarks(exact:{bookmark})"),
497	            "--no-graph",
498	            "-T",
499	            r#"commit_id.short(12) ++ "\n""#,
500	            "-R",
501	            &repo_str(repo),
502	        ],
503	        Path::new("."),
504	    )?;
505	    Ok(out
506	        .lines()
507	        .map(|l| l.trim().to_string())
508	        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
509	        .collect())
510	}
511	
512	/// Return the short commit id that `rev` resolves to in `repo`.
513	///
514	/// Errors when the revset is unresolvable or matches multiple heads
515	/// — callers that want to tolerate "revset doesn't resolve" should use
516	/// `try_commit_id`.
517	fn commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
518	    let out = run(
519	        "jj",
520	        &[
521	            "log",
522	            "-r",
523	            rev,
524	            "--no-graph",
525	            "-T",
526	            "commit_id.short(12)",
527	            "-R",
528	            &repo_str(repo),
529	        ],
530	        Path::new("."),
531	    )?;
532	    Ok(out.trim().to_string())
533	}
534	
535	/// Like `commit_id`, but `Ok(None)` when the revset doesn't resolve.
536	///
537	/// jj reports missing revisions via stderr strings like
538	/// `Revision \`foo@origin\` doesn't exist` or `No such revision …`;
539	/// both get mapped to `Ok(None)` so callers can distinguish "missing"
540	/// from "other subprocess failure".
541	fn try_commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
542	    match commit_id(repo, rev) {
543	        Ok(id) if id.is_empty() => Ok(None),
544	        Ok(id) => Ok(Some(id)),
545	        Err(e) => {
546	            let msg = e.to_string();
547	            if msg.contains("doesn't exist") || msg.contains("No such revision") {
548	                Ok(None)
549	            } else {
550	                Err(e)
551	            }
552	        }
553	    }
554	}
555	
556	/// Return `true` when `revset` matches at least one commit in `repo`.
557	fn revset_nonempty(repo: &Path, revset: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
558	    let out = run(
559	        "jj",
560	        &[
561	            "log",
562	            "-r",
563	            revset,
564	            "--no-graph",
565	            "-T",
566	            r#"commit_id.short() ++ "\n""#,
567	            "-R",
568	            &repo_str(repo),
569	        ],
570	        Path::new("."),
571	    )?;
572	    Ok(!out.trim().is_empty())
573	}
574	
575	/// Return `true` when `repo` has any conflicted commits.
576	fn has_conflicts(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
577	    revset_nonempty(repo, "conflicts()")
578	}
579	
580	/// Convert a path to a `String` suitable for passing as a subprocess arg.
581	fn repo_str(p: &Path) -> String {
582	    p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()
583	}
584	
585	#[cfg(test)]
586	mod tests {
587	    use super::*;
588	
589	    /// Default flags: neither `--check` nor `--no-check` set (the
590	    /// implicit default is check mode), bookmark "main", remote "origin",
591	    /// no `-R` given (caller will fall back to `DEFAULT_REPOS`),
592	    /// `--quiet` off.
593	    #[test]
594	    fn parse_defaults() {
595	        use clap::Parser;
596	        #[derive(Parser)]
597	        struct Cli {
598	            #[command(flatten)]
599	            args: SyncArgs,
600	        }
601	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test"]).unwrap();
602	        assert!(!cli.args.check);
603	        assert!(!cli.args.no_check);
604	        assert!(!cli.args.quiet);
605	        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, "main");
606	        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, "origin");
607	        assert!(cli.args.repos.is_empty());
608	    }
609	
610	    /// `-q` / `--quiet` CLI form is honored.
611	    #[test]
612	    fn parse_quiet_flag() {
613	        use clap::Parser;
614	        #[derive(Parser)]
615	        struct Cli {
616	            #[command(flatten)]
617	            args: SyncArgs,
618	        }
619	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "--quiet"]).unwrap();
620	        assert!(cli.args.quiet);
621	        let cli_short = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "-q"]).unwrap();
622	        assert!(cli_short.args.quiet);
623	    }
624	
625	    /// Overrides: `--no-check`, `--bookmark`, `--remote` all honored.
626	    #[test]
627	    fn parse_overrides() {
628	        use clap::Parser;
629	        #[derive(Parser)]
630	        struct Cli {
631	            #[command(flatten)]
632	            args: SyncArgs,
633	        }
634	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from([
635	            "test",
636	            "--no-check",
637	            "--bookmark",
638	            "dev",
639	            "--remote",
640	            "upstream",
641	        ])
642	        .unwrap();
643	        assert!(cli.args.no_check);
644	        assert!(!cli.args.check);
645	        assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, "dev");
646	        assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, "upstream");
647	        assert!(cli.args.repos.is_empty());
648	    }
649	
650	    /// `--check` flag parses as the explicit form of the default.
651	    #[test]
652	    fn parse_check_flag() {
653	        use clap::Parser;
654	        #[derive(Parser)]
655	        struct Cli {
656	            #[command(flatten)]
657	            args: SyncArgs,
658	        }
659	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "--check"]).unwrap();
660	        assert!(cli.args.check);
661	        assert!(!cli.args.no_check);
662	    }
663	
664	    /// `--check` and `--no-check` together are rejected by clap.
665	    #[test]
666	    fn parse_check_no_check_conflict() {
667	        use clap::Parser;
668	        #[derive(Parser)]
669	        struct Cli {
670	            #[command(flatten)]
671	            args: SyncArgs,
672	        }
673	        assert!(Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "--check", "--no-check"]).is_err());
674	    }
675	
676	    /// Empty `-R` resolves to the dual-repo default pair.
677	    #[test]
678	    fn resolve_repos_default() {
679	        let resolved = resolve_repos(&[]);
680	        assert_eq!(resolved, vec![PathBuf::from("."), PathBuf::from(".claude")]);
681	    }
682	
683	    /// Single `-R` passthrough (single-repo project case).
684	    #[test]
685	    fn resolve_repos_single() {
686	        let raw = vec![PathBuf::from("/tmp/some-repo")];
687	        assert_eq!(resolve_repos(&raw), vec![PathBuf::from("/tmp/some-repo")]);
688	    }

690	    /// Repeated `-R` flags combine into the final list in order.
691	    #[test]
692	    fn resolve_repos_repeated() {
693	        let raw = vec![
694	            PathBuf::from("/a"),
695	            PathBuf::from("/b"),

708	    /// Comma-separated values inside a single `-R` are split and trimmed.
709	    #[test]
710	    fn resolve_repos_comma_separated() {
711	        let raw = vec![PathBuf::from(" /a , /b ,/c")];
712	        assert_eq!(

714	            vec![

717	                PathBuf::from("/c"),

719	        );

721	
722	    /// Mixed repeated + comma forms compose naturally.
723	    #[test]
724	    fn resolve_repos_mixed() {
725	        let raw = vec![PathBuf::from("/a,/b"), PathBuf::from("/c")];
726	        assert_eq!(

728	            vec![
729	                PathBuf::from("/a"),

732	            ]
733	        );

735	
736	    /// `-R path` CLI form is accepted for a single repo.
737	    #[test]
738	    fn parse_single_repo_flag() {
739	        use clap::Parser;
740	        #[derive(Parser)]
741	        struct Cli {
742	            #[command(flatten)]
743	            args: SyncArgs,
744	        }
745	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "-R", "/tmp/x"]).unwrap();
746	        assert_eq!(cli.args.repos, vec![PathBuf::from("/tmp/x")]);
747	    }
748	
749	    /// Repeated `-R` CLI form accumulates into the `repos` vec.
750	    #[test]
751	    fn parse_repeated_repo_flag() {
752	        use clap::Parser;
753	        #[derive(Parser)]
754	        struct Cli {
755	            #[command(flatten)]
756	            args: SyncArgs,
757	        }
758	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "-R", "/a", "-R", "/b"]).unwrap();
759	        assert_eq!(
760	            cli.args.repos,
761	            vec![PathBuf::from("/a"), PathBuf::from("/b")]
762	        );

764	}

766	#[cfg(test)]
767	mod integration_tests {
768	    //! End-to-end tests for `sync_repos` against real dual-repo jj
769	    //! fixtures. Each test builds an isolated fixture (bare-git
770	    //! remotes + colocated jj repos) under a unique tempdir via
771	    //! `Fixture::new` (driving `init::init_with_symlink` in
772	    //! `--repo-local` mode), then drives the scenario with plain
773	    //! `jj` subprocess calls. Requires `jj` in `PATH`.
774	    //!
775	    //! Fixtures clean themselves up via `Fixture`'s `Drop` impl so a
776	    //! panicking test still removes its tempdir.
777	
778	    use super::*;
779	    use crate::test_helpers::Fixture;
780	    use std::fs;
781	    use std::process::Command;
782	
783	    /// Run `jj <args> -R <repo>` and assert success; returns trimmed stdout.
784	    fn jj(repo: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> String {
785	        let out = Command::new("jj")
786	            .args(args)
787	            .arg("-R")
788	            .arg(repo)
789	            .output()
790	            .expect("spawn jj");
791	        assert!(
792	            out.status.success(),
793	            "jj {args:?} failed in {}: {}",
794	            repo.display(),
795	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
796	        );
797	        String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string()
798	    }
799	
800	    /// Resolve `rev` to its short commit id in `repo`.
801	    fn cid(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> String {
802	        jj(
803	            repo,
804	            &["log", "-r", rev, "--no-graph", "-T", "commit_id.short(12)"],
805	        )
806	    }
807	
808	    /// Sync args with `--no-check` set (apply mode).
809	    ///
810	    /// Integration tests pass explicit repo paths through `sync_repos`
811	    /// directly, so `repos` stays empty here and the CLI-side default
812	    /// resolution is not exercised by this helper.
813	    fn apply_args() -> SyncArgs {
814	        SyncArgs {
815	            check: false,
816	            no_check: true,
817	            quiet: false,
818	            bookmark: "main".to_string(),
819	            remote: "origin".to_string(),
820	            repos: Vec::new(),
821	        }
822	    }
823	
824	    /// Add a local-only commit on `main` in `repo` (not pushed), then
825	    /// restore `@` to an empty child so pre-flight still passes.
826	    ///
827	    /// Sequence: write file → describe `@` → advance `main` to `@` →
828	    /// create a fresh empty `@` above it.
829	    fn add_local_commit(repo: &Path, file: &str, content: &str, msg: &str) {
830	        fs::write(repo.join(file), content).expect("write local file");
831	        jj(repo, &["describe", "@", "-m", msg]);
832	        jj(repo, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
833	        jj(repo, &["new"]);
834	    }
835	
836	    /// Clone `remote_url` into `<base>/<work_name>`, add a commit, push it.
837	    ///
838	    /// Used to make the remote advance beyond the fixture's `main`
839	    /// from a separate working copy. Returns the pushed commit's id.
840	    fn push_from_clone(
841	        base: &Path,
842	        remote_url: &Path,
843	        work_name: &str,
844	        file: &str,
845	        content: &str,
846	        msg: &str,
847	    ) -> String {
848	        let workdir = base.join(work_name);
849	        let out = Command::new("jj")
850	            .args(["git", "clone", "--colocate"])
851	            .arg(remote_url)
852	            .arg(&workdir)
853	            .output()
854	            .expect("spawn jj clone");
855	        assert!(
856	            out.status.success(),
857	            "jj git clone failed: {}",
858	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
859	        );
860	        fs::write(workdir.join(file), content).expect("write remote file");
861	        jj(&workdir, &["describe", "@", "-m", msg]);
862	        jj(&workdir, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
863	        jj(&workdir, &["git", "push", "--bookmark", "main"]);
864	        cid(&workdir, "main")
865	    }
866	
867	    /// Scenario 1: fresh fixture, nothing to do — `sync` leaves both
868	    /// repos untouched.
869	    #[test]
870	    fn sync_up_to_date() {
871	        let fx = Fixture::new("up-to-date");
872	        let work_main = cid(&fx.work, "main");
873	        let claude_main = cid(&fx.claude, "main");
874	        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect("sync should succeed");
875	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, "main"), work_main);
876	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, "main"), claude_main);
877	    }
878	
879	    /// Scenario 2a: a non-empty `@` on top of main (simulates `/exit`
880	    /// trailing session writes in `.claude`) is tolerated when there's
881	    /// nothing new on the remote. `@`'s commit id changes because jj
882	    /// snapshots the written file, but its content is preserved and
883	    /// `@` stays reachable from main.
884	    #[test]
885	    fn sync_tolerates_trailing_at_up_to_date() {
886	        let fx = Fixture::new("trailing-uptodate");
887	        let pre_main = cid(&fx.claude, "main");
888	        fs::write(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl"), "{\"line\":1}\n").expect("write trailing file");
889	        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect("sync should succeed");
890	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, "main"), pre_main, "main should not move");
891	        let on_main = jj(
892	            &fx.claude,
893	            &[
894	                "log",
895	                "-r",
896	                "main::@",
897	                "--no-graph",
898	                "-T",
899	                r#"commit_id.short(12) ++ "\n""#,
900	            ],
901	        );
902	        assert!(!on_main.trim().is_empty(), "@ should stay on main's line");
903	        assert_eq!(
904	            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl")).unwrap(),
905	            "{\"line\":1}\n",
906	            "trailing content preserved"
907	        );
908	    }
909	
910	    /// Scenario 2b: `@` has trailing writes and the remote advanced
911	    /// while the session was offline. jj's fetch auto-ff's main but
912	    /// leaves `@` behind; sync must then rebase `@` onto the new main
913	    /// so the session tail doesn't end up orphaned. Content is
914	    /// preserved across the rebase.
915	    #[test]
916	    fn sync_rebases_trailing_at_when_main_moves() {
917	        let fx = Fixture::new("trailing-rebase");
918	        let remote_claude = fx.base.join("remote-claude.git");
919	        let remote_head = push_from_clone(
920	            &fx.base,
921	            &remote_claude,
922	            "claude2",
923	            "remote.md",
924	            "remote\n",
925	            "feat: remote-added",
926	        );
927	        // Trailing writes on @
928	        fs::write(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl"), "{\"line\":2}\n").expect("write trailing file");
929	        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect("sync should succeed");
930	        assert_eq!(
931	            cid(&fx.claude, "main"),
932	            remote_head,
933	            "local main should match remote after auto-ff"
934	        );
935	        // @ should now be a descendant of main.
936	        let on_main = jj(
937	            &fx.claude,
938	            &[
939	                "log",
940	                "-r",
941	                "main::@",
942	                "--no-graph",
943	                "-T",
944	                r#"commit_id.short(12) ++ "\n""#,
945	            ],
946	        );
947	        assert!(
948	            !on_main.trim().is_empty(),
949	            "@ should be reachable from main after ensure_at_on_main"
950	        );
951	        // Trailing content preserved on disk.
952	        assert_eq!(
953	            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl")).unwrap(),
954	            "{\"line\":2}\n",
955	            "trailing content preserved across @ rebase"
956	        );
957	    }
958	
959	    /// Scenario 2c: `@` has trailing writes and local+remote both
960	    /// modify the same file differently. Rebase produces conflicts;
961	    /// sync fails and op-restores both repos. The trailing content
962	    /// must survive the revert — jj documents that op restore rolls
963	    /// back operation history without clobbering the working copy.
964	    #[test]
965	    fn sync_conflict_preserves_trailing_at_on_revert() {
966	        let fx = Fixture::new("trailing-conflict");
967	        let remote_claude = fx.base.join("remote-claude.git");
968	        push_from_clone(
969	            &fx.base,
970	            &remote_claude,
971	            "claude2",
972	            "shared.txt",
973	            "remote-version\n",
974	            "feat: remote shared",
975	        );
976	        // Local commit on main with conflicting content
977	        add_local_commit(
978	            &fx.claude,
979	            "shared.txt",
980	            "local-version\n",
981	            "feat: local shared (conflicting)",
982	        );
983	        // Trailing writes on new @
984	        fs::write(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl"), "{\"line\":3}\n").expect("write trailing file");
985	
986	        let pre_main = cid(&fx.claude, "main");
987	        let pre_remote = cid(&fx.claude, "main@origin");
988	
989	        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args())
990	            .unwrap_err()
991	            .to_string();
992	        assert!(
993	            err.contains("conflicts"),
994	            "expected conflict error, got: {err}"
995	        );
996	
997	        // Post-revert: main and main@origin back to pre-sync.
998	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.claude, "main"), pre_main, "main reverted");
999	        assert_eq!(
1000	            cid(&fx.claude, "main@origin"),
1001	            pre_remote,
1002	            "main@origin reverted"
1003	        );
1004	        // No conflicts remain.
1005	        let conflicts = jj(
1006	            &fx.claude,
1007	            &[
1008	                "log",
1009	                "-r",
1010	                "conflicts()",
1011	                "--no-graph",
1012	                "-T",
1013	                r#"commit_id ++ "\n""#,
1014	            ],
1015	        );
1016	        assert!(
1017	            conflicts.trim().is_empty(),
1018	            "no conflicts should remain after revert"
1019	        );
1020	        // Trailing content preserved on disk.
1021	        assert_eq!(
1022	            fs::read_to_string(fx.claude.join("trailing.jsonl")).unwrap(),
1023	            "{\"line\":3}\n",
1024	            "trailing content preserved across op restore"
1025	        );
1026	    }
1027	
1028	    /// Scenario 3: local has commits not yet pushed; sync classifies
1029	    /// `ahead` and leaves the local bookmark alone even under
1030	    /// `--no-check`.
1031	    #[test]
1032	    fn sync_ahead_is_noop() {
1033	        let fx = Fixture::new("ahead");
1034	        add_local_commit(&fx.work, "local.txt", "local\n", "feat: local only");
1035	        let ahead_head = cid(&fx.work, "main");
1036	        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect("sync should succeed");
1037	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, "main"), ahead_head);
1038	    }
1039	
1040	    /// Scenario 4: clean divergence — both sides advance main on
1041	    /// different files; sync rebases local onto remote and the result
1042	    /// is conflict-free.
1043	    #[test]
1044	    fn sync_diverged_rebases() {
1045	        let fx = Fixture::new("diverged");
1046	        let remote_code = fx.base.join("remote-code.git");
1047	        let remote_head = push_from_clone(
1048	            &fx.base,
1049	            &remote_code,
1050	            "work2",
1051	            "remote.txt",
1052	            "remote\n",
1053	            "feat: remote only",
1054	        );
1055	        add_local_commit(&fx.work, "local.txt", "local\n", "feat: local only");
1056	
1057	        sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args()).expect("sync should succeed");
1058	
1059	        // Remote tracking bookmark now points at the pushed remote commit.
1060	        assert_eq!(
1061	            cid(&fx.work, "main@origin"),
1062	            remote_head,
1063	            "main@origin should match pushed remote commit"
1064	        );
1065	        // Local main is ahead of remote — rebased local commit sits on top.
1066	        let main_after = cid(&fx.work, "main");
1067	        assert_ne!(
1068	            main_after, remote_head,
1069	            "local main should be ahead of remote after rebase"
1070	        );
1071	        // Remote is an ancestor of local post-rebase.
1072	        let anc = jj(
1073	            &fx.work,
1074	            &[
1075	                "log",
1076	                "-r",
1077	                &format!("{remote_head}::{main_after}"),
1078	                "--no-graph",
1079	                "-T",
1080	                r#"commit_id.short(12) ++ "\n""#,
1081	            ],
1082	        );
1083	        assert!(
1084	            !anc.trim().is_empty(),
1085	            "remote should be ancestor of local after rebase"
1086	        );
1087	        // No conflicts.
1088	        let conflicts = jj(
1089	            &fx.work,
1090	            &[
1091	                "log",
1092	                "-r",
1093	                "conflicts()",
1094	                "--no-graph",
1095	                "-T",
1096	                r#"commit_id ++ "\n""#,
1097	            ],
1098	        );
1099	        assert!(conflicts.trim().is_empty(), "no conflicts expected");
1100	    }
1101	
1102	    /// Scenario 5: conflicting divergence — both sides modify the
1103	    /// same path differently. Rebase produces conflicts; sync fails
1104	    /// and reverts both repos to their pre-sync state.
1105	    #[test]
1106	    fn sync_diverged_conflict_reverts() {
1107	        let fx = Fixture::new("conflict");
1108	        let remote_code = fx.base.join("remote-code.git");
1109	        push_from_clone(
1110	            &fx.base,
1111	            &remote_code,
1112	            "work2",
1113	            "shared.txt",
1114	            "remote-version\n",
1115	            "feat: remote shared",
1116	        );
1117	        add_local_commit(
1118	            &fx.work,
1119	            "shared.txt",
1120	            "local-version\n",
1121	            "feat: local shared (conflicting)",
1122	        );
1123	
1124	        let pre_main = cid(&fx.work, "main");
1125	        let pre_remote = cid(&fx.work, "main@origin");
1126	
1127	        let err = sync_repos(&fx.repos(), &apply_args())
1128	            .unwrap_err()
1129	            .to_string();
1130	        assert!(
1131	            err.contains("rebase produced conflicts"),
1132	            "unexpected error: {err}"
1133	        );
1134	
1135	        // After revert: main and main@origin are back where they started.
1136	        assert_eq!(cid(&fx.work, "main"), pre_main, "main should be reverted");
1137	        assert_eq!(
1138	            cid(&fx.work, "main@origin"),
1139	            pre_remote,
1140	            "main@origin should be reverted (pre-fetch state)"
1141	        );
1142	        // No conflicts remain.
1143	        let conflicts = jj(
1144	            &fx.work,
1145	            &[
1146	                "log",
1147	                "-r",
1148	                "conflicts()",
1149	                "--no-graph",
1150	                "-T",
1151	                r#"commit_id ++ "\n""#,
1152	            ],
1153	        );
1154	        assert!(
1155	            conflicts.trim().is_empty(),
1156	            "no conflicts should remain after revert"
1157	        );
1158	    }
1159	}
1160
```

```
106	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
107	fn run_retry(
108	    cmd: &str,
109	    args: &[&str],
110	    cwd: &Path,
111	    retries: u32,
112	    delay_secs: u64,
113	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
114	    let mut last_err = String::new();
115	    for attempt in 1..=retries {
116	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
117	            Ok(out) => {
118	                if attempt > 1 {
119	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
120	                }
```

51	## Generalize --scope to all commands (design)
52	
53	Surfaced 2026-04-23 (from the user, top of todo): vc-x1 commands
54	should support both single and dual repo modes. The technique
55	sketched for `vc-x1 push` (`--scope=app|claude|both`) is the
56	right shape, but the scope concept should be a project-wide
57	convention rather than push-only.
58	
59	**Naming:** `--scope=app|other|both` — "other" rather than
60	"claude" because the second repo isn't always `.claude`; could
61	be a different bot or a different sibling project structure.
62	
63	**Why scope-first** (before adding more commands or push
64	features): each new command that operates across both repos
65	would otherwise need its own ad-hoc handling. Locking in the
66	convention now means new commands inherit it for free, and
67	existing commands (sync, finalize, push) get retrofitted under
68	the same vocabulary.
69	
70	**Rough scope of the change:**
71	
72	- Shared CLI flag definition + parsing helper (likely in
73	  `common.rs`).
74	- `sync`: already accepts `-R` for single-repo. Migrate to
75	  `--scope`; alias or deprecate `-R` (TBD below).
76	- `push`: `--scope` per the existing design, with
77	  warn-on-WC-mismatch.
78	- `finalize`: already takes `--repo`; rationalize naming.
79	- New commands (`status` etc.) start with `--scope` from day 1.
80	
81	**Open:** do we deprecate `-R` immediately, alias it, or keep
82	both indefinitely? Probably: alias for one minor cycle,
83	deprecate, remove. Decide before implementation.
84	
85	## Push hardening: state + stage sanity (design)
86	
87	Two related correctness fixes for `vc-x1 push` resume behavior.
88	Both surfaced 2026-04-22 from a 0.37.1 dogfood incident and are
89	described together because they tackle the same failure mode
90	(silent false success after stale state).
91	
92	**The incident:** 0.37.1 push errored at finalize-claude;
93	out-of-band recovery (manual finalize + force-push of a squash)
94	moved the world forward; 0.37.2 push then resumed at the parked
95	finalize-claude, no-op'd, and falsely declared "completed all
96	stages" while working copies still held uncommitted changes.
97	
98	**State-sanity preflight on resume.** Before any stage runs,
99	verify saved state matches reality:
100	
101	- `state.app_chid` still exists (not abandoned/rewritten).
102	- `main` bookmark at `state.app_chid`'s commit.
103	- `state.claude_chid` consistent with `.claude` working copy.
104	
105	On mismatch, refuse with a loud "state is stale — run
106	`vc-x1 push --restart`" error.
107	
108	**Stage-prereq verification + honest completion.** Each stage
109	declares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app;
110	bookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both; etc.); the
111	dispatcher checks before running. "Completed all stages" should
112	only print when stages genuinely ran or were
113	verified-already-done, not when they were skipped without
114	verification.
115	
116	**Implementation order:** state-sanity first (broader guard),
117	then per-stage prereqs (more invasive). Both should land before
118	the `--squash` work to avoid building on a known-fragile resume
119	path.
120	
121	## bm-track silent-when-clean (design)
122	
123	Today bm-track prints two lines per command (entry + exit)
124	unconditionally. In steady state both lines say
125	`tracked,tracked` — pure noise. Defer-flagged in 0.37.4 until
126	signal-confidence was established through more dogfood; several
127	0.37.x cycles have now used the probe with no false positives
128	or missed regressions.
129	
130	**Proposed behavior:**
131	
132	- Always probe on entry and exit (no change).
133	- Print on entry only when state isn't fully tracked.
134	- Print on exit when state isn't fully tracked OR when the exit
135	  state differs from entry.
136	- When printing on exit, include the entry state in the output
137	  so the transition is explicit:
138	  `bm-track vc-x1 <cmd>: enter=<state> → exit=<state>`.
139	
140	The entry-state-in-exit-output requirement is important —
141	"entry was ok (not printed), exit failed" is inference;
142	"entry=tracked, exit=NOT_TRACKED" in one line is evidence.
143	
144	**Implementation note:** record entry state in a local variable
145	at the enter call site and pass into the exit call.
146	
147	## Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection (design)
148	
149	Diagnosed 2026-04-22 dogfood: jj's tracking state is
150	**per-workspace** (local `.jj` store), not shared via git refs.
151	Sync across machines transfers refs but not the tracking flag —
152	so a fresh workspace fetched-into never auto-tracks. The failure
153	surfaces only when `jj git push` is attempted, which is too late
154	(push-app already succeeded in our case).
155	
156	**Policy (decided):** error loudly, with the exact
157	`jj bookmark track <b> --remote=<r> -R <repo>` remediation
158	command. No self-heal — keeps the fix explicit and visible.
159	
160	**Scope (every command that creates or mutates repo state):**
161	
162	- `vc-x1 init`: already correct (Step 10 calls `jj bookmark
163	  track`). Add the check as a post-condition sanity assertion
164	  anyway.
165	- `vc-x1 clone`: does `git clone` then `jj git init --colocate`.
166	  Whether that combination auto-establishes tracking in jj's
167	  workspace store is unclear; probably needs an explicit
168	  `jj bookmark track` for each cloned bookmark after the init.
169	  Verify and fix.
170	- `vc-x1 test-fixture`: Step 7's `jj git push --bookmark main`
171	  establishes tracking as a side effect of the first push.
172	  Works correctly — confirmed via `jj bookmark list --tracked`
173	  on a fresh fixture. No change needed; the post-condition
174	  sanity check would naturally cover it.
175	- `vc-x1 sync` preflight: detect + error.
176	- `vc-x1 push` preflight: detect + error (before any mutation).
177	- `vc-x1 finalize`: detect + error before the squash, so a
178	  failed push doesn't leave a half-finalized state.
179	
180	**Shared helper:** `common::verify_tracking(repo, bookmark,
181	remote) -> Result<(), Err>` or similar. Probably use
182	`jj bookmark list --tracked -T <template>` under the hood
183	rather than parsing human-readable output.
184	
185	## Scope design refinements (0.37.8)
186	
187	Notes-only release. Captures six refinements to the scope and
188	squash designs ([57], [60]) from a 2026-04-23 dogfood discussion
189	(the manual squash workflow used to fold late changes into the
190	0.37.7 commit, plus follow-up Q&A on cwd independence and
191	plain-old-repo handling).
192	
193	**Scope semantic invariant.** With `--scope=app|other|both`
194	(per [60]):
195	
196	- `--scope=both` (or omitted; `both` is the default) → dual-repo
197	  operation.
198	- `--scope=app` → single-repo operation on the app repo (direct
199	  reference).
200	- `--scope=other` → single-repo operation on the "other" repo
201	  (indirect reference; resolves via `.vc-config.toml`'s
202	  `[workspace].other-repo` field, which already exists).
203	
204	Anything that isn't `both` is single-repo. The dispatcher branches
205	once on this invariant; new commands inheriting `--scope` get the
206	semantic for free.
207	
208	**`--other` is project config, not a per-command flag.** Came up
209	during the same discussion: should there be a `--other=PATH` flag
210	to override the project's "other" repo per command? Answer: no.
211	That belongs in `.vc-config.toml` (already there as
212	`[workspace].other-repo`). Keeping `--other` out of the
213	per-command surface keeps the flag set small and the per-command
214	vocabulary uniform across all commands — "other" always resolves
215	the same way.
216	
217	**Cwd independence + counterpart-rename suggestion.** Scope names
218	are workspace-anchored, not cwd-relative. Each repo has its own
219	`.vc-config.toml` self-describing its role:
220	
221	- App's config: `[workspace] path = "/" other-repo = ".claude"`.
222	- `.claude`'s config: `[workspace] path = "/.claude"
223	  other-repo = ".."`.
224	
225	`path = "/"` identifies the app repo (only the app has it). The
226	dispatcher reads the local config, determines "am I app or other?"
227	via `path`, then resolves `--scope=app` and `--scope=other` to the
228	right physical paths regardless of cwd. From `cd .claude`:
229	
230	- `--scope=app` → `..` (the app repo, found via `.claude`'s
231	  `other-repo`).
232	- `--scope=other` → `.` (`.claude` itself, since `path != "/"`
233	  → I AM the workspace's "other").
234	
235	Naming gotcha: the config field `other-repo` means "this repo's
236	counterpart" (cwd-flavored direction; from `.claude` it's `..` =
237	the app), while CLI `--scope=other` means "the workspace's 'other'
238	role" (workspace-anchored; always = `.claude`). They coincide
239	from app's cwd (both physically = `.claude`) but diverge from
240	`.claude`'s cwd (config `other-repo` = `..`, CLI `--scope=other`
241	= `.`). Consider renaming the config field to `counterpart` or
242	`peer` to disambiguate from the CLI scope name.
243	
244	**Plain-old-repo handling: three workspace states.** vc-x1 should
245	be practical without a full workspace too. Three distinguishable
246	states, all described by `.vc-config.toml` presence/contents:
247	
248	- **POR** — no `.vc-config.toml`. Pure git/jj repo, vc-x1
249	  doesn't know it exists. Implicit `--scope=app`;
250	  `--scope=other/both` errors with "not in a vc-x1 workspace
251	  (no `.vc-config.toml`) — drop --scope or use --scope=app".
252	- **Single-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path`
253	  only (no `other-repo`). vc-x1-aware, no companion. Example:
254	  `vc-template-x1`. Implicit `--scope=app`; `--scope=other/both`
255	  errors with "no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = …`
256	  to `.vc-config.toml` to enable dual-repo operations".
257	- **Dual-repo workspace** — `.vc-config.toml` with `path` +
258	  `other-repo`. Full setup. Example: `vc-x1` itself.
259	  `--scope=app/other/both` all valid.
260	
261	Edge cases: a `.vc-config.toml` with `path != "/"` and no
262	`other-repo` (e.g. `path = "/.claude"`) — the repo identifies as
263	the "other" side but its companion is missing. Error: "config
264	identifies this as `/.claude` but no `other-repo` to point at the
265	app side; companion is missing." Dual-repo with `other-repo`
266	pointing somewhere that doesn't exist → loud error (workspace
267	state corrupted), not POR fallback.
268	
269	**`vc-x1 push --squash` composition.** With `--scope` in place,
270	the squash design (per [57]) composes naturally:
271	
272	- `vc-x1 push --squash` (no scope) → squash both repos and
273	  force-push both. End-to-end version of the manual two-step
274	  recipe in CLAUDE.md "Late changes after push".
275	- `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=app` → squash app only.
276	- `vc-x1 push --squash --scope=other` → squash other only.
277	
278	**Dogfood validation (2026-04-23).** The manual recipe was applied
279	successfully to both repos in sequence to fold late changes into
280	the 0.37.7 commit. Observations worth carrying into the `--squash`
281	implementation:
282	
283	- jj's `jj squash --ignore-immutable` preserves the changeID of
284	  the squashed-into commit. Both repos' ochid trailers stayed
285	  valid through the squash without any fixup. The future
286	  `--squash` implementation can rely on this — no ochid rewrite
287	  needed.
288	- The trimmed "Late changes after push" recipe (0.37.6, two
289	  lines for the `@-` case) worked identically on both sides;
290	  no `jj bookmark set` needed since the bookmark moves with the
291	  rewritten commit.
292	- Push state was already cleared at the end of the prior
293	  successful push (state reported "completed all stages (state
294	  cleared)"). The post-recovery `--restart` / `rm` step
295	  documented in CLAUDE.md "Manual finalize fallback" wasn't
296	  needed in this case — but the doc remains correct for cases
297	  where prior push didn't clean up.
298	
299	- `notes/chores-06.md` — this subsection.
300	- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry + new `[65]` reference + new
301	  Todo entry for the template restructure (vc-template-x1 +
302	  `.claude/` subdir, captured separately for later
303	  implementation).
304	
305	## Bookmark tracking verification (0.38.0)
306	
307	Cross-command tracking verification per the [63] design — every
308	repo-modifying command checks its target bookmark for non-tracking
309	remote refs and errors with the exact remediation command if any
310	are found. Multi-step rollout:
311	
312	- **0.38.0-0** — shared helper + tests (foundational refactor; no
313	  behavior change beyond finalize).
314	- **0.38.0-1** — wire into setup commands (`init`, `clone`,
315	  `test-fixture`).
316	- **0.38.0-2** — wire into preflight commands (`sync`, `push`).
317	- **0.38.0** — release commit (notes + any doc tweaks).
318	
319	### 0.38.0-0: shared helper + tests
320	
321	`finalize` already had `find_non_tracking_remote` + a preflight
322	check at finalize.rs:163,215-232 (added in an earlier 0.37.x
323	release). Step 0 promotes that logic into `common.rs` so the
324	remaining commands can share it without copy-paste.
325	
326	Signature deviates slightly from the [63] sketch
327	(`verify_tracking(repo, bookmark, remote)`): kept the existing
328	"detect any non-tracking remote for this bookmark" semantic
329	rather than checking a specific `(bookmark, remote)` pair —
330	matches what the call sites actually want, and avoids a second
331	helper layer.
332	
333	```rust
334	pub fn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String>;
335	pub fn verify_tracking(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;
336	```
337	
338	`verify_tracking` runs `jj bookmark list -a <bookmark> -R <repo>`,
339	parses the output via `find_non_tracking_remote`, and returns
340	`Err` with the standard message:
341	
342	```
343	bookmark '{b}' has non-tracking remote '{b}@{r}' —
344	run `jj bookmark track {b} --remote={r} -R {repo}` to fix
345	```
346	
347	- `src/common.rs` — added `find_non_tracking_remote` + `verify_tracking`
348	  + 4 parser tests (moved from `finalize.rs`).
349	- `src/finalize.rs` — removed the local `find_non_tracking_remote`
350	  fn + 4 tests; preflight now calls `crate::common::verify_tracking`.
351	  Behavior unchanged.
352	- `notes/chores-06.md` — new `## Bookmark tracking verification
353	  (0.38.0)` parent + this `### 0.38.0-0` sub-section + stubs for
354	  `### 0.38.0-1` / `### 0.38.0-2`.
355	- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry for this step + In Progress entries

```
14	/// Read the other repo path from a repo's `.vc-config.toml`.
15	///
16	/// Returns the `workspace.other-repo` value, which is the relative path
17	/// to the counterpart repo from the repo's directory.
18	pub fn other_repo_from_config(
19	    config: &HashMap<String, String>,
20	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
21	    toml_simple::toml_get(config, "workspace.other-repo")
22	        .cloned()
23	        .ok_or_else(|| "missing workspace.other-repo in .vc-config.toml".into())
24	}
25	
26	/// Derive the ochid prefix from a repo's `.vc-config.toml`.
27	///
28	/// The `workspace.path` value is the repo's path relative to the workspace
29	/// root, which is exactly the ochid prefix (with a trailing `/` when the
30	/// path isn't just `/`).
31	pub fn ochid_prefix_from_config(
32	    config: &HashMap<String, String>,
33	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
34	    let ws_path = toml_simple::toml_get(config, "workspace.path")
35	        .ok_or("missing workspace.path in .vc-config.toml")?;
36	    let trimmed = ws_path.trim_end_matches('/');
37	    if trimmed.is_empty() {
38	        Ok("/".to_string())
39	    } else {
40	        Ok(format!("{trimmed}/"))
41	    }
42	}

44	/// Problems found with an ochid trailer, with details for reporting.

48	    pub wrong_prefix: Option<(String, String)>,
49	    /// None if length is correct, Some((actual_len, expected_len)) if wrong.
50	    pub wrong_length: Option<(usize, usize)>,
51	    /// true if the bare ID does not resolve in the other repo.
52	    pub not_found: bool,
53	    /// The bare ID extracted from the ochid value.
54	    pub bare_id: String,
55	}
56	
57	impl OchidIssues {
58	    pub fn any(&self) -> bool {
59	        self.wrong_prefix.is_some() || self.wrong_length.is_some() || self.not_found
60	    }
61	
62	    pub fn summary(&self) -> String {
63	        let mut parts = Vec::new();
64	        if let Some((ref actual, ref expected)) = self.wrong_prefix {
65	            parts.push(format!("prefix: {actual} (want {expected})"));
66	        }
67	        if let Some((actual, expected)) = self.wrong_length {
68	            parts.push(format!("len: {actual} (want {expected})"));
69	        }
70	        if self.not_found {
71	            parts.push(format!("not found: {}", self.bare_id));
72	        }
73	        parts.join(", ")
74	    }
75	}
```

```
220	/// invocations broke it. Originally added in 0.37.2 as a temporary
221	/// diagnostic; promoted to permanent in 0.37.4 after the user
222	/// reported "happens more than once" — the signal is worth keeping
223	/// in place until confidence in its correctness is well-established
224	/// across varied use, at which point a "silent when clean"
225	/// refinement would remove the steady-state noise without losing
226	/// detection value (see `notes/todo.md`).
227	///
228	/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory
229	/// whose `.vc-config.toml` has `path = "/"`), then probes `<root>`
230	/// and `<root>/.claude`. Same labeling whether the user runs from
231	/// the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.
232	fn bm_track(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
233	    let header = format!("bm-track {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}");
234	    let root = match find_workspace_root() {
235	        Some(r) => r,
236	        None => {
237	            log::info!("{header}: no-workspace");
238	            return;
239	        }
240	    };
241	    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] =
242	        [(root.clone(), "app"), (root.join(".claude"), ".claude")];
243	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
244	    for (repo, label) in repos {
245	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
246	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
247	            continue;
248	        }
249	        match bm_track_one(&repo, "main", "origin") {
250	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
251	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
252	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
253	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
254	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
255	            )),
256	        }
257	    }
258	    log::info!("{header}: {}", parts.join(", "));
259	}
260	
261	/// Walk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = "/"`.
262	/// That's the workspace root — the app-repo side of the dual-repo
263	/// pair. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to filesystem
264	/// root (e.g., running from outside any vc-x1 workspace).
265	fn find_workspace_root() -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {
266	    let mut cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
267	    loop {
268	        let config = cur.join(".vc-config.toml");
269	        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&config) {
270	            // Match either `path = "/"` or `path="/"` (with/without spaces).
271	            let compact: String = content
272	                .lines()
273	                .find(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with("path"))
274	                .unwrap_or("") // OK: no `path =` line ⇒ empty match, continues walking up
275	                .chars()
276	                .filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace())
277	                .collect();
278	            if compact.starts_with("path=\"/\"") {
279	                return Some(cur);
280	            }
281	        }
282	        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();
283	    }
284	}
285	
286	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
287	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows
288	/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or
289	/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both
290	/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists
291	/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on
292	/// subprocess failure.
293	fn bm_track_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
294	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
295	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
296	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
297	        .output()
298	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
299	    if !output.status.success() {
300	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
301	    }
302	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
303	    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated
304	    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still
305	    // represents a tracking relationship.
306	    let colon = format!("@{remote}:");
307	    let paren = format!("@{remote} ");
308	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {
309	        let t = l.trim_start();
310	        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)
311	    }))
312	}
313	
314	fn main() -> ExitCode {
315	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
316	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
317	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
318	        Ok(cli) => cli,
319	        Err(e) => {
320	            e.exit();
321	        }
322	    };
323	
324	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
325	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
326	
327	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
328	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
329	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
330	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
331	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
332	        cli.command,
333	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
334	    );
335	
336	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
337	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
338	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
339	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
```

```
27	
28	/// Banner string emitted as the first line of normal command runs
29	/// and shown at the top of subcommand `--help` output. Built from
30	/// `Cargo.toml`'s name + version at compile time so it stays in
31	/// sync with the bumped version.
32	const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
33	
34	/// Top-level about line — name, version, and the project tagline
35	/// on a single line. Used as the top-level `about` so `vc-x1 -h`
36	/// reads as one banner-plus-tagline header instead of two stacked
37	/// lines.
38	const TOP_ABOUT: &str = concat!(
39	    env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
40	    " ",
41	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
42	    " - jj workspace tooling"
43	);
44	
45	/// Build the clap command tree with `BANNER` set as `before_help`
46	/// on every subcommand (transitively). Top-level skips `before_help`
47	/// because its own `about` already carries the name+version+tagline.
48	/// Walks via `mut_subcommand` so individual subcommand
49	/// `#[command(long_about = ...)]` blocks don't have to repeat the
50	/// banner text.
51	fn cli_with_banner() -> clap::Command {
52	    fn add_to_subs(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
53	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
54	            .get_subcommands()
55	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
56	            .collect();
57	        for name in names {
58	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
59	        }
60	        cmd
61	    }
62	    fn add_with_banner(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
63	        cmd = cmd.before_help(BANNER);
64	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
65	            .get_subcommands()
66	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
67	            .collect();
68	        for name in names {
69	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
70	        }
71	        cmd
72	    }
73	    add_to_subs(Cli::command())
74	}
75	
76	#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
77	#[command(
78	    version,
79	    propagate_version = true,
80	    about = TOP_ABOUT,
81	    max_term_width = 80
82	)]
83	pub struct Cli {
84	    /// Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace
85	    #[arg(short, long, global = true, action = clap::ArgAction::Count)]
86	    pub verbose: u8,
87	
88	    /// Log file path (captures all levels)
89	    #[arg(long, global = true)]
90	    pub log: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
91	
92	    #[command(subcommand)]
93	    pub(crate) command: Commands,
94	}
95	
96	#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
97	pub(crate) enum Commands {
98	    /// Print the changeID for a revision
99	    Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
100	
101	    /// Show full description of a commit
102	    Desc(desc::DescArgs),
103	
104	    /// List commits in a jj repo
105	    List(list::ListArgs),
106	
107	    /// Show commit details and diff summary
108	    Show(show::ShowArgs),
109	
110	    /// Validate commit descriptions against the other repo
111	    #[command(
112	        long_about = "Validate commit descriptions against the other repo.\n\n\
113	        Output columns: STATUS  CHANGEID  TITLE  [DETAILS]\n\n\
114	        Status labels:\n  \

146	        Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and\n\
147	        `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo\n\
148	        projects or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces. Flag is repeatable\n\
149	        or comma-separated (e.g. `-R . -R .claude` or `-R .,.claude`).\n\n\
150	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
151	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
152	        Per repo:\n  \
153	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
154	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
155	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
156	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
157	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
158	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
159	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
160	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
161	        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\n\
162	        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\n\
163	        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\n\
164	        orphaned on a stale branch.\n\n\
165	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
166	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
167	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
168	        Output shape:\n  \
169	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
170	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \
171	          - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions\n  \
172	          - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success")]
```
