187	        single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a\n\
188	        state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can\n\
189	        resume without re-doing completed stages.\n\n\
190	        Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)\n\
191	        → message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →\n\
192	        commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both\n\
193	        → push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /\n\
194	        commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via\n\
195	        `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.\n\
196	        After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\n\
197	        recovery is forward-only.\n\n\
198	        Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes\n\
199	        to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body\n\
200	        across resumes so only the first invocation needs them."
201	    )]

208	        Err(e) => {
209	            error!("{e}");
210	            ExitCode::FAILURE
211	        }
212	    }
213	}

> AGENT

I've got the lay of the land. Here's my read of 0.41.0-2 before I start writing code:

**Goal**: replace sync's hard-coded `[".", ".claude"]` with scope-driven repo resolution, mirroring how `init` already exposes `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`.

**Proposed shape**:

1. Add `--scope=SIDES` to `SyncArgs` (clap-parsed `Vec<Side>` like init), `conflicts_with = "repos"` so `--scope` and `-R` are mutually exclusive — they answer different questions ("workspace roles" vs "arbitrary repo set"). `-R` stays for back-compat and multi-repo setups.

2. New helpers — likely in `common.rs` so push/finalize can reuse next step:
   - `default_scope() -> Scope` — walks for workspace root, reads its `.vc-config.toml`'s `[workspace].other-repo`. Non-empty → `Scope(Code,Bot)`, missing/empty → `Scope(Code)`, no workspace root (POR) → `Scope(Code)`.
   - `scope_to_repos(&Scope) -> Vec<PathBuf>` — `Code` → workspace root; `Bot` → `workspace_root.join(other-repo)`. Errors loudly if `Bot` requested but `other-repo` is absent.

3. `sync()` precedence:
   - `-R` non-empty → existing `resolve_repos` path.
   - Else `--scope` set → `scope_to_repos(&Scope(args.scope))`.
   - Else → `scope_to_repos(&default_scope())`.

4. Side-effect benefit: cwd-portable. From `.claude/`, `vc-x1 sync` (no flags) walks up to find the workspace root and resolves repos by absolute path — current behavior fails there because `[".", ".claude"]` is cwd-relative.

5. Integration tests stay on `sync_repos(&fx.repos(), …)` — bypass the resolver, no breakage. Add unit tests covering the three default-resolution cases (POR, single-repo workspace, dual-repo workspace).

**Open question for you** — anything about scope vocabulary or precedence I should adjust before I start? Otherwise I'll proceed: bump to `0.41.0-2`, implement, run the pre-commit checklist, and stop for review.

> AGENT

My take, ranked:

1. **`<CODE|BOT|CODE,BOT>`** — best. Shows the literal allowed values and the comma form right in the help. Mirrors the existing `--use-template <CODE[,BOT]>` style. A reader sees `--scope <CODE|BOT|CODE,BOT>` and doesn't need to read a paragraph to know what's accepted.
2. **`<SCOPE>`** — flag-name match (clap's default convention; matches `--bookmark <BOOKMARK>`, `--remote <REMOTE>` already used here). Terser, less self-documenting.
3. `<SIDES>` — current, accurate-but-abstract. The status quo.

Skip:
- `SCOPE_VAL` / `SCOPEV` — meta filler ("the value of --scope") with no extra information.
- `APPBOT` — re-introduces the `app` vocabulary that 0.40.0 deliberately dropped in favor of `code/bot`.
- `AP` — too cryptic; a fresh reader has no chance.

Recommendation: go with `<CODE|BOT|CODE,BOT>` for sync and retroactively update `init` to match. One consistent pattern across the surface, and the help is self-explanatory.

106	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
107	fn run_retry(
108	    cmd: &str,
109	    args: &[&str],
110	    cwd: &Path,
111	    retries: u32,
112	    delay_secs: u64,
113	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
114	    let mut last_err = String::new();
115	    for attempt in 1..=retries {
116	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
117	            Ok(out) => {
118	                if attempt > 1 {
119	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
120	                }
121	                return Ok(out);
122	            }
123	            Err(e) => {
124	                last_err = e.to_string();
125	                if attempt < retries {
126	                    debug!("attempt {attempt}/{retries} failed: {last_err}");
127	                    debug!("retrying in {delay_secs}s...");
128	                    std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_secs(delay_secs));
129	                }
130	            }
131	        }
132	    }
133	    Err(format!("failed after {retries} attempts: {last_err}").into())
134	}

138	/// Parse the `--use-template` value into `(code, bot)` template paths.
139	///
140	/// Format: `CODE[,BOT]`. If `BOT` is omitted, the default is the sibling
141	/// directory whose name is `<CODE-basename>.claude` (via
142	/// `Path::with_file_name`, so a trailing slash on `CODE` does not produce
143	/// a different result).
144	pub(crate) fn parse_use_template(
145	    s: &str,
146	) -> Result<(PathBuf, PathBuf), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
147	    let mut parts = s.splitn(2, ',');
148	    let code_raw = parts.next().unwrap_or(""); // OK: splitn always yields at least one element
149	    let bot_raw = parts.next();
150	    let code_trim = code_raw.trim();
151	    if code_trim.is_empty() {
152	        return Err("--use-template: code template path is empty".into());
153	    }
154	    let code = PathBuf::from(code_trim);
155	    let bot = match bot_raw.map(str::trim) {
156	        Some(b) if !b.is_empty() => PathBuf::from(b),
157	        _ => {
158	            let file_name = code.file_name().ok_or_else(|| {
159	                format!(
160	                    "--use-template: cannot derive default bot path from '{}' (no file name component)",
161	                    code.display()
162	                )
163	            })?;
164	            let new_name = format!("{}.claude", file_name.to_string_lossy());
165	            code.with_file_name(new_name)
166	        }
167	    };
168	    Ok((code, bot))
169	}

1	use std::collections::HashMap;
2	use std::path::Path;
3	
4	/// Load a TOML file into a flat key-value map.
5	///
6	/// Handles `[section]` headers, bare `key = "value"` pairs, comments, and
7	/// blank lines. Keys under a section are stored as `section.key`. Quoted
8	/// string values have their quotes stripped; unquoted values are stored
9	/// as-is.
10	///
11	/// This is intentionally minimal — just enough for `.vc-config.toml`.
12	pub fn toml_load(path: &Path) -> Result<HashMap<String, String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
13	    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
14	        .map_err(|e| format!("cannot read '{}': {e}", path.display()))?;
15	
16	    let mut map = HashMap::new();
17	    let mut section = String::new();
18	
19	    for line in content.lines() {
20	        let trimmed = line.trim();
21	
22	        // Skip blank lines and comments
23	        if trimmed.is_empty() || trimmed.starts_with('#') {
24	            continue;
25	        }
26	
27	        // Section header: [name]
28	        if let Some(inner) = trimmed.strip_prefix('[').and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(']')) {
29	            section = inner.trim().to_string();
30	            continue;
31	        }
32	
33	        // Key = value
34	        if let Some((key, value)) = trimmed.split_once('=') {
35	            let key = key.trim();
36	            let value = value.trim();
37	
38	            // Strip surrounding quotes from string values
39	            let value = if (value.starts_with('"') && value.ends_with('"'))
40	                || (value.starts_with('\'') && value.ends_with('\''))
41	            {
42	                &value[1..value.len() - 1]
43	            } else {
44	                value
45	            };
46	
47	            let full_key = if section.is_empty() {
48	                key.to_string()
49	            } else {
50	                format!("{section}.{key}")
51	            };
52	
53	            map.insert(full_key, value.to_string());
54	        }
55	    }
56	
57	    Ok(map)
58	}
59	
60	/// Look up a dotted key (e.g. `"workspace.path"`) in a loaded config map.
61	pub fn toml_get<'a>(map: &'a HashMap<String, String>, key: &str) -> Option<&'a String> {
62	    map.get(key)
63	}
64

1	//! Shared test helpers for dual-repo integration tests.
2	//!
3	//! Provides a `Fixture` that wraps `crate::init::init_with_symlink`
4	//! plus a per-process unique-tempdir helper so parallel tests don't
5	//! collide. Lifted out of the `sync` test module (originally inline at
6	//! `sync.rs:521–560`) so `push`'s tests (0.37.0) and any future
7	//! subcommand's tests can sit on the same harness without
8	//! copy-paste drift.
9	//!
10	//! Test-only — the whole module is gated at its declaration site via
11	//! `#[cfg(test)] mod test_helpers;`.
12	
13	use std::fs;
14	use std::path::PathBuf;
15	use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
16	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
17	
18	use crate::common::write_file;
19	use crate::init::{InitArgs, init_with_symlink};
20	
21	/// Per-process counter so same-nanosecond tempdir collisions yield
22	/// distinct paths when tests run in parallel.
23	static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
24	
25	/// Build a unique tempdir path for a test fixture.
26	///
27	/// Combines a nanosecond timestamp with a per-process atomic counter
28	/// so parallel tests and same-nanosecond collisions both yield
29	/// distinct paths. Prefix is `vc-x1-test-<tag>-<ts>-<n>` so callers
30	/// from different subcommand tests stay discoverable in `$TMPDIR`.
31	pub fn unique_base(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
32	    let ts = SystemTime::now()
33	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
34	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
35	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: clock error → 0 is harmless for unique tempdir naming
36	    let n = COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
37	    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("vc-x1-test-{tag}-{ts}-{n}"))
38	}
39	
40	/// Owned dual-repo fixture with RAII cleanup.
41	///
42	/// Builds a fresh throwaway workspace under a unique tempdir by
43	/// driving `init::init_with_symlink` in `--repo-local` mode with
44	/// `create_symlink=false`, exposing the two repo paths (`work` and
45	/// `work/.claude`). The tempdir tree is removed when the value is
46	/// dropped, so a panicking test still cleans up after itself.
47	pub struct Fixture {
48	    /// Root tempdir that owns both repos and their bare-git remotes.
49	    pub base: PathBuf,
50	    /// Code repo path (`<base>/work`).
51	    pub work: PathBuf,
52	    /// Bot session repo path (`<base>/work/.claude`).
53	    pub claude: PathBuf,
54	}
55	
56	impl Fixture {
57	    /// Build a fresh fixture in a unique tempdir.
58	    pub fn new(tag: &str) -> Self {
59	        Self::new_opts(tag, false, None)
60	    }
61	
62	    /// Build a fresh fixture with optional pending changes and a
63	    /// template seed.
64	    ///
65	    /// - `with_pending` — after init, write a small file into each
66	    ///   repo so `@` carries uncommitted changes (useful for
67	    ///   finalize / push tests).
68	    /// - `use_template` — `CODE[,BOT]` forwarded to init.
69	    pub fn new_opts(tag: &str, with_pending: bool, use_template: Option<String>) -> Self {
70	        let base = unique_base(tag);
71	        // init refuses to reuse an existing project_dir, so `base`
72	        // must not exist yet. `unique_base` already guarantees that.
73	        let args = InitArgs {
74	            name: Some("work".to_string()),
75	            owner: None,
76	            dir: None,
77	            private: false,
78	            dry_run: false,
79	            push_retries: 5,
80	            push_retry_delay: 3,
81	            use_template,
82	            repo_local: Some(base.to_string_lossy().into_owned()),
83	            repo_remote: None,
84	            scope: None, // default → code,bot
85	        };
86	        init_with_symlink(&args, false).expect("build test fixture via init");
87	
88	        let work = base.join("work");
89	        let claude = work.join(".claude");
90	
91	        if with_pending {
92	            write_file(&work.join("TODO.md"), "# TODO\n- first feature\n")
93	                .expect("write pending TODO.md");
94	            write_file(
95	                &claude.join("session-notes.md"),
96	                "# Session notes\n- simulated pending work\n",
97	            )
98	            .expect("write pending session-notes.md");
99	        }
100	
101	        Fixture { base, work, claude }
102	    }
103	
104	    /// Convenience: return both repo paths as a `Vec<PathBuf>`
105	    /// suitable for `sync_repos` (or any other `&[PathBuf]` API).
106	    pub fn repos(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
107	        vec![self.work.clone(), self.claude.clone()]
108	    }
109	}
110	
111	impl Drop for Fixture {
112	    /// Remove the fixture tree on drop. Best-effort; a failure here
113	    /// doesn't fail the test.
114	    fn drop(&mut self) {
115	        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.base);
116	    }
117	}
118	
```

410	
411	```
412	vc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo default, --check (verify)
413	vc-x1 sync --check                    # explicit form of the default
414	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # dual-repo, apply
415	vc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project
416	vc-x1 sync -R .,.claude -R ../other   # mixed: repeat + comma-separate
417	```
418	
419	Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
420	explicitly rather than rely on the default — defaults can shift,
421	explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive use can take the
422	default.
423	
424	| Flag | Description |
425	|------|-------------|
426	| `-R, --repo <PATH>` | Repo to sync; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`] |
427	| `--check` | Verify only — fetch + classify, error if any repo needs action (default) |
428	| `--no-check` | Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase/fast-forward as needed |
429	| `-q, --quiet` | Suppress all output; exit code signals result (for scripts) |
430	| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Bookmark to sync in each repo [default: main] |
431	| `--remote <NAME>` | Remote to sync against [default: origin] |
432	
433	**Output shape.** Sync collapses output based on what it finds:
434	
435	- **All up-to-date** — one-line summary:
436	  `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`. Nothing else.
437	  Makes "sprinkle sync everywhere" genuinely cheap. Scope is
438	  bookmark-vs-remote tracking — `@` may have uncommitted
439	  working-copy changes; sync intentionally doesn't speak to
440	  that (use `jj st` for working-copy state).
441	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) — per-repo fetch +
442	  state lines, then **fatal in `--check` mode** with
443	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
444	  and re-run`. Under `--no-check`, the actions run instead.
445	- **`--quiet`** — no output at any level; exit code is the only
446	  signal. Intended for scripts that just need success/failure.
447	
448	**Note on the `behind` case.** jj's `git fetch` already fast-forwards a
449	tracked local bookmark when it's a strict ancestor of the incoming
450	remote, so in the common case `sync` reports `up-to-date` rather than
451	`behind`. The `behind` branch covers untracked bookmarks and edge
452	configs where auto-advance is disabled.
453	
454	### finalize
455	
456	Atomically squash, set bookmark, and push a jj repo. The primary use
457	case is the bot finalizing its own session repo (`.claude`) at the end
458	of a session. The bot can't just `jj commit` because the act of
459	committing generates more session data — files written after the commit
```

109	/// Resolve positional and flag args into a `DotSpec` ready for `collect_ids`.
110	///
111	/// Handles: dot notation parsing, flag vs positional precedence,
112	/// count-means-total semantics (subtracts 1 for anchor).
113	pub fn resolve_spec(
114	    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
115	    pos_count: Option<usize>,
116	    flag_rev: &str,
117	    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
118	    default_rev: &str,
119	) -> DotSpec {
120	    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
121	    let rev_str = if flag_rev_set {
122	        flag_rev
123	    } else {
124	        pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev) // OK: positional absent → fall back to CLI default
125	    };
126	    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
127	
128	    let count = if flag_limit.is_some() {
129	        flag_limit
130	    } else {
131	        pos_count
132	    };
133	
134	    if let Some(c) = count {
135	        let n = c.saturating_sub(1);
136	        let both_open = spec.desc_count.is_none() && spec.anc_count.is_none();
137	        if both_open {
138	            // Split budget across both sides: ancestors get the extra
139	            let desc_n = n / 2;
140	            let anc_n = n - desc_n;
141	            spec.desc_count = Some(desc_n);
142	            spec.anc_count = Some(anc_n);
143	        } else {
144	            if spec.desc_count.is_none() {
145	                spec.desc_count = Some(n);
146	            }
147	            if spec.anc_count.is_none() {
148	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
149	            }
150	            // bare `x 5` → treat as `x.. 5` (ancestors)
151	            if spec.desc_count == Some(0) && spec.anc_count == Some(0) {
152	                spec.anc_count = Some(n);
153	            }
154	        }
155	    }
156	
157	    spec
158	}
159	
160	/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.
161	///
162	/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:
163	/// - **stderr at `debug!`** on success — jj's chatter (`Moved 1 bookmarks
164	///   to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) is hidden by default
165	///   and surfaced with `-v`. The debug formatter indents each line two
166	///   spaces so subprocess output sits visually under the caller's own
167	///   `info!` line in `-v` mode.
168	/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data
169	///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), and `info!` would flood the user

578	pub fn find_non_tracking_remote(list_output: &str, bookmark: &str) -> Option<String> {
579	    let prefix = format!("{bookmark}@");
580	    for line in list_output.lines() {
581	        if line.starts_with(&prefix)
582	            && let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix(&prefix)
583	            && let Some((remote, _)) = rest.split_once(':')
584	        {
585	            return Some(remote.to_string());
586	        }
587	    }
588	    None
589	}
590	
591	/// Verify all remote refs for `bookmark` in `repo` are tracked.
592	///
593	/// Returns `Err` with the exact `jj bookmark track …` remediation command if
594	/// any non-tracking remote ref is found. Used as a preflight by repo-modifying
595	/// commands (sync, push, finalize) and as a post-condition assertion by setup
596	/// commands (init, clone, test-fixture).
597	pub fn verify_tracking(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
598	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
599	    let cwd = Path::new(".");
600	    let all = run(
601	        "jj",
602	        &["bookmark", "list", "-a", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
603	        cwd,
604	    )?;
605	    if let Some(remote) = find_non_tracking_remote(&all, bookmark) {
606	        return Err(format!(
607	            "bookmark '{bookmark}' has non-tracking remote '{bookmark}@{remote}' — \
608	             run `jj bookmark track {bookmark} --remote={remote} -R {repo_str}` to fix"
609	        )
610	        .into());
611	    }
612	    Ok(())
613	}
614

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs",
  "old_string": "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn find_non_tracking_remote_tracked() {",
  "new_string": "/// Walk up from `start` to find the workspace root.\n///\n/// The workspace root is the directory whose `.vc-config.toml` has\n/// `path = \"/\"`. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to the\n/// filesystem root — the caller is in a \"plain old repo\" (POR) with\n/// no vc-x1 workspace metadata.\npub fn find_workspace_root_from(start: &Path) -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    let mut cur = start.to_path_buf();\n    loop {\n        let cfg = cur.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE);\n        if let Ok(map) = toml_simple::toml_load(&cfg)\n            && toml_simple::toml_get(&map, \"workspace.path\").map(String::as_str) == Some(\"/\")\n        {\n            return Some(cur);\n        }\n        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();\n    }\n}\n\n/// Cwd-anchored wrapper over [`find_workspace_root_from`].\npub fn find_workspace_root() -> Option<PathBuf> {\n    let cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;\n    find_workspace_root_from(&cur)\n}\n\n/// Resolve the workspace's default `--scope`.\n///\n/// Reads `<workspace_root>/.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`:\n///\n/// - non-empty `other-repo` → `Scope([Code, Bot])`\n/// - missing / empty `other-repo` → `Scope([Code])`\n/// - `workspace_root = None` (POR) → `Scope([Code])`\npub fn default_scope(workspace_root: Option<&Path>) -> Scope {\n    let Some(root) = workspace_root else {\n        return Scope(vec![Side::Code]);\n    };\n    let cfg = match toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE)) {\n        Ok(c) => c,\n        Err(_) => return Scope(vec![Side::Code]),\n    };\n    match toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, \"workspace.other-repo\") {\n        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),\n        _ => Scope(vec![Side::Code]),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolve a `Scope` to concrete repo paths.\n///\n/// - `Side::Code` → `workspace_root` (or cwd's `.` when `workspace_root` is None).\n/// - `Side::Bot` → `workspace_root.join(other-repo)`.\n///\n/// Errors when `Side::Bot` is requested but the workspace doesn't define\n/// one (POR or single-repo workspace).\npub fn scope_to_repos(\n    scope: &Scope,\n    workspace_root: Option<&Path>,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut repos = Vec::new();\n    for side in &scope.0 {\n        match side {\n            Side::Code => repos.push(\n                workspace_root\n                    .map(Path::to_path_buf)\n                    .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(\".\")),\n            ),\n            Side::Bot => {\n                let root = workspace_root.ok_or(\n                    \"--scope=bot: not in a vc-x1 workspace (no .vc-config.toml with path = \\\"/\\\") — drop --scope or use --scope=code\",\n                )?;\n                let cfg = toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;\n                let other = toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, \"workspace.other-repo\")\n                    .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())\n                    .ok_or(\n                        \"--scope=bot: no other-repo configured. Add `other-repo = \\\"…\\\"` to .vc-config.toml to enable dual-repo operations\",\n                    )?;\n                repos.push(root.join(other));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(repos)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn find_non_tracking_remote_tracked() {"
}

ws_tempdir(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {\n        let ts = std::time::SystemTime::now()\n            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)\n            .map(|d| d.as_nanos())\n            .unwrap_or(0);\n        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(\"vc-x1-ws-{tag}-{ts}\"));\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect(\"mkdir tempdir\");\n        dir\n    }\n\n    /// Workspace root walk finds the dir whose `.vc-config.toml`\n    /// has `path = \"/\"`, even when starting from a deep subdir.\n    #[test]\n    fn find_workspace_root_walks_up() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"walk-up\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        let nested = root.join(\"a\").join(\"b\").join(\"c\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&nested).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(find_workspace_root_from(&nested).as_deref(), Some(&*root));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Walking from a directory with no enclosing workspace yields None.\n    #[test]\n    fn find_workspace_root_none_outside() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"none-outside\");\n        let nested = base.join(\"nested\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&nested).unwrap();\n        assert!(find_workspace_root_from(&nested).is_none());\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// `path` set to something other than `/` keeps walking — only the\n    /// `path = \"/\"` config marks the workspace root.\n    #[test]\n    fn find_workspace_root_skips_non_root_path() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"skip-non-root\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        let claude = root.join(\".claude\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&claude).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            claude.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/.claude\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\"..\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        // From inside .claude, the root walker still resolves to the\n        // app root (path = \"/\"), not to .claude itself.\n        assert_eq!(find_workspace_root_from(&claude).as_deref(), Some(&*root));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Default scope: workspace with non-empty `other-repo` → dual.\n    #[test]\n    fn default_scope_dual_workspace() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"default-dual\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(default_scope(Some(&root)), Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Default scope: workspace with no `other-repo` → code-only.\n    #[test]\n    fn default_scope_single_repo_workspace() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"default-single\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(default_scope(Some(&root)), Scope(vec![Side::Code]));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Default scope: empty `other-repo` value treated like missing.\n    #[test]\n    fn default_scope_empty_other_repo() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"default-empty\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\"\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(default_scope(Some(&root)), Scope(vec![Side::Code]));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// Default scope: POR (None workspace_root) → code-only.\n    #[test]\n    fn default_scope_por() {\n        assert_eq!(default_scope(None), Scope(vec![Side::Code]));\n    }\n\n    /// `scope_to_repos`: dual workspace resolves to root + root/other-repo.\n    #[test]\n    fn scope_to_repos_dual() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"repos-dual\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        let repos = scope_to_repos(&Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]), Some(&root)).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(repos, vec![root.clone(), root.join(\".claude\")]);\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// `scope_to_repos`: code-only inside a workspace yields just root.\n    #[test]\n    fn scope_to_repos_code_only() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"repos-code\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\nother-repo = \\\".claude\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        let repos = scope_to_repos(&Scope(vec![Side::Code]), Some(&root)).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(repos, vec![root.clone()]);\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n\n    /// `scope_to_repos`: code-only with POR → cwd `.`.\n    #[test]\n    fn scope_to_repos_code_por() {\n        let repos = scope_to_repos(&Scope(vec![Side::Code]), None).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(repos, vec![PathBuf::from(\".\")]);\n    }\n\n    /// `scope_to_repos`: bot in POR errors with the documented message.\n    #[test]\n    fn scope_to_repos_bot_por_errors() {\n        let err = scope_to_repos(&Scope(vec![Side::Bot]), None)\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"not in a vc-x1 workspace\"), \"got: {err}\");\n    }\n\n    /// `scope_to_repos`: bot in single-repo workspace errors.\n    #[test]\n    fn scope_to_repos_bot_single_repo_errors() {\n        let base = ws_tempdir(\"repos-bot-single\");\n        let root = base.join(\"ws\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();\n        std::fs::write(\n            root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE),\n            \"[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n        let err = scope_to_repos(&Scope(vec![Side::Bot]), Some(&root))\n            .unwrap_err()\n            .to_string();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"no other-repo configured\"), \"got: {err}\");\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&base).ok();\n    }\n}"

{

  "old_string": "/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n///\n/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any\n/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually\n/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every\n/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.\n///\n/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`\n/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,\n/// explicit flags lock in the contract.\n///\n/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and\n/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects\n/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SyncArgs {\n    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.\n    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"no_check\")]\n    pub check: bool,\n\n    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"check\")]\n    pub no_check: bool,\n\n    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub quiet: bool,\n\n    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"main\")]\n    pub bookmark: String,\n\n    /// Remote to sync against\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"origin\")]\n    pub remote: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`]\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long = \"repo\", value_name = \"PATH\")]\n    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n///\n/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any\n/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually\n/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every\n/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.\n///\n/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`\n/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,\n/// explicit flags lock in the contract.\n///\n/// Repo set is resolved (in order):\n///\n/// - `-R` / `--repo` → exactly that list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo).\n/// - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` → workspace roles, resolved via the\n///   workspace root's `.vc-config.toml`.\n/// - Neither → default scope: `code,bot` if the workspace's\n///   `[workspace] other-repo` is non-empty, else `code`. POR (no\n///   `.vc-config.toml`) → `code` resolved to cwd.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SyncArgs {\n    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.\n    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"no_check\")]\n    pub check: bool,\n\n    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"check\")]\n    pub no_check: bool,\n\n    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub quiet: bool,\n\n    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"main\")]\n    pub bookmark: String,\n\n    /// Remote to sync against\n    #[arg(long, default_value = \"origin\")]\n    pub remote: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated.\n    ///\n    /// Mutually exclusive with `--scope`. When neither is given the\n    /// repo set is derived from `--scope` defaults.\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long = \"repo\", value_name = \"PATH\", conflicts_with = \"scope\")]\n    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// Which side(s) of the workspace to sync.\n    ///\n    /// `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot`:\n    ///\n    /// - `code` — single-repo operation on the app repo.\n    /// - `bot` — single-repo operation on the bot repo (errors if the\n    ///   workspace doesn't define one).\n    /// - `code,bot` — dual-repo (default in a dual-repo workspace).\n    #[arg(\n        long,\n        value_name = \"SCOPE\",\n        value_delimiter = ',',\n        verbatim_doc_comment\n    )]\n    pub scope: Option<Vec<Side>>,\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Default repo set when `-R` isn't given — the canonical dual-repo pair.\nconst DEFAULT_REPOS: [&str; 2] = [\".\", \".claude\"];\n\n/// Resolve the caller's `-R` flags into a concrete repo list.\n///\n/// Empty input (no `-R` given) falls back to `DEFAULT_REPOS`. Each\n/// provided value is also split on `,` and trimmed, so\n/// `-R .,.claude` works identically to `-R . -R .claude`.\nfn resolve_repos(raw: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {\n    if raw.is_empty() {\n        return DEFAULT_REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();\n    }\n    raw.iter()\n        .flat_map(|p| {\n            p.to_string_lossy()\n                .split(',')\n                .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        })\n        .collect()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Split the caller's `-R` flags into a concrete repo list.\n///\n/// Each value is split on `,` and trimmed, so `-R .,.claude` works\n/// identically to `-R . -R .claude`. Caller is responsible for not\n/// passing an empty slice — `sync()` consults `--scope` instead.\nfn split_repos(raw: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {\n    raw.iter()\n        .flat_map(|p| {\n            p.to_string_lossy()\n                .split(',')\n                .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))\n                .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        })\n        .collect()\n}\n\n/// Resolve `args` into the concrete repo list `sync_repos` operates on.\n///\n/// Precedence: explicit `-R` → `--scope` → workspace-default scope.\n/// See [`SyncArgs`] for the full contract.\nfn resolve_args_to_repos(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if !args.repos.is_empty() {\n        return Ok(split_repos(&args.repos));\n    }\n    let workspace_root = find_workspace_root();\n    let scope = match &args.scope {\n        Some(sides) if sides.is_empty() => return Err(\"--scope: value is empty\".into()),\n        Some(sides) => Scope(sides.clone()),\n        None => default_scope(workspace_root.as_deref()),\n    };\n    scope_to_repos(&scope, workspace_root.as_deref())\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Walk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = \"/\"`.\n/// That's the workspace root — the app-repo side of the dual-repo\n/// pair. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to filesystem\n/// root (e.g., running from outside any vc-x1 workspace).\nfn find_workspace_root() -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {\n    let mut cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;\n    loop {\n        let config = cur.join(\".vc-config.toml\");\n        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&config) {\n            // Match either `path = \"/\"` or `path=\"/\"` (with/without spaces).\n            let compact: String = content\n                .lines()\n                .find(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(\"path\"))\n                .unwrap_or(\"\") // OK: no `path =` line ⇒ empty match, continues walking up\n                .chars()\n                .filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace())\n                .collect();\n            if compact.starts_with(\"path=\\\"/\\\"\") {\n                return Some(cur);\n            }\n        }\n        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();\n    }\n}\n\n/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.",

}

vc-x1 0.41.0-2
---
vc-x1 0.41.0-2

Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.

Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
`.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo
projects or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces. Flag is repeatable
or comma-separated (e.g. `-R . -R .claude` or `-R .,.claude`).

Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs
action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).
Per repo:
  - up-to-date        nothing to do
  - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote
  - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)
  - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts
  - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip

Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check
explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the
contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.

After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly
advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing
working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up
orphaned on a stale branch.

On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via
`jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the
revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.

Output shape:
  - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)
  - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error
  - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions
  - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success

Usage: vc-x1 sync [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --check
          Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action. This
          is the default; pass explicitly in scripts

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)
```

revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.

Output shape:
  - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)
  - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error
  - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions
  - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success

Usage: vc-x1 sync [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --check
          Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action. This
          is the default; pass explicitly in scripts

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --no-check
          Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed

  -q, --quiet
          Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)

      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to sync in each repo
          
          [default: main]

      --remote <REMOTE>
          Remote to sync against
          
          [default: origin]

  -R, --repo <PATH>
          Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated.
          
          Mutually exclusive with `--scope`. When neither is given the repo set
          is derived from `--scope` defaults.

      --scope <SCOPE>
          Which side(s) of the workspace to sync.
          
          `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot`:
          
          - `code` — single-repo operation on the app repo.
          - `bot` — single-repo operation on the bot repo (errors if the
            workspace doesn't define one).
          - `code,bot` — dual-repo (default in a dual-repo workspace).
          
          [possible values: code, bot]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "- `notes/cargo-locked-issue.md`: new standalone\n  background doc with the failing build output, the\n  asymmetry table, and links to the upstream cargo\n  issues + community design discussion.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "- `notes/cargo-locked-issue.md`: new standalone\n  background doc with the failing build output, the\n  asymmetry table, and links to the upstream cargo\n  issues + community design discussion.\n\n### 0.41.0-2: sync --scope\n\nWires `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` into `vc-x1 sync` and\ngeneralizes its repo-resolution rules.\n\n**Repo-set precedence (decided).** Sync now resolves its\noperand list in this order:\n\n1. `-R` / `--repo` — exactly that list (back-compat /\n   arbitrary multi-repo).\n2. `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` — workspace roles via the\n   workspace root's `.vc-config.toml`.\n3. Neither — workspace-default scope, per [71]: `code,bot`\n   when `[workspace] other-repo` is non-empty, else `code`.\n   POR (no `.vc-config.toml`) → `code` resolved to cwd.\n\n`-R` and `--scope` are mutually exclusive (`conflicts_with =\n\"scope\"` on the `repos` field). They answer different\nquestions — \"arbitrary repo set\" vs \"workspace roles\" — so\ncombining them is ambiguous, not additive.\n\n**Cwd-portable.** Previously `[\".\", \".claude\"]` was a literal\nrelative-path default; running `vc-x1 sync` from `.claude/`\ntried to resolve `.claude/.claude` and failed. Scope\nresolution walks up to the workspace root and emits absolute\npaths, so the same command works from anywhere inside the\nworkspace.\n\n**Helpers in `common.rs`.** Three helpers, designed to be\nreused by push (0.41.0-3) and finalize (0.41.0-4):\n\n- `find_workspace_root_from(start)` / `find_workspace_root()`\n  — walks up looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = \"/\"`.\n  The cwd-anchored wrapper replaces `main.rs`'s private copy\n  (now deleted; `bm_track` calls the shared helper).\n- `default_scope(workspace_root)` — applies the [71] rule\n  for the no-flag default case.\n- `scope_to_repos(scope, workspace_root)` — maps a `Scope`\n  to concrete paths. Errors with the [65]-prescribed messages\n  when `bot` is requested but no other-repo is configured\n  (or no workspace at all).\n\n**Vocabulary alignment.** Renamed `--scope`'s clap\n`value_name` from `SIDES` (init's original choice) to `SCOPE`\non both `init` and `sync`. Doc comments now lead with\n`SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot` so the help reads as the same\nself-describing pattern across commands. Summary of 0.41.0-2:

- **`Cargo.toml`** — bump to `0.41.0-2`.
- **`src/sync.rs`** — new `--scope SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot` flag, `conflicts_with` `-R`. `split_repos` + `resolve_args_to_repos` replace the old `DEFAULT_REPOS = [".", ".claude"]` literal. Three new clap-parse tests + four renamed split tests; integration `apply_args` picks up `scope: None`.
- **`src/init.rs`** — `--scope` `value_name` `SIDES` → `SCOPE`, doc comment leads with `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot`. Wording fixed from "side(s)" → "repo(s)" per your call.
- **`src/common.rs`** — three new helpers: `find_workspace_root_from` / `find_workspace_root` (lifted from `main.rs`), `default_scope`, `scope_to_repos`. 11 unit tests covering POR, single-repo workspace, dual workspace, and the bot-without-other-repo error paths.
- **`src/main.rs`** — drops the private `find_workspace_root`; `bm_track` now calls `common::find_workspace_root`. Sync's `long_about` rewritten to describe the new resolution rules.
- **`README.md`** — sync section rewritten with new examples, a precedence list, and the `--scope` flag row.
- **`notes/chores-06.md`** — new `### 0.41.0-2: sync --scope` subsection.
- **`notes/todo.md`** — In Progress entry updated; Done entries added for `0.41.0-1` and `0.41.0-2`.

Dogfooded all four scope shapes (default, `code,bot`, `code`, `bot`) from both the app root and `.claude/` cwd — all match expectations, with the `1 repo` / `2 repos` count noun pluralizing correctly. `-R` back-compat preserved; clap rejects `--scope` + `-R` together; POR + `--scope=bot` errors with the documented message.

Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

> AGENT

It's clap's auto-layout rule, not anything I set deliberately.

clap-derive picks two-column vs over-under per command based on the widest flag spec relative to terminal width. With `max_term_width = 80` (set on the top-level `Cli`), the threshold for the left column is roughly 1/3 of that. When any flag exceeds it, clap switches the whole command to next-line layout for visual consistency.

- **sync** — widest spec is `--bookmark <BOOKMARK>` at ~21 chars. Fits the two-column budget.
- **init** — widest is `--push-retry-delay <PUSH_RETRY_DELAY>` at ~37 chars. Pushes past the threshold and forces over-under for every flag in init.

The offenders are init's `push_retries` / `push_retry_delay` fields — they have no explicit `value_name`, so clap defaults to the upper-cased field name (`<PUSH_RETRIES>`, `<PUSH_RETRY_DELAY>`).

If you want init back to two-column, easiest fix is shorter `value_name`s on those two:

```rust
#[arg(long, default_value_t = 5, value_name = "N")]
pub push_retries: u32,

#[arg(long, default_value_t = 3, value_name = "SECS")]
pub push_retry_delay: u64,
```

That'd drop the longest spec to `--push-retry-delay <SECS>` (~25 chars) and clap should switch back to two-column.

Want me to apply that as part of this commit (or as a separate cleanup), or leave init's help in over-under?

> DEVELOPER

Can't we chose

> AGENT

Yes, clap exposes `Command::next_line_help(true)` to force the over-under layout. The reverse — forcing two-column when the widest spec exceeds the budget — isn't supported; clap will always wrap in that case.

So the choices are:

1. **Uniform over-under everywhere** — set `next_line_help(true)` on the root `Cli` (or apply it via the existing `cli_with_banner` walker that already touches every subcommand). One look across the whole CLI; each flag's description gets full-width room. Costs vertical space when it isn't needed (e.g. `chid -h`, `desc -h`).

2. **Uniform two-column where it fits** — keep the auto-pick, but shrink offending flags (init's retry pair) so every command lands in two-column. Trickier to maintain — every new long flag needs awareness.

3. **Status quo (mixed)** — clap auto-picks per command. Sync stays two-column, init stays over-under.

My take: option 1 if you want the visual consistency you're flagging. The vertical cost is real but minor on a help screen, and it eliminates the surprise of layout shifting per subcommand.

Which do you want?

Is there a test where of sync using a --repo-local?

> AGENT

Sort of — let me be precise.

**What we have**: every sync integration test uses `Fixture::new(...)` which builds the workspace via `init_with_symlink` in `--repo-local` mode (test_helpers.rs:82). So the fixture *is* a real `--repo-local`-generated dual-repo workspace.

**What we don't have**: those tests call `sync_repos(&fx.repos(), ...)` directly with explicit absolute paths — they bypass `sync()` and the new `resolve_args_to_repos` / `default_scope` / `scope_to_repos` chain entirely. None of them exercise the resolver against an init-generated `.vc-config.toml`.

The new helpers' unit tests (`default_scope_dual_workspace`, `scope_to_repos_dual`, etc.) build hand-written `.vc-config.toml` files, not init-generated ones. They prove the helpers work; they don't prove init's output is shaped the way the helpers expect.

That's a real coverage gap. Closing it cleanly: add an integration test that builds a `Fixture` and then calls `default_scope(Some(&fx.work))` and `scope_to_repos(&Scope([Code, Bot]), Some(&fx.work))`, asserting they resolve to `[fx.work, fx.claude]`. That exercises `init --repo-local`'s output against the new resolver end-to-end.

(Going further — driving `sync()` itself instead of `sync_repos()` — would need a cwd change, which isn't safe under parallel `cargo test` without serialization plumbing. Not worth it for this commit.)

Want me to add the integration test before we commit?

> DEVELOPER

y

action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).

  - up-to-date        nothing to do
  - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote
  - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)
  - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts
  - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip

> DEVELOPER

Which other subcommands need --scope; push, clone, finalize, chid, desc, list, show, validate-desc, thoughts

> AGENT

Quick read per subcommand:

**Already planned in 0.41.0**:
- **push** (0.41.0-3) — needed. Today's state machine assumes dual; single-repo scope means skip `commit-claude`/`finalize-claude`, bookmark one side only.
- **finalize** (0.41.0-4) — *might* be no-op. Already takes `--repo` for a single path. `--scope` would just be a vocabulary alias unless we extend finalize to accept a dual scope and finalize both in sequence (probably not worth it; that's what push does).

**High value, not yet planned**:
- **clone** — yes. Parallel to `init --scope=code|bot|code,bot`. Today clones both URLs; `--scope=code` would clone single-repo from a `vc-template-x1`-shape remote. Symmetric with init; users will expect it.
- **validate-desc / fix-desc** — yes. Both are inherently counterpart-aware (validate-desc reads the *other* repo's commits to check ochid trailers). Scope semantics shift slightly: `--scope=code` means "validate the app repo's commits *against* the bot repo" — which side is the *subject*, not which side is in scope. Useful, but the vocabulary is subtly different from sync/push and may need a renamed flag (e.g. `--side=code|bot`) instead of `--scope`. Worth a design sub-step.

**Low value, vocabulary-only**:
- **chid, desc, list, show** — read-only, multi-repo. Already accept repeatable `-R`/`--repo` (via `CommonArgs`); `--scope=code,bot` would be exactly equivalent to `-R . -R .claude`. Nice for uniformity, ~zero new behavior. Worth deferring until the cycle that touches `CommonArgs`, and doing all four at once.

**No**:
- **symlink** — bot-only by definition (creates `~/.claude/projects/<path>/.claude` symlink). Scope doesn't apply.
- **init, sync** — already done.

Suggested order:
1. **push --scope** (0.41.0-3, already in plan).
2. **finalize --scope** review (0.41.0-4, already in plan; may downgrade to no-op).
3. **clone --scope** — adjacent cycle, mechanically simple.
4. **validate-desc / fix-desc** — separate cycle with its own vocabulary review.
5. **CommonArgs (chid/desc/list/show)** — bottom of the priority list; bundle as one cycle when the value-of-the-vocabulary-uniformity case is felt strongly enough.

Want me to capture #3, #4, #5 as todos so they're not forgotten once the 0.41.0 cycle wraps?

> DEVELOPER

ATM I go for consistency and --repo would be for backwards compatibility in finalize. As for validate-desc/fix-desc I think scope is fine, code says validate code against bot. bot mean do it it opposite order and code,bot is the default. Same with fix, I see no reason to change vocabulary, IMO

> AGENT

Confirming my read of your direction:

- **Consistency principle**: every subcommand that touches one or both repos gets `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`. No vocabulary forks.
- **finalize**: `--scope` becomes the canonical flag; `--repo` stays as a back-compat alias.
- **validate-desc / fix-desc**: keep `--scope` (no rename to `--side`). Semantic: `code` validates code's commits against bot, `bot` reverses, `code,bot` does both — that last is the new default.
- **chid/desc/list/show** (via `CommonArgs`): add `--scope` alongside the existing `-R`. Same conflict-or-back-compat call to make as sync.

That gives a sweep of 0.41.0-3 (push), 0.41.0-4 (finalize, now with substance not just review), then post-0.41.0 cycles for clone / validate-desc+fix-desc / CommonArgs.

Want me to capture this as edits to `notes/todo.md` (three new follow-up entries) + a refinement to chores-06.md's [71] plan section noting the finalize back-compat decision and the validate-desc/fix-desc vocabulary call? See subsection below.\n- **0.41.0-2** — `vc-x1 sync --scope=…`. Sync currently\n  hard-codes `[\".\", \".claude\"]` as the default repo set;\n  scope drives that instead. Default-scope resolution\n  reads `.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`\n  (present + non-empty → `code,bot`, else `code`).\n  *Shipped.*\n- **0.41.0-3** — capture `--scope` rollout direction\n  beyond this cycle (consistency principle, finalize\n  back-compat call, validate-desc/fix-desc vocabulary,\n  CommonArgs sweep). Notes only; informs the rest of\n  the cycle and seeds post-0.41.0 follow-ups in\n  `notes/todo.md`. See subsection below.\n- **0.41.0-4** — `vc-x1 push --scope=…` (was `-3`).\n  Trickier: push's state machine assumes dual. Likely\n  splits into a single-repo path (skip `commit-claude` +\n  bookmark for `.claude` + `finalize-claude`) plus a\n  scope-aware preflight summary.\n- **0.41.0-5** — `vc-x1 finalize --scope=…` (was `-4`).\n  Per the 0.41.0-3 direction, `--scope` is the canonical\n  flag and `--repo` becomes a back-compat alias rather\n  than the no-op the original sketch hedged toward.\n- **0.41.0 (final)** — cycle close-out + dogfood\n  validation on a single-repo project (e.g. apply the\n  full sync→edit→push flow against a `--scope=code`\n  fixture)."
}

179	### Versioning
180	
181	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
182	on scope:
183	
184	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
185	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
186	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
187	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
188	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
189	
190	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
191	starting.
192	
193	For multi-step:
194	
195	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
196	   marker.
197	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
198	   needed).
199	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
200	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
201	   marker.
202	
203	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
204	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
205	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
206	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
207	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
208	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
209	doubles typing per commit.
210	
211	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
212	amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
213	to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
214	
```
