```
1	# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
2	
3	## Project Structure
4	
5	This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
6	
7	1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
8	2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
9	
10	Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
11	
12	### Why two repos
13	
14	The split exists to keep the *what* separate from the *why* and
15	*how*, while preserving both as durable history:
16	
17	- **App repo** holds the "what" — code, docs, notes; the artefact
18	  the project is producing.
19	- **Bot session repo** holds the "why" and "how" — the bot's
20	  reasoning, dead ends, and in-session context that produced
21	  each change.
22	- **Cross-referencing** the two via `ochid:` trailers (see below)
23	  produces a coherent narrative across time. Any future reader —
24	  human or bot — can land on a commit and jump to its session
25	  counterpart to recover the *why*.
26	- **Splitting** also keeps app history free of session noise
27	  while preserving session data instead of discarding it after
28	  the turn.
29	
30	### ochid trailers
31	
32	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
33	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
34	path followed by the changeID:
35	
36	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
37	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
38	
39	`vc-x1 push` appends the correct trailer per repo automatically.
40	For manual commits, use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both
41	changeIDs (first line is app repo, second is `.claude`).
42	
43	## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
44	
45	- App repo: `.` (project root)
46	- Bot session repo: `.claude`
47	  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
48	
49	The bot session repo is this project's **`other-repo`** — the
50	path is set by `[workspace].other-repo` in `.vc-config.toml`
51	(default `.claude`, as used here). Concrete examples below use
52	`.claude` for readability; treat the path as configurable where
53	it appears in prose.
54	
55	## Working Directory
56	
57	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
58	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
59	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
60	command failures downstream.
61	
62	When invoking shell commands, prefer the shortest unambiguous path —
63	usually relative to cwd (`ls notes/`, not
64	`ls /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-template-x1/notes/`). Long absolute
65	paths clutter the transcript without adding information. Out-of-workspace
66	paths (`/tmp/...`, `~/.config/...`) stay absolute. Read/Edit/Write
67	tool args also stay absolute — that's a tool-boundary constraint,
68	not a stylistic choice.
69	
70	## Memory
71	
72	Do not use the bot's per-project memory directory
73	(`~/.claude/projects/<path>/memory/`). In a dual-repo setup with
74	CLAUDE.md it provides no capability CLAUDE.md doesn't already cover,
75	and it loses on discoverability:
76	
77	- **CLAUDE.md** — at the repo root, a well-known location for bot
78	  instructions, committed, reviewable, visible to every collaborator
79	  (human or bot).
80	- **Memory directory** — hidden under the user's home, tied to one
81	  machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed.
82	
83	Easy for everyone to find beats convenient for the bot alone. Put
84	durable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.
85	
86	## Speculation marker
87	
88	Durable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit bodies,
89	chores sections — should stick to observations and direct descriptions
90	of the code or data. If a mechanism, hypothesis, or causal claim
91	enters the text, prefix it with "The bot thinks ..." so a reader can
92	tell the measured from the inferred.
93	
94	**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future reader
95	(or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a known fact when
96	it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction worth keeping visible
97	in the written record.
98	
99	**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no
100	marker. Prefix with "The bot thinks ..." (or a close variant like
101	"The bot's guess is ...") when the claim is a mechanism ("X wins
102	because Y caches better"), a cause ("the drift was due to thermal
103	state"), a prediction ("this should scale linearly"), or any
104	reasoning not directly supported by the data on hand.
105	
106	## Writing style: prefer sub-bullets
107	
108	Durable text the bot writes should itemize with sub-bullets
109	rather than run as large prose paragraphs. Applies to:
110	
111	- `notes/chores-*.md`, `notes/todo.md`, committed READMEs, design notes.
112	- Module `//!` docstrings, item `///` doc comments on traits / structs / functions / methods, and any other doc comment that runs more than a few sentences.
113	
114	Short intro sentences (1–3) at the top of a section / item are
115	fine; detail goes into hierarchical bullet lists.
116	
117	**Why:** easier to scan and cross-reference during review;
118	matches how the user reads and edits committed reference
119	material. A wall of prose hides structure that a nested list
120	makes visible — and the same is true inside doc comments,
121	where rustdoc renders the list cleanly.
122	
123	**How to apply:**
124	
125	- Default to bullet structure for any non-trivial section or
126	  doc comment. Reserve paragraphs for brief intros and
127	  occasional contextual framing.
128	- If a paragraph runs past ~3 sentences, look for the implicit
129	  list inside it and break it out.
130	- Design-decision entries benefit most — the *reason* /
131	  *what-was-rejected* / *how-it-applies* decomposition reads
132	  naturally as sub-bullets.
133	- Single-line doc comments on trivial methods stay one line —
134	  bullets are not mandatory, just *available* when content is
135	  long enough to warrant them.
136	
137	**Clap-derive args caveat.** Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]`
138	fields drive `--help` output. Clap reflows by default and
139	collapses bullets into running prose. Add
140	`#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on any field whose doc
141	comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on its own line in
142	the rendered help.
143	
144	## jj Basics
145	
146	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
147	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
148	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
149	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
150	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
151	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
152	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
153	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
154	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
155	  session because session data updates continuously.
156	
157	## vc-x1 Basics
158	
159	`vc-x1` is the workspace tool that wraps jj operations across the
160	app and bot session repos. Once installed, it should drive 99% of
161	the commit / push / finalize flow — direct `jj` is the fallback
162	when something goes off-rails.
163	
164	Subcommands in current use (vc-x1 0.41.0; run
165	`vc-x1 <sub> --help` for the authoritative flag set):
166	
167	- `vc-x1 sync` — fetch and verify (or apply) bookmark divergence
168	  across all workspace repos. See
169	  [Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync`](#pre-step-vc-x1-sync) below.
170	- `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — full commit + push + finalize flow
171	  with two interactive approval gates (review, then message).
172	  The primary command for shipping a step. See
173	  [Run `vc-x1 push`](#run-vc-x1-push) below.
174	- `vc-x1 finalize` — squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo
175	  (every flag is opt-in). Used by `vc-x1 push` internally; also
176	  the manual fallback. See
177	  [Manual finalize fallback](#manual-finalize-fallback) below.
178	- `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` — print change IDs for the
179	  current working commit in each repo, one per line. Used to
180	  compose `ochid:` trailers when committing manually.
181	- `vc-x1 validate-desc` / `vc-x1 fix-desc` — verify (and repair)
182	  cross-repo commit descriptions, including `ochid:` trailers.
183	  `fix-desc` is dry-run by default.
184	- `vc-x1 desc` / `vc-x1 list` / `vc-x1 show` — read-only commit
185	  inspection helpers.
186	- `vc-x1 init` / `vc-x1 clone` / `vc-x1 symlink` — workspace
187	  bootstrap commands.
188	
189	The bot thinks `--scope` is an in-flight `vc-x1 push` flag
190	intended to let push work consistently across single-repo and
191	dual-repo workspaces; the default behavior remains dual-repo,
192	which is what this template demonstrates. When the user
193	references other in-flight flags, treat them as planned
194	additions until `vc-x1 push --help` lists them. For the
195	currently-shipped flag set, see
196	[Run `vc-x1 push`](#run-vc-x1-push) below.
197	
198	For the full command surface, run `vc-x1 --help` and
199	`vc-x1 <subcommand> --help`.
200	
201	## Commits
202	
203	### Per-repo commit commands
204	
205	When committing manually (i.e. not via `vc-x1 push`), use `-R`
206	(`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
207	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the
208	verb/action visible at the start of the command.
209	
210	#### App repo
211	```
212	jj commit -m \
213	"title" \
214	-m "body
215	
216	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
217	-R .
218	```
219	
220	#### Bot session repo
221	```
222	jj commit -m \
223	"title" \
224	-m "body
225	
226	ochid: /<changeID>" \
227	-R .claude
228	```
229	
230	(See [ochid trailers](#ochid-trailers) under Project Structure
231	for what the trailer is and why.)
232	
233	### Commit message style
234	
235	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
236	a version suffix:
237	
238	```
239	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
240	```
241	
242	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
243	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
244	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
245	- **Body** (same across both repos): short intro paragraph (1–3
246	  sentences), then a terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds
247	  one-to-one with the edits structure already documented in
248	  `notes/chores-*.md` for this step — just the file and a
249	  one-line gist (e.g. `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not*
250	  restate the detail that lives in chores; the commit body is a
251	  scan-able index, not a duplicate. The chores section is the
252	  source of truth.
253	- **Session notes** (optional tail, appended to the body): if the
254	  session produced items worth preserving that don't fit the
255	  code-change index — in-session tooling work, dead ends,
256	  rationale — append a trailing subsection titled
257	  `## <other-repo> session notes:` (e.g.
258	  `## .claude session notes:`) with those items as bullets. The
259	  same body (main content plus any session-notes subsection)
260	  lands in both commits.
261	- **`ochid` trailer**: do *not* include `ochid:` in the body you
262	  pass via `--body`. `vc-x1 push` appends the correct per-repo
263	  trailer at the commit stages
264	  (`ochid: /.claude/<chid>` for the app-repo commit;
265	  `ochid: /<chid>` for the `.claude` commit). When committing
266	  manually, append the trailer yourself per the templates above.
267	- Examples:
268	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
269	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
270	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
271	
272	### Chores section headers
273	
274	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
275	
276	```
277	## Description (X.Y.Z)
278	```
279	
280	Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``
281	
282	### Notes references
283	
284	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
285	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
286	
287	### Markdown anchor links
288	
289	GitHub anchor algorithm: lowercase, strip non-alphanumeric
290	characters in place, map remaining spaces to hyphens 1-for-1. Do
291	**not** collapse adjacent whitespace — so `a + b` → `a--b` (spaces
292	on both sides of `+`), but `a: b` → `a-b` (only trailing space on
293	`:`). General markdown reference:
294	[markdownguide.org](https://www.markdownguide.org). GitHub
295	publishes no official spec for auto-generated anchors; the
296	de-facto reference implementation is
297	[github-slugger](https://github.com/Flet/github-slugger).
298	
299	## Code Conventions
300	
301	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
302	
303	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
304	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
305	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
306	comment exists, not that it be long.
307	
308	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
309	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
310	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
311	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
312	non-obvious WHY.
313	
314	For longer doc comments, follow
315	[Writing style: prefer sub-bullets](#writing-style-prefer-sub-bullets)
316	— short intro then a bullet list, with the `verbatim_doc_comment`
317	caveat for clap-derive args.
318	
319	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
320	
321	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
322	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
323	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
324	
325	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition,
326	  invariant, or domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't
327	  self-evident.
328	- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
329	  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).
330	
331	Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment).
332	Abbreviations (e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder
333	ring for readers seeing the code out of context.
334	
335	For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
336	`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable
337	the project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.
338	
339	```rust
340	let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
341	let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");  // OK: obvious
342	
343	match matches.len() {
344	    1 => {
345	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
346	        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
347	        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
348	    }
349	    // ...
350	}
351	```
352	
353	Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the
354	correct test behavior.
355	
356	### Ask for clarification on ambiguous input
357	
358	When user input is ambiguous or missing necessary detail, stop and
359	ask a specific question. Do not proceed on a guess and hope the
360	result lands right — a clarifying question costs a few seconds;
361	redoing misaligned work costs much more.
362	
363	### Recognize when stuck
364	
365	If a simple task has eaten 5+ minutes of thinking or back-and-forth
366	without progress, stop. Summarize what's blocking — unclear
367	requirements, unfamiliar API surface, conflicting signals — and ask.
368	Continued flailing produces worse outcomes than a direct "I'm stuck
369	on X."
370	
371	## Workflow
372	
373	The end-to-end flow for a step is: **edit → review → commit → push
374	→ finalize**, executed via `vc-x1 push` with two interactive
375	approval gates (review, message). Finalize is launched detached as
376	the absolute last action in the turn so trailing bot writes land in
377	the session repo.
378	
379	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end.
380	Single-step and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a
381	single-step change is one `push` invocation; a multi-step change
382	is one `push` per `X.Y.Z-N` commit plus one for the final release
383	commit. Each step gets its own commit, its own push, and its own
384	finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in `.claude` as
385	they happen rather than being batched until the end.
386	
387	### User approval points
388	
389	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without
390	the user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first;
391	only run them after the user confirms. This applies to late
392	changes too — pause for review before squashing into an existing
393	commit. The two approval gates surfaced by `vc-x1 push` (review,
394	message) are part of this discipline, not a substitute for it.
395	
396	### Review before proposing the commit block
397	
398	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
399	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the commit / push commands.
400	Wait for the user to signal review is complete before proposing
401	the commit block. Changes during review are the norm, not the
402	exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise and
403	signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
404	
405	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets
406	a review pause before its commit block appears.
407	
408	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
409	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
410	next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
411	previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
412	step before starting the next; don't ask.
413	
414	### Versioning
415	
416	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
417	on scope:
418	
419	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
420	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
421	- **Multi-step** (for scope that splits naturally into
422	  independently-reviewable steps): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`, implement
423	  across multiple `X.Y.Z-N` commits incrementing the numeric
424	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix to close the ladder.
425	
426	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
427	starting.
428	
429	For multi-step:
430	
431	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
432	   marker.
433	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
434	   needed). Every `X.Y.Z-N` commit is a complete checkpoint in
435	   its own right — the `-N` suffix only signals that more steps
436	   in the same ladder are coming, not that the commit is partial
437	   or exploratory. Run the full pre-commit checklist (including
438	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` updates) on every
439	   `-N` commit exactly as you would on a single-step release.
440	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) to close the ladder.
441	
442	Multi-step cycles surface the ladder at the top of
443	`notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` as a bullet list with `(done)` /
444	`(current)` markers — see the file's intro paragraph for the
445	format. Update the markers as each step ships so the In Progress
446	view stays current at a glance.
447	
448	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
449	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
450	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
451	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the ladder:
452	each `-N` sorts before its final unsuffixed sibling. Cargo
453	accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix adds no information the
454	git log doesn't already convey and doubles typing per commit.
455	
456	The final release commit (no suffix) closes the ladder rather
457	than amending prior commits. This keeps history readable: a
458	chain of complete checkpoints ending in an `X.Y.Z` marker.
459	
460	### Pre-commit checklist
461	
462	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
463	
464	1. `cargo check`
465	2. `cargo fmt`
466	3. `cargo clippy`
467	4. `cargo test`
468	5. `cargo test --release`
469	6. `cargo install --path . --locked` (if applicable) — `--locked`
470	   is required, **not optional**. See
471	   [notes/cargo-locked-issue.md](notes/cargo-locked-issue.md) for
472	   the full story; short version: plain `cargo install` ignores
473	   `Cargo.lock` and re-resolves dependencies from scratch, which
474	   can pick incompatible versions even when `cargo build` /
475	   `cargo test` succeed.
476	7. Retest after install
477	8. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
478	9. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
479	10. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
480	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
481	
482	Push's preflight re-runs `vc-x1 sync` + `cargo fmt` +
483	`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo test` as a
484	safety net. Run the checklist above locally anyway — fmt / clippy /
485	test iteration is cheaper in-conversation than bouncing off
486	preflight after review approval. Steps 6–7 (`cargo install --path .`
487	and retest) are **not** in push's preflight (project-specific); the
488	bot runs them.
489	
490	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync`
491	
492	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` as its first step. Check
493	mode verifies divergence without applying — if anything is `behind`
494	or `diverged`, preflight errors and you resolve explicitly with
495	`vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. This keeps the
496	two approval gates (review, message) authoritative; push never
497	performs an unsupervised rebase.
498	
499	Running sync manually before you *start* editing is still cheap
500	(one line when clean) and surfaces remote changes earlier:
501	
502	```
503	vc-x1 sync --check                    # dual-repo workspace, verify
504	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # apply (rebase / fast-forward)
505	vc-x1 sync --check -R .               # single-repo project
506	vc-x1 sync --check --quiet            # silent; exit code signals result
507	```
508	
509	Bots and scripts must pass `--check` or `--no-check` explicitly —
510	defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive
511	use can take the default (which is `--check`).
512	
513	Output shape:
514	
515	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`.
516	  Note: scope is bookmark-vs-remote tracking, not working-copy
517	  cleanliness — `@` may have uncommitted changes; sync doesn't
518	  speak to that. Proceed.
519	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) under `--check`:
520	  per-repo fetch + state lines, then a fatal
521	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
522	  and re-run`. Inspect, run `vc-x1 sync --no-check`, then proceed.
523	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
524	
525	### Run `vc-x1 push`
526	
527	**Prerequisites — one-time per repo:**
528	
529	- `.gitignore` must include `/.vc-x1` so push's in-progress
530	  state files (`.vc-x1/push-state.toml`) don't get committed.
531	  Push warns at preflight if the entry is missing.
532	- The target bookmark must have a tracking remote
533	  (`jj bookmark track <bookmark>@origin -R .`). `jj git fetch`
534	  creates remote bookmarks as non-tracking by default; push
535	  currently surfaces this as a raw jj error at `push-app` after
536	  commits are locally made, so confirm tracking is set up
537	  before the first push in a new clone.
538	
539	**`vc-x1 push <bookmark>` wraps the full flow in one command with
540	two interactive approval gates.** Push runs preflight (sync + fmt
541	+ clippy + test), prompts for review, composes the commit message
542	(via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`), prompts for message
543	approval, then commits both repos → advances bookmarks → pushes
544	app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the local mutation
545	window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`; after `push-app`
546	succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and recovery is
547	forward-only.
548	
549	```
550	vc-x1 push main                                      # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
551	vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."           # flags skip $EDITOR
552	vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."     # full non-interactive
553	vc-x1 push main --dry-run                            # preview (no side effects)
554	vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                    # resume from specific stage
555	vc-x1 push --status                                  # show saved state
556	vc-x1 push main --restart                            # clear saved state; start fresh
557	```
558	
559	The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:
560	
561	1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
562	   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
563	2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
564	   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
565	   reads right".
566	
567	Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
568	per-repo `ochid:` trailers are appended by push itself. See
569	[Commit message style](#commit-message-style) for body structure.
570	
571	For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`.
572	
573	### Bot communication during the flow
574	
575	When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
576	each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
577	etc.):
578	
579	1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
580	   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
581	   something like:
582	
583	   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
584	   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.
585	
586	2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
587	   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
588	   Do **not** spell out the full
589	   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
590	   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
591	   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
592	   debugging).
593	
594	3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
595	   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
596	   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
597	   detached and absolute-last; see
598	   [Finalize is the absolute last action](#finalize-is-the-absolute-last-action)
599	   below).
600	
601	### Late changes after push
602	
603	If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
604	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
605	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes into `@-` — the
606	bookmark moves with the rewritten commit — then re-push:
607	
608	```
609	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
610	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
611	```
612	
613	If you squash somewhere other than `@-`, add
614	`jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r <target> -R .` between those
615	two commands so the bookmark lands on the rewritten commit.
616	
617	`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
618	push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
619	writes so you rarely hit this case there.
620	
621	### Finalize is the absolute last action
622	
623	`vc-x1 finalize` runs detached with `--squash` and a short
624	`--delay` so any writes the bot makes immediately after launching
625	finalize still land in the squash window and end up in the
626	session-repo commit. **That guarantee depends on finalize being
627	the last thing in the turn.**
628	
629	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session
630	per-step or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a
631	next step, edit files, run tools, or emit any text (prose,
632	recaps, acknowledgements), until the user explicitly directs you
633	to continue. Treat finalize as a hard stop for the whole turn.
634	Any final words (e.g. "next is ...") must be said in the
635	approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the finalize `Bash`
636	call is the last thing in the turn and nothing follows it.
637	
638	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
639	N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
640	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a
641	checkpoint they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or
642	take a break at. Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and
643	produces unwanted writes between finalize and the next explicit
644	instruction.
645	
646	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
647	already displays it. Any trailing text creates writes that miss
648	the finalize squash window.
649	
650	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized
651	at this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
652	
653	### Manual finalize fallback
654	
655	If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
656	was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
657	run finalize by hand:
658	
659	```
660	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
661	```
662	
663	The same "finalize is the absolute last action" rule applies —
664	nothing should happen after finalize. If any work is done after
665	finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
666	
667	**Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery** —
668	manual `vc-x1 finalize`, manual `jj squash --ignore-immutable`
669	+ force-push, etc., all leave `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` pointing
670	at a now-stale halt point. Either `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml`
671	or run `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart` (which clears and
672	restarts in one go) before the next `vc-x1 push`. Otherwise
673	push resumes from a bogus stage and can falsely declare
674	success.
675	
```

> TOOL

tool_result

1	# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions
2	
3	## Project Structure
4	
5	This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
6	
7	1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
8	2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
9	
10	Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
11	
12	## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
13	
14	- App repo: `.` (project root)
15	- Bot session repo: `.claude`
16	  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
17	
18	## Working Directory
19	
20	Prefer staying in the project root. Use `-R` flags or absolute paths
21	to target other directories rather than `cd`. If `cd` seems necessary,
22	discuss with the user first — losing track of cwd causes subtle
23	command failures downstream.
24	
25	When invoking shell commands, prefer the shortest unambiguous path —
26	usually relative to cwd (`ls notes/`, not
27	`ls /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/`). Long absolute paths
28	clutter the transcript without adding information. Out-of-workspace
29	paths (`/tmp/...`, `~/.config/...`) stay absolute. Read/Edit/Write
30	tool args also stay absolute — that's a tool-boundary constraint,
31	not a stylistic choice.
32	
33	## Memory
34	
35	Do not use the bot's per-project memory directory
36	(`~/.claude/projects/<path>/memory/`). In a dual-repo setup with
37	CLAUDE.md it provides no capability CLAUDE.md doesn't already cover,
38	and it loses on discoverability:
39	
40	- **CLAUDE.md** — at the repo root, a well-known location for bot
41	  instructions, committed, reviewable, visible to every collaborator
42	  (human or bot).
43	- **Memory directory** — hidden under the user's home, tied to one
44	  machine, invisible to anyone but the bot, never diffed or reviewed.
45	
46	Easy for everyone to find beats convenient for the bot alone. Put
47	durable context in CLAUDE.md (or committed `notes/`) instead.
48	
49	## Speculation marker
50	
51	Durable text the bot writes — CLAUDE.md, `notes/`, commit bodies,
52	chores sections — should stick to observations and direct descriptions
53	of the code or data. If a mechanism, hypothesis, or causal claim
54	enters the text, prefix it with "The bot thinks ..." so a reader can
55	tell the measured from the inferred.
56	
57	**Why:** unmarked speculation reads like evidence, and a future reader
58	(or the bot on a later session) can pick it up as a known fact when
59	it's not. Measured / inferred is a distinction worth keeping visible
60	in the written record.
61	
62	**How to apply:** observations and factual descriptions need no
63	marker. Prefix with "The bot thinks ..." (or a close variant like
64	"The bot's guess is ...") when the claim is a mechanism ("X wins
65	because Y caches better"), a cause ("the drift was due to thermal
66	state"), a prediction ("this should scale linearly"), or any
67	reasoning not directly supported by the data on hand.
68	
69	## Committing
70	
71	Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
72	relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
73	visible at the start of the command.
74	
75	### App repo
76	```
77	jj commit -m \
78	"title" \
79	-m "body
80	
81	ochid: /.claude/<changeID>" \
82	-R .
83	```
84	
85	### Bot session repo
86	```
87	jj commit -m \
88	"title" \
89	-m "body
90	
91	ochid: /<changeID>" \
92	-R .claude
93	```
94	
95	## jj Basics
96	
97	- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
98	- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
99	- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
100	- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
101	- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
102	  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
103	- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
104	  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
105	- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
106	  session because session data updates continuously.
107	
108	## Commit Message Style
109	
110	Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) with
111	a version suffix:
112	
113	```
114	<type>: <short description> (<version>)
115	```
116	
117	- **Title**: target ~50 chars, short summary of *what* changed.
118	  Include the version. Common types: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`,
119	  `test`, `docs`, `chore`.
120	- **App-repo body**: short intro paragraph (1–3 sentences), then a
121	  terse bullet list. Each bullet corresponds one-to-one with the
122	  edits structure already documented in `notes/chores-*.md` for
123	  this step — just the file and a one-line gist (e.g.
124	  `README.md: new Overview intro`). Do *not* restate the detail
125	  that lives in chores; the commit body is a scan-able index, not
126	  a duplicate. The chores section is the source of truth.
127	- **Session-repo body**: terse intro + a few session-activity
128	  bullets. Doesn't need to mirror chores since it describes
129	  in-session work, not code changes.
130	- Examples:
131	  - `feat: add fix-ochid subcommand (0.22.0)`
132	  - `fix: fix-ochid prefix bug (0.22.1)`
133	  - `refactor: deduplicate common CLI flags (0.21.1)`
134	
135	## Pre-commit Requirements
136	
137	### User approval
138	
139	Never execute commit, squash, push, or finalize commands without the
140	user's explicit approval. Present changes for review first; only run
141	them after the user confirms. This applies to late changes too —
142	pause for review before squashing into an existing commit.
143	
144	### Review before proposing the commit block
145	
146	After finishing a unit of work, **summarize what changed and stop
147	there**. Do not pre-emptively lay out the Checkpoint-1 commit
148	commands. Wait for the user to signal review is complete before
149	proposing the commit block. Changes during review are the norm,
150	not the exception; proposing commit text too early creates noise
151	and signals that I consider the work done when it usually isn't.
152	
153	This applies per-step in a multi-step flow too — each step gets a
154	review pause before its commit block appears.
155	
156	Signals that review is complete include explicit approval ("let's
157	commit", "looks good, commit it") **and any directive to start the
158	next step** ("do step 4", "next", "go N+1"). In that case the
159	previous step must be committed first — always commit the current
160	step before starting the next; don't ask.
161	
162	### Notes references
163	
164	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
165	See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
166	
167	### Markdown anchor links
168	
169	GitHub anchor algorithm: lowercase, strip non-alphanumeric
170	characters in place, map remaining spaces to hyphens 1-for-1. Do
171	**not** collapse adjacent whitespace — so `a + b` → `a--b` (spaces
172	on both sides of `+`), but `a: b` → `a-b` (only trailing space on
173	`:`). General markdown reference:
174	[markdownguide.org](https://www.markdownguide.org). GitHub
175	publishes no official spec for auto-generated anchors; the
176	de-facto reference implementation is
177	[github-slugger](https://github.com/Flet/github-slugger).
178	
179	### Versioning
180	
181	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based
182	on scope:
183	
184	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump
185	  directly to `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
186	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-0`,
187	  implement across multiple commits incrementing the numeric
188	  suffix. The final commit drops the suffix.
189	
190	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before
191	starting.
192	
193	For multi-step:
194	
195	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-0` with the plan and commit as a chore
196	   marker.
197	2. Implement in one or more `X.Y.Z-N` commits (increment N as
198	   needed).
199	3. Final commit bumps to `X.Y.Z` (no suffix), updates
200	   `notes/todo.md` and `notes/chores-*.md` — this is the "done"
201	   marker.
202	
203	Multi-step cycles surface the ladder at the top of
204	`notes/todo.md > ## In Progress` as a bullet list with `(done)` /
205	`(current)` markers — see the file's intro paragraph for the
206	format. Update the markers as each step ships so the In Progress
207	view stays current at a glance.
208	
209	**Why numeric suffixes (`-0`, `-1`, …) rather than `-devN`:**
210	semver pre-release identifiers may consist of a single numeric
211	component, and they compare numerically per spec. So
212	`X.Y.Z-1 < X.Y.Z-2 < … < X.Y.Z` correctly orders the dev ladder
213	below the done marker. Cargo accepts this form. The `-dev` prefix
214	adds no information the git log doesn't already convey and
215	doubles typing per commit.
216	
217	The final release commit (no suffix) signals completion rather than
218	amending prior commits. This keeps history readable and makes it easy
219	to see which commits were exploratory vs final.
220	
221	### Chores section headers
222	
223	Chores section headers use trailing version format:
224	
225	```
226	## Description (X.Y.Z)
227	```
228	
229	Example: `` ## Add `fn claude-symlink` (0.27.0) ``
230	
231	### Pre-commit checklist
232	
233	Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
234	
235	1. `cargo fmt`
236	2. `cargo clippy`
237	3. `cargo test`
238	4. `cargo install --path . --locked` (if applicable) — `--locked`
239	   is required: without it, `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock`
240	   and re-resolves from scratch, which can pick incompatible
241	   versions even when `cargo build` / `cargo test` succeed.
242	5. Retest after install
243	6. Update `notes/todo.md` — add to `## Done` if completing a task
244	7. Update `notes/chores-*.md` — add a subsection describing the change
245	8. Update `notes/README.md` — if functionality changed (new flags,
246	   new subcommands, changed behavior)
247	
248	## Code Conventions
249	
250	### Doc comments on every file, function, and method
251	
252	Every `.rs` file must begin with a `//!` module docstring. Every
253	function and method must have a `///` doc comment. Keep them brief —
254	one sentence of purpose is often enough; the discipline is that the
255	comment exists, not that it be long.
256	
257	This is a deliberate override of the generic "write no comments"
258	default that applies to inline `//` comments. Doc comments on the
259	module / item surface are expected; inline explanatory comments
260	inside function bodies remain discouraged unless they capture a
261	non-obvious WHY.
262	
263	**Shape:** short intro (1–3 sentences, shorter is better), then
264	a `-` bullet list for any structured details. Avoid long prose
265	paragraphs — they read as a wall of text and hide the structure
266	that bullets make scannable. Same shape applies to:
267	
268	- Module / function / struct / field doc comments in `.rs` files.
269	- Chore descriptions in `notes/chores-NN.md`.
270	- Todo entries in `notes/todo.md` (when an item needs more than
271	  one line of detail; pure one-liners are still fine).
272	
273	**Clap-derive args:** doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive
274	`--help` output. Clap reflows by default and collapses bullets
275	into running prose. Add `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, ...)]` on
276	any field whose doc comment uses bullets so each `- …` lands on
277	its own line in the rendered help.
278	
279	### `// OK: …` comments on `unwrap*` calls (Rust)
280	
281	Non-test code that calls `.unwrap()`, `.unwrap_or(…)`,
282	`.unwrap_or_default()`, or `.unwrap_or_else(…)` must have a trailing
283	`// OK: …` comment that justifies why the call is acceptable.
284	
285	- `// OK: <specific reason>` — document the real precondition,
286	  invariant, or domain reason. Preferred whenever the reason isn't
287	  self-evident.
288	- `// OK: obvious` — the default is self-evident from context (e.g.
289	  `desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("")` — empty desc → empty title).
290	
291	Bare `// OK` is not used (reads like a truncated comment).
292	Abbreviations (e.g. `SE`) are not used because they require a decoder
293	ring for readers seeing the code out of context.
294	
295	For provably-unreachable `.unwrap()` calls, also prefix with
296	`#[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]` so the site stays silent if we enable
297	the project-wide `clippy::unwrap_used` lint later.
298	
299	```rust
300	let max = stderr_level.unwrap_or(LevelFilter::Info); // OK: default verbosity when -v/-vv absent
301	let first_line = desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");  // OK: obvious
302	
303	match matches.len() {
304	    1 => {
305	        #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]
306	        // OK: `1 =>` arm guarantees matches.len() == 1
307	        Ok(TitleMatch::One(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))
308	    }
309	    // ...
310	}
311	```
312	
313	Tests (`#[cfg(test)]`) are exempt — panicking on setup failure is the
314	correct test behavior.
315	
316	### Ask for clarification on ambiguous input
317	
318	When user input is ambiguous or missing necessary detail, stop and
319	ask a specific question. Do not proceed on a guess and hope the
320	result lands right — a clarifying question costs a few seconds;
321	redoing misaligned work costs much more.
322	
323	### Recognize when stuck
324	
325	If a simple task has eaten 5+ minutes of thinking or back-and-forth
326	without progress, stop. Summarize what's blocking — unclear
327	requirements, unfamiliar API surface, conflicting signals — and ask.
328	Continued flailing produces worse outcomes than a direct "I'm stuck
329	on X."
330	
331	## ochid Trailers
332	
333	Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
334	counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
335	path followed by the changeID:
336	
337	- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
338	- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
339	
340	Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
341	is app repo, second is `.claude`).
342	
343	## Commit-Push-Finalize Flow
344	
345	**Use `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` — it wraps the full flow in one
346	command with two interactive approval gates.** Push runs
347	preflight (sync + fmt + clippy + test), prompts for review,
348	composes the commit message (via `--title`/`--body` or `$EDITOR`),
349	prompts for message approval, then commits both repos → advances
350	bookmarks → pushes app → finalizes `.claude`. Failures inside the
351	local mutation window roll both repos back via `jj op restore`;
352	after `push-app` succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
353	recovery is forward-only.
354	
355	**Run this flow after every step** — not only at session end.
356	Single-step and multi-step changes are of equal importance: a
357	single-step change is one `push` invocation; a multi-step change
358	is one `push` per `X.Y.Z-N` commit plus one for the final release
359	commit. Each step gets its own commit, its own push, and its own
360	finalize — so dev markers land on the remote and in `.claude` as
361	they happen rather than being batched until the end.
362	
363	### Run `vc-x1 push`
364	
365	```
366	vc-x1 push main                                      # interactive (review + $EDITOR)
367	vc-x1 push main --title "..." --body "..."           # flags skip $EDITOR
368	vc-x1 push main --yes --title "..." --body "..."     # full non-interactive
369	vc-x1 push main --dry-run                            # preview (no side effects)
370	vc-x1 push main --from commit-app                    # resume from specific stage
371	vc-x1 push --status                                  # show saved state
372	vc-x1 push main --restart                            # clear saved state; start fresh
373	```
374	
375	The two approval gates are surfaced by push itself:
376	
377	1. **Review** — push prints `jj diff --stat` for both repos and
378	   prompts `[y/N]`. Approve = "the work is done right".
379	2. **Message** — push either uses `--title`/`--body` (non-editor
380	   path) or opens `$EDITOR` on a template. Approve = "the message
381	   reads right".
382	
383	Both titles and bodies are the **same** across the two commits;
384	only the `ochid:` trailer differs per repo. Push collects the
385	pre-commit chids internally so you don't hand-manage them.
386	
387	For the full flag list and stage machine, see `vc-x1 push --help`
388	and `notes/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)`.
389	
390	### Bot communication during the flow
391	
392	When applying the flow on the user's behalf, use plain prose at
393	each gate — no insider jargon ("Gate N signal", "Checkpoint N",
394	etc.):
395	
396	1. **After completing the work** — summarize what changed
397	   (file-by-file or feature-by-feature, terse) and end with
398	   something like:
399	
400	   > Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the
401	   > commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.
402	
403	2. **After review approval** — present the proposed `$TITLE` and
404	   `$BODY` explicitly, then ask permission to run `vc-x1 push`.
405	   Do **not** spell out the full
406	   `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"`
407	   invocation by default — it's mechanical and obvious from the
408	   title/body. Show it only on explicit request (verbose mode /
409	   debugging).
410	
411	3. **After execution approval** — run the push command. `push`
412	   handles commit + bookmark + push + finalize internally;
413	   nothing should be output after the push command (finalize is
414	   detached and absolute-last; see "After finalize: stop and
415	   wait" below).
416	
417	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)
418	
419	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` as its first step. Check
420	mode verifies divergence without applying — if anything is `behind`
421	or `diverged`, preflight errors and you resolve explicitly with
422	`vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. This keeps the
423	two approval gates (review, message) authoritative; push never
424	performs an unsupervised rebase.
425	
426	Running sync manually before you *start* editing is still cheap
427	(one line when clean) and surfaces remote changes earlier:
428	
429	```
430	vc-x1 sync --check                    # dual-repo workspace, verify
431	vc-x1 sync --no-check                 # apply (rebase / fast-forward)
432	vc-x1 sync --check -R .               # single-repo project
433	vc-x1 sync --check --quiet            # silent; exit code signals result
434	```
435	
436	Bots and scripts must pass `--check` or `--no-check` explicitly —
437	defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive
438	use can take the default (which is `--check`).
439	
440	Output shape:
441	
442	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`.
443	  Note: scope is bookmark-vs-remote tracking, not working-copy
444	  cleanliness — `@` may have uncommitted changes; sync doesn't
445	  speak to that. Proceed.
446	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) under `--check`:
447	  per-repo fetch + state lines, then a fatal
448	  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check
449	  and re-run`. Inspect, run `vc-x1 sync --no-check`, then proceed.
450	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
451	
452	### After finalize: stop and wait
453	
454	After `vc-x1 finalize` is launched — **whether mid-session per-step
455	or at session end** — you **MUST NEVER** proceed to a next step, edit
456	files, run tools, or emit any text (prose, recaps, acknowledgements),
457	until the user explicitly directs you to continue. Treat finalize as
458	a hard stop for the whole turn. Any final words (e.g. "next is ...")
459	must be said in the approval prompt *before* executing finalize; the
460	finalize `Bash` call is the last thing in the turn and nothing
461	follows it.
462	
463	This holds even when the next step seems obvious (e.g. "next is
464	N+1" or "now I should bump the version and commit the release").
465	Wait. The user controls cadence — every push+finalize is a checkpoint
466	they may want to inspect, think about, hand off, or take a break at.
467	Auto-proceeding bypasses that checkpoint and produces unwanted writes
468	between finalize and the next explicit instruction.
469	
470	Exceptions to this rule may emerge later but are not authorized at
471	this stage. Until told otherwise, treat as absolute.
472	
473	### Late changes after push
474	
475	If the app repo needs a tweak after `push-app` succeeded (e.g.
476	updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is immutable. Use
477	`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes into `@-` — the
478	bookmark moves with the rewritten commit — then re-push:
479	
480	```
481	jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
482	jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
483	```
484	
485	If you squash somewhere other than `@-`, add
486	`jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r <target> -R .` between those
487	two commands so the bookmark lands on the rewritten commit.
488	
489	`.claude` is also mutable via this pattern when needed, though
490	push's `finalize-claude` stage normally handles trailing session
491	writes so you rarely hit this case there.
492	
493	### Manual finalize fallback
494	
495	If push exited before `finalize-claude` (e.g. `--no-finalize`
496	was set, or a failure between `push-app` and `finalize-claude`),
497	run finalize by hand:
498	
499	```
500	vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
501	```
502	
503	**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no
504	tool calls, no additional output. If any work is done after
505	finalize, run finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
506	Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
507	already displays it.
508	
509	**Clear push's saved state after any out-of-band recovery** —
510	manual `vc-x1 finalize`, manual `jj squash --ignore-immutable`
511	+ force-push, etc., all leave `.vc-x1/push-state.toml` pointing
512	at a now-stale halt point. Either `rm .vc-x1/push-state.toml`
513	or run `vc-x1 push <bookmark> --restart` (which clears and
514	restarts in one go) before the next `vc-x1 push`. Otherwise
515	push resumes from a bogus stage and can falsely declare
516	success.
517	
```

> AGENT

Read both. CLAUDE2.md is a substantive upgrade — almost everything is the same content, but reorganized and supplemented in ways I think are clear wins. Concise opinion:

**Strong wins (adopt as-is)**

- **"Why two repos" subsection** under Project Structure. Captures the *what / why / how* split rationale that's currently nowhere in CLAUDE.md but is the answer to the most natural question a new reader asks.
- **ochid trailers moved into Project Structure** instead of a stray section near the bottom. Trailers describe the structure; that's where they belong. Eliminates the duplicate.
- **Repo Paths "other-repo" callout** — `.claude` is the configurable counterpart, not a hardcoded path. Aligns with the `.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo` field, and dovetails with the `--scope` rollout.
- **"Writing style: prefer sub-bullets" as its own top-level section** — currently CLAUDE.md has "Shape" guidance only inside the doc-comment subsection. Promoting it generalizes the rule across all durable text and removes the awkward "this also applies to chores/todo/etc" qualifier inside a Rust-doc-specific section.
- **"Commits" parent gathers per-repo commands + message style + chores headers + notes refs + anchor links** — they're all about the same artefact (the commit), and grouping them is more coherent than the current scatter.
- **"Workflow" parent gathers the whole `vc-x1 push` flow + Versioning + Pre-commit checklist** — same reasoning: one umbrella for the end-to-end pipeline rather than three half-overlapping headings.
- **Push prerequisites callout** (`.gitignore` + bookmark tracking) — useful for fresh repos.
- **"Every -N commit is a complete checkpoint"** wording in Versioning — important clarification that -N isn't a partial / WIP marker.
- **"Finalize is the absolute last action"** rename + the new "depends on finalize being the last thing" sentence — clearer than the current "After finalize: stop and wait" header.

**Behavior delta worth a decision (not just adopt blindly)**

- **Commit body policy**. Current CLAUDE.md: app-repo body is the chores index, session-repo body is "terse intro + session-activity bullets" (i.e., *different*). CLAUDE2.md: same body across both, with an optional `## .claude session notes:` tail. The template wording matches what `vc-x1 push` actually does today — push sends one `--body` and uses it for both commits, only the ochid trailer differs. The "different bodies" rule in current CLAUDE.md is aspirational and isn't being enforced. I'd adopt the template wording (matches reality, and the optional session-notes tail gives a place for the activity bullets when they're worth keeping).
- **Pre-commit checklist additions**: CLAUDE2.md adds step 1 `cargo check` and step 5 `cargo test --release`. `cargo check` is essentially free and worth keeping. `cargo test --release` catches release-only bugs but doubles test time on every step — defensible, but I'd flag it for explicit user agreement before adopting.

**Local fix-ups required before swapping in**

- L64 references `vc-template-x1` (template artefact) — swap to `vc-x1`.
- Lines 189–196 contain a "the bot thinks --scope is an in-flight push flag" paragraph appropriate for the template (where --scope hasn't shipped yet) but stale here. In *this* repo, `--scope` is shipped on `init` and `sync`, in flight on `push` (0.41.0-4) and `finalize` (0.41.0-5), planned for clone / validate-desc / fix-desc / CommonArgs. The local version should drop the speculation marker and state the actual rollout state.
- "0.41.0" in the vc-x1-Basics blurb tracks well with where we are; keep it parameterized to update on each minor cycle.

**Recommendation**

Swap CLAUDE.md → CLAUDE2.md (with the three local fix-ups above), pick a side on the body-policy delta, and decide on `cargo test --release`. This is a notes-only change and is independent of the 0.41.0 cycle work — fits cleanly as either a 0.41.0-N notes step or a 0.41.1 single-step refresh after 0.41.0 closes. Probably better as a standalone single-step (own commit, easier to review) than folded into the rollout.

Agreed. The deferral is the right call:

- **Don't change the rulebook mid-cycle.** I'm in the middle of executing the workflow CLAUDE.md describes, and there are still 0.41.0-4 (push --scope), 0.41.0-5 (finalize --scope), 0.41.0 close-out, and `--message-file PATH` ahead. Swapping the doc out from under an in-flight cycle invites subtle drift — the kind where I half-apply old rules and half-apply new ones because the conversation context fights with the file.

- **The "jj vs vc-x1" confusion theory checks out.** In `vc-template-x1` you're a consumer of vc-x1; if the docs don't actively prescribe `vc-x1 push`, falling back to plain `jj commit` is the default failure mode for a bot that knows jj from training. The CLAUDE2.md "vc-x1 Basics" section + the workflow-flavored framing are direct answers to that gap. On *this* repo I'm reflexively in vc-x1 terms because vc-x1 is the artefact I'm editing — the local pull toward `jj` doesn't exist in the same way. So the missing scaffolding hurts you less here, and the cost of swapping prematurely is higher than the benefit.

- **Bundling later is cleaner.** Once `--message-file PATH` ships (and the 0.41.0 cycle closes), the CLAUDE.md refresh is a single-step notes change with three things to settle in one go: (a) body-policy delta (same body + optional session-notes tail vs different bodies), (b) `cargo check` + `cargo test --release` checklist additions, (c) local fix-ups (`vc-x1` over `vc-template-x1`, the `--scope` speculation paragraph rewritten for actual state). One review, one commit. That's nicer than three contested decisions inside an active feature cycle.

- **One small worry to flag now so it isn't lost.** The `--message-file PATH` work will likely interact with the message-handling stage in push (`Stage::Message`, the $EDITOR template, `--title`/`--body` precedence). The current CLAUDE.md doesn't say anything about it because it doesn't ship yet. When CLAUDE2.md drops in, the Run-vc-x1-push table needs to learn about `--message-file` too — easy to miss because it lives in a different invocation column. Mental note for swap time.

1	//! `push` subcommand — collapse the dual-repo commit+push+finalize
2	//! ceremony into a single resumable command.
3	//!
4	//! See `notes/chores-05.md > Add push subcommand (0.37.0)` for the
5	//! full design.
6	//!
7	//! Dev-step ladder (expanded from original 4 to 6 after adding an
8	//! integration-test step ahead of the first dogfood):
9	//!
10	//! - `0.37.0-0` — scaffolding: flag surface, `Stage` enum, stub `push()`
11	//! - `0.37.0-1` — state file + stage-dispatch loop with stage stubs;
12	//!   `--status`, `--restart`, `--from`
13	//! - `0.37.0-2` — real stage bodies (commits, bookmarks, push,
14	//!   finalize) + `jj op` snapshot rollback
15	//! - `0.37.0-3` — integration tests + workspace-root refactor
16	//!   (thread `root: &Path` through every stage so fixtures can
17	//!   point them at tempdirs); first `vc-x1 push` dogfood ships
18	//!   this commit
19	//! - `0.37.0-4` — interactivity: two approval gates, `$EDITOR`,
20	//!   message persistence across resumes
21	//! - `0.37.0-5` — polish: `--dry-run`, `--step`, non-tty handling,
22	//!   `.gitignore` coherence warning
23	//! - `0.37.0` — docs + workflow migration (done marker)

37	/// Named stages of the `push` state machine.
38	///
39	/// Used by `--from <stage>` to resume at a specific point and by
40	/// `--status` to report the current position. Ordered top-down so
41	/// `Stage as u8` comparisons reflect progress through the flow.
42	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
43	#[value(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
44	pub enum Stage {
45	    /// Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest.
46	    Preflight,
47	    /// Present diff for the first approval gate.
48	    Review,
49	    /// Compose / edit the commit message; present for second gate.
50	    Message,
51	    /// Commit the app repo.
52	    CommitApp,
53	    /// Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if empty).
54	    CommitClaude,
55	    /// Advance both bookmarks to `@-`.
56	    BookmarkBoth,
57	    /// `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`.
58	    PushApp,
59	    /// `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b> ...`.
60	    FinalizeClaude,
61	}
62	
63	impl Stage {
64	    /// Return the stage's kebab-case string identifier (matches what
65	    /// the CLI accepts via `--from <stage>` and what `PushState`
66	    /// persists to disk).
67	    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
68	        match self {
69	            Stage::Preflight => "preflight",
70	            Stage::Review => "review",
71	            Stage::Message => "message",
72	            Stage::CommitApp => "commit-app",
73	            Stage::CommitClaude => "commit-claude",
74	            Stage::BookmarkBoth => "bookmark-both",
75	            Stage::PushApp => "push-app",
76	            Stage::FinalizeClaude => "finalize-claude",
77	        }
78	    }
79	
80	    /// Parse a kebab-case stage name back into a `Stage`.
81	    ///
82	    /// Unknown names return `None`; callers should surface a helpful
83	    /// error rather than silently substituting a default.
84	    pub fn from_str(name: &str) -> Option<Self> {
85	        match name {
86	            "preflight" => Some(Stage::Preflight),
87	            "review" => Some(Stage::Review),
88	            "message" => Some(Stage::Message),
89	            "commit-app" => Some(Stage::CommitApp),
90	            "commit-claude" => Some(Stage::CommitClaude),
91	            "bookmark-both" => Some(Stage::BookmarkBoth),
92	            "push-app" => Some(Stage::PushApp),
93	            "finalize-claude" => Some(Stage::FinalizeClaude),
94	            _ => None,
95	        }
96	    }
97	
98	    /// The stage that follows this one, or `None` when this is the
99	    /// last stage (`FinalizeClaude`).
100	    pub fn next(self) -> Option<Self> {
101	        match self {
102	            Stage::Preflight => Some(Stage::Review),
103	            Stage::Review => Some(Stage::Message),
104	            Stage::Message => Some(Stage::CommitApp),
105	            Stage::CommitApp => Some(Stage::CommitClaude),
106	            Stage::CommitClaude => Some(Stage::BookmarkBoth),
107	            Stage::BookmarkBoth => Some(Stage::PushApp),
108	            Stage::PushApp => Some(Stage::FinalizeClaude),
109	            Stage::FinalizeClaude => None,
110	        }
111	    }
112	
113	    /// The first stage in the flow — used when no saved state exists.
114	    pub fn first() -> Self {
115	        Stage::Preflight
116	    }
117	}
118	
119	/// CLI arguments for the `push` subcommand.
120	///
121	/// Flag set mirrors the design in `notes/chores-05.md`. Flags are
122	/// parsed in 0.37.0-0; their real effects land across the remaining
123	/// 0.37.0-N steps. See the module docstring for which flags activate
124	/// when.
125	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
126	pub struct PushArgs {
127	    /// Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
128	    ///
129	    /// Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
130	    /// without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an
131	    /// alias for scripts and for composition with other args. The two
132	    /// forms conflict if both supplied.
133	    #[arg(value_name = "BOOKMARK", conflicts_with = "bookmark")]
134	    pub bookmark_pos: Option<String>,
135	
136	    /// Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional).
137	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "bookmark_pos")]

139	
140	    /// Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1.
141	    #[arg(long)]

143	
144	    /// Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use).
145	    #[arg(long, value_name = "STAGE")]

147	
148	    /// Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate.

534	pub fn push(args: &PushArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
535	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
536	    push_in(&cwd, args)
537	}
538	
539	/// `push` parameterized on the workspace root. CLI dispatch calls
540	/// this with `std::env::current_dir()`; integration tests call it
541	/// with a fixture tempdir so the stage bodies mutate the fixture's
542	/// repos instead of the developer's working tree.
543	pub(crate) fn push_in(
544	    workspace_root: &Path,
545	    args: &PushArgs,
546	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
547	    let layout = resolve_state_layout(workspace_root);
548	
549	    if args.status {
550	        return cmd_status(&layout);
551	    }
552	
553	    if args.dry_run {
554	        info!("push: DRY-RUN — no side effects (no commits, no pushes, no state written)");
555	    }
556	
557	    // Gitignore coherence check (fatal). Only checked when we
558	    // actually have a file path with a parent (resolve_state_layout
559	    // always gives one, so this is effectively always-on).
560	    if let Some(dir_name) = layout
561	        .path
562	        .parent()
563	        .and_then(|p| p.file_name())
564	        .and_then(|s| s.to_str())
565	    {
566	        check_gitignore_coherence(workspace_root, dir_name)?;
567	    }
568	
569	    if args.restart && layout.path.exists() {
570	        fs::remove_file(&layout.path)?;
571	        debug!("push: --restart cleared state at {}", layout.path.display());
572	    }
573	
574	    // Load existing state, or require a bookmark (positional or flag)
575	    // to bootstrap one.
576	    let mut state = match PushState::load(&layout.path)? {
577	        Some(s) => s,
578	        None => {
579	            let bookmark = args
580	                .bookmark_pos
581	                .as_deref()
582	                .or(args.bookmark.as_deref())
583	                .ok_or(
584	                    "push: no saved state; a bookmark is required to start a new run \
585	                     — pass it as a positional (`vc-x1 push main`) or via `--bookmark main`",
586	                )?;
587	            PushState::new_for(bookmark)
588	        }
589	    };
590	
591	    // `--from` overrides the resumed stage (does not affect bookmark
592	    // or other persisted fields).
593	    if let Some(from) = args.from {
594	        state.stage = from;
595	    }
596	
597	    run_from(workspace_root, &mut state, args, &layout)
598	}
599	
600	/// Full path of the `.claude` session repo for a given workspace
601	/// root. Centralized so a future layout change (e.g. configurable
602	/// session-repo name) has one caller to update.
603	fn claude_path(workspace_root: &Path) -> PathBuf {
604	    workspace_root.join(".claude")
605	}
606	
607	/// Print the resumed stage (or "no saved state") and return.
608	fn cmd_status(layout: &StateLayout) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
609	    match PushState::load(&layout.path)? {
610	        Some(state) => {
611	            info!(
612	                "push-state: stage={} bookmark={} started={} (file: {})",
613	                state.stage.as_str(),
614	                state.bookmark,
615	                state.started_at,
616	                layout.path.display()
617	            );
618	        }
619	        None => {
620	            info!(
621	                "push-state: no saved state ({} does not exist)",
622	                layout.path.display()
623	            );
624	        }
625	    }
626	    Ok(())
627	}
628	
629	/// Walk the state machine from `state.stage` to the end, saving
630	/// progress after each stage.
631	///
632	/// Records a `jj op` snapshot in both repos the first time we enter
633	/// `commit-app` and leaves it in state so resume inherits the
634	/// rollback target. On failure inside the rollback-eligible window
635	/// (`commit-app` / `commit-claude` / `bookmark-both`), both repos
636	/// are restored before the error propagates.
637	fn run_from(
638	    root: &Path,
639	    state: &mut PushState,
640	    args: &PushArgs,
641	    layout: &StateLayout,
642	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
643	    // State-sanity preflight: catch stale-state-after-out-of-band-recovery
644	    // (no-op on fresh state). Runs before save so a bogus halt point isn't
645	    // re-persisted. Per chores-06 [61] design.
646	    verify_state_sanity(root, state)?;
647	
648	    // Only persist state in real runs. Dry-runs are inspection-only
649	    // — carrying their inferred chids / op snapshots into real runs
650	    // would mislead the user about what was actually recorded.
651	    if !args.dry_run {
652	        state.save(&layout.path)?;
653	    }
654	    loop {
655	        let stage = state.stage;
656	
657	        // Snapshot op ids once on first `commit-app` entry. Skipped
658	        // in dry-run since we won't mutate anything to roll back.
659	        if stage == Stage::CommitApp && state.op_app.is_none() && !args.dry_run {
660	            state.op_app = Some(current_op_id(root)?);
661	            state.op_claude = Some(current_op_id(&claude_path(root))?);
662	            state.save(&layout.path)?;
663	        }
664	
665	        let result = run_stage(root, stage, state, args, layout);
666	
667	        if let Err(e) = &result {
668	            if stage_is_rollback_eligible(stage) && !args.dry_run {
669	                rollback_on_failure(root, state, e.as_ref());
670	            }
671	            return result;
672	        }
673	
674	        let next = stage.next();
675	
676	        // --step: pause between every stage so the user can inspect
677	        // intermediate state before the next one runs. Only if
678	        // there's a next stage to gate, and skipped entirely when
679	        // --yes is set or stdin isn't a tty (the prompt would hang
680	        // in scripted contexts — the script is presumed consenting).
681	        if args.step && next.is_some() {
682	            if args.yes {
683	                debug!(
684	                    "push step: --yes skips step gate between {} and next",
685	                    stage.as_str()
686	                );
687	            } else if !is_stdin_tty() {
688	                return Err("push: --step requires a tty (stdin is not interactive); \
689	                            add --yes to bypass step gates in non-interactive contexts"
690	                    .into());
691	            } else {
692	                let answer = prompt(&format!(
693	                    "push step: {} done. Continue to {}? [y/N] ",
694	                    stage.as_str(),
695	                    next.map(Stage::as_str).unwrap_or("")
696	                ))?;
697	                let normalized = answer.trim().to_ascii_lowercase();
698	                if normalized != "y" && normalized != "yes" {
699	                    return Err(format!(
700	                        "push: step gate declined after {} (got {answer:?})",
701	                        stage.as_str()
702	                    )
703	                    .into());
704	                }
705	            }
706	        }
707	
708	        match next {
709	            Some(n) => {
710	                state.stage = n;
711	                if !args.dry_run {
712	                    state.save(&layout.path)?;
713	                }
714	            }
715	            None => break,
716	        }
717	    }
718	    if args.dry_run {
719	        info!("push: DRY-RUN complete — no changes written");
720	    } else {
721	        // Honest completion: verify the world matches state before
722	        // declaring success. Catches the 0.37.1 false-success symptom
723	        // (stages "ran" but didn't actually commit / push what state
724	        // says they did). Warning-only because work has already
725	        // crossed the remote boundary by this point — rollback isn't
726	        // sound, the user needs to investigate.
727	        match verify_completion_sanity(root, state) {
728	            Ok(()) => {
729	                if layout.path.exists() {
730	                    fs::remove_file(&layout.path)?;
731	                }
732	                info!("push: completed all stages (verified, state cleared)");
733	            }
734	            Err(e) => {
735	                if layout.path.exists() {
736	                    fs::remove_file(&layout.path)?;
737	                }
738	                warn!("push: completed stages but post-completion verification failed:");
739	                warn!("  {e}");
740	                warn!(
741	                    "  state cleared anyway (work landed on remote); investigate the \
742	                     discrepancy before next push"
743	                );
744	            }
745	        }
746	    }
747	    Ok(())
748	}
749	
750	/// Whether a failure in `stage` should trigger the cross-repo op
751	/// restore. Anything at or past `push-app` crosses the remote
752	/// boundary — the app commit is live on origin, rollback is no
753	/// longer sound — so those failures propagate without touching
```
