789	fn run_stage(
790	    root: &Path,
791	    stage: Stage,
792	    state: &mut PushState,
793	    args: &PushArgs,
794	    layout: &StateLayout,
795	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
796	    match stage {
797	        Stage::Preflight => stage_preflight(root, state, args),
798	        Stage::Review => stage_review(root, args),
799	        Stage::Message => stage_message(root, state, args, layout),
800	        Stage::CommitApp => stage_commit_app(root, state, args),
801	        Stage::CommitClaude => stage_commit_claude(root, state, args),
802	        Stage::BookmarkBoth => stage_bookmark_both(root, state, args),
803	        Stage::PushApp => stage_push_app(root, state, args),
804	        Stage::FinalizeClaude => stage_finalize_claude(root, state, args),
805	    }
806	}
807	
808	/// Preflight: `vc-x1 sync --check && cargo fmt && cargo clippy
809	/// -D warnings && cargo test`.
810	///
811	/// Sync runs in `--check` mode so divergence with the remote is
812	/// surfaced before we burn cargo cycles, but **not** auto-resolved —
813	/// a rebase mid-push is exactly the kind of unsupervised mutation
814	/// the two approval gates exist to prevent. If sync reports action
815	/// needed, preflight errors and the user resolves explicitly with
816	/// `vc-x1 sync --no-check` before re-running push. The cargo steps
817	/// match CLAUDE.md's pre-commit checklist (minus `cargo install` /
818	/// retest, which are project-specific). All subprocesses run in the
819	/// workspace root so cargo picks up the right `Cargo.toml`. Skipped
820	/// in `--dry-run` since `cargo fmt` writes files.
821	fn stage_preflight(
822	    root: &Path,
823	    state: &PushState,
824	    args: &PushArgs,
825	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
826	    if args.dry_run {
827	        info!(
828	            "push preflight: [dry-run] would run verify-tracking / vc-x1 sync --check / cargo fmt / clippy / test"
829	        );
830	        return Ok(());
831	    }
832	    info!("push preflight: verify bookmark tracking");
833	    crate::common::verify_tracking(root, &state.bookmark)?;
834	    crate::common::verify_tracking(&claude_path(root), &state.bookmark)?;
835	    info!("push preflight: vc-x1 sync --check");
836	    run("vc-x1", &["sync", "--check"], root)?;
837	    info!("push preflight: cargo fmt");
838	    run("cargo", &["fmt"], root)?;
839	    info!("push preflight: cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings");
840	    run(
841	        "cargo",
842	        &["clippy", "--all-targets", "--", "-D", "warnings"],
843	        root,
844	    )?;
845	    info!("push preflight: cargo test");
846	    run("cargo", &["test"], root)?;
847	    Ok(())
848	}
849	
850	/// Review: first approval gate — "is the work done right?".
851	///
852	/// Shows a `jj diff --stat` of the pending changes in both repos
853	/// and prompts the user to continue. `--yes` short-circuits the
854	/// prompt (required for scripted / non-tty use). In `--dry-run`
855	/// the diff is still shown (that's the point of dry-run — see
856	/// what *would* be reviewed) but approval is auto-granted.
857	fn stage_review(root: &Path, args: &PushArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
858	    let claude = claude_path(root);
859	    let app_arg = root.to_string_lossy();
860	    let claude_arg = claude.to_string_lossy();
861	    info!("push review: pending changes:");
862	    info!("  app ({app_arg}):");
863	    let app_stat = run("jj", &["diff", "--stat", "-R", &app_arg], root)?;
864	    for line in app_stat.lines() {
865	        info!("    {line}");
866	    }
867	    info!("  .claude ({claude_arg}):");
868	    let claude_stat = run("jj", &["diff", "--stat", "-R", &claude_arg], root)?;
869	    for line in claude_stat.lines() {
870	        info!("    {line}");
871	    }
872	    if args.yes {
873	        info!("push review: auto-approved (--yes)");
874	        return Ok(());
875	    }
876	    if args.dry_run {
877	        info!("push review: [dry-run] auto-approved");
878	        return Ok(());
879	    }
880	    if !is_stdin_tty() {
881	        return Err("push review: stdin is not a tty; \
882	                    pass --yes to auto-approve in non-interactive contexts"
883	            .into());
884	    }
885	    let answer = prompt("push review: approve and continue to message stage? [y/N] ")?;
886	    let normalized = answer.trim().to_ascii_lowercase();
887	    if normalized == "y" || normalized == "yes" {
888	        Ok(())
889	    } else {
890	        Err(format!(
891	            "push review: declined (got {answer:?}); re-run with --yes or confirm with 'y'"
892	        )
893	        .into())
894	    }
895	}
896	
897	/// Message: collect pre-commit changeIDs and record whether
898	/// `.claude` has pending changes so `commit-claude` can skip when
899	/// empty. `--title` and `--body` are required in 0.37.0-2; `$EDITOR`
900	/// support + message persistence land in 0.37.0-3.
901	fn stage_message(
902	    root: &Path,
903	    state: &mut PushState,
904	    args: &PushArgs,
905	    layout: &StateLayout,
906	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
907	    // Resolve title/body by priority:
908	    //   1. --title / --body flags (both must be present to skip editor)
909	    //   2. persisted title/body from prior stage run (resume case)
910	    //   3. $EDITOR template (interactive); fails if --yes and nothing else
911	    let (title, body) = match (args.title.clone(), args.body.clone()) {
912	        (Some(t), Some(b)) => (t, b),
913	        _ => match (state.title.clone(), state.body.clone()) {
914	            (Some(t), Some(b)) => (t, b),
915	            _ => {
916	                if args.yes {
917	                    return Err("push message: --yes given but --title/--body missing \
918	                                and no persisted message to resume — pass both flags \
919	                                or run interactively."
920	                        .into());
921	                }
922	                if args.dry_run {
923	                    return Err("push message: --dry-run given but --title/--body missing \
924	                                and no persisted message — pass both flags so dry-run \
925	                                has a message to preview."
926	                        .into());
927	                }
928	                if !is_stdin_tty() {
929	                    return Err("push message: stdin is not a tty and no --title/--body \
930	                                supplied; cannot launch $EDITOR in a non-interactive \
931	                                context. Pass --title and --body, or run interactively."
932	                        .into());
933	                }
934	                compose_message_via_editor(layout)?
935	            }
936	        },
937	    };
938	
939	    // Persist the composed message so a later resume (e.g. after a
940	    // commit-app retry) doesn't need the flags re-passed.
941	    state.title = Some(title.clone());
942	    state.body = Some(body.clone());
943	
944	    let claude = claude_path(root);
945	    let app_chid = get_change_id(root, "@")?;
946	    let claude_empty = jj_log_empty(&claude, "@")?;
947	    let claude_had_changes = !claude_empty;
948	    let claude_ref = if claude_had_changes { "@" } else { "@-" };
949	    let claude_chid = get_change_id(&claude, claude_ref)?;
950	
951	    info!(
952	        "push message: title=\"{}\", app_chid={app_chid}, claude_chid={claude_chid}, claude_had_changes={claude_had_changes}",
953	        title.lines().next().unwrap_or("") // OK: obvious
954	    );
955	    state.app_chid = Some(app_chid);
956	    state.claude_chid = Some(claude_chid);
957	    state.claude_had_changes = Some(claude_had_changes);
958	    Ok(())
959	}
960	
961	/// Launch `$EDITOR` (falling back to `vi`) on a template file under
962	/// `state_dir`, then parse the saved content into a `(title, body)`
963	/// tuple. Lines starting with `#` are treated as comments and
964	/// stripped. Empty input aborts the push.
965	fn compose_message_via_editor(
966	    layout: &StateLayout,
967	) -> Result<(String, String), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
968	    let editor = std::env::var("EDITOR")
969	        .or_else(|_| std::env::var("VISUAL"))
970	        .unwrap_or_else(|_| "vi".to_string()); // OK: POSIX fallback when nothing is configured
971	    let msg_path = layout
972	        .path
973	        .parent()
974	        .ok_or("push message: state layout has no parent dir")?
975	        .join("push-message.txt");
976	    if let Some(parent) = msg_path.parent() {
977	        fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
978	    }
979	    let template = "\
980	# Leave as-is to abort. Enter the Title on the first line,
981	# optionally followed by the Body on subsequent lines. The
982	# blank line between Title and Body is inserted automatically,
983	# and the ochid trailer is appended per-repo automatically —
984	# don't add either here.
985	# Lines starting with `#` are ignored.
986	";
987	    fs::write(&msg_path, template)?;
988	    info!("push message: launching {editor} on {}", msg_path.display());
989	    let status = std::process::Command::new(&editor)
990	        .arg(&msg_path)
991	        .status()
992	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to launch {editor}: {e}"))?;
993	    if !status.success() {
994	        return Err(format!("{editor} exited non-zero ({status})").into());
995	    }
996	    let content = fs::read_to_string(&msg_path)?;
997	    let _ = fs::remove_file(&msg_path);
998	    parse_message(&content)
999	        .ok_or_else(|| "push message: template was left empty or all-comments — aborting".into())
1000	}
1001	
1002	/// Parse the editor-saved message into `(title, body)`. Strips
1003	/// `#`-prefixed comment lines, trims surrounding blanks, and treats
1004	/// the first non-comment line as the title with everything after as
1005	/// the body (a blank line between is allowed but not required —
1006	/// the commit stages insert the title/body separator themselves).
1007	/// Returns `None` when the message has no non-comment content.
1008	fn parse_message(raw: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {
1009	    let mut meaningful = String::new();
1010	    for line in raw.lines() {
1011	        let trimmed_left = line.trim_start();
1012	        if trimmed_left.starts_with('#') {
1013	            continue;
1014	        }
1015	        meaningful.push_str(line);
1016	        meaningful.push('\n');
1017	    }
1018	    let trimmed = meaningful.trim();
1019	    if trimmed.is_empty() {
1020	        return None;
1021	    }
1022	    let mut lines = trimmed.lines();
1023	    let title = lines.next().unwrap_or("").trim().to_string(); // OK: trimmed non-empty ⇒ first line exists
1024	    let body = lines.collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n").trim().to_string();
1025	    if title.is_empty() {
1026	        return None;
1027	    }
1028	    Some((title, body))
1029	}

1056	            "-R",
1057	            &app_arg,
1058	            "-m",

> AGENT

OK, I have a working mental model. Here's my proposed shape for 0.41.0-4 before I start writing code.

**Architectural read on push**

Push is an 8-stage state machine with persistent state across resumes. Stages are tightly bound to the dual-repo assumption: `commit-app` / `commit-claude` are sister stages, ochid trailers are baked in, `bookmark-both` literally iterates two paths, `push-app` and `finalize-claude` are the per-repo terminal stages. `claude_had_changes` already gives us conditional skipping (for empty `.claude`); scope adds a second axis.

**Approach: scope as a pruner, not a parallel state machine**

Keep the same 8 stages; each becomes a no-op when its target side is out of scope. Cleaner than forking the dispatcher, preserves resume-from-stage semantics, minimizes test churn.

**Changes by stage**

| Stage | code,bot (today) | code only | bot only |
|---|---|---|---|
| Preflight | sync+verify both | sync `--scope=code` + verify app | sync `--scope=bot` + verify claude |
| Review | both diffs | app diff | claude diff |
| Message | both chids | app chid only, no trailer | claude chid only, no trailer |
| CommitApp | with `/.claude/<chid>` trailer | no trailer | skip |
| CommitClaude | with `/<chid>` trailer (skip if empty) | skip | no trailer (skip if empty) |
| BookmarkBoth | both | app only | claude only |
| PushApp | as today | as today | skip |
| FinalizeClaude | as today | skip | as today (still detached, captures trailing writes) |

Cargo fmt/clippy/test in preflight stay always-on — they're workspace-level safety, cheap, and prove the app compiles even on bot-only pushes.

**State persistence**

Add `scope: Vec<Side>` to `PushState`. Resolved once at push entry: `--scope` flag → state's existing scope (resume) → `common::default_scope(Some(workspace_root))`. Once stored, resumes use it verbatim — re-resolving from current state would be racy if the user edited config between runs.

**Verify hooks** (`verify_state_sanity`, `verify_completion_sanity`)

Both currently check both sides. Make them check only in-scope sides.

**Edge cases worth flagging now**

1. **Bot-only push with empty `.claude`** — there's nothing to do. Should error early at message stage with a useful message ("--scope=bot but .claude has no pending changes").
2. **cwd portability** — push currently requires cwd = workspace root (`claude_path(workspace_root)` is `workspace_root.join(".claude")`). I'd leave this as-is; cwd-portability for push is a separate cycle.
3. **Tests** — existing integration tests on dual fixtures should pass unchanged (default scope on a dual workspace = code,bot = today's behavior). Add three new tests: explicit `--scope=code,bot`, `--scope=code`, `--scope=bot` against the same Fixture.

**Cycle proposal**

Single `0.41.0-4` step rather than splitting further. The scope-as-pruner approach keeps the diff focused and reviewable as one unit. Estimated ~250-350 lines of code change across `src/push.rs` (flag + state + per-stage guards + verify hooks) plus tests + chores + README.

Anything you want me to adjust before I bump and start implementing? Particular friction points: the bot-only-with-empty-claude error wording, whether bot-only should bypass FinalizeClaude (i.e., do a plain push without the squash+detach), or whether `--scope` should ever dispatch to `vc-x1 finalize --scope=bot` rather than running the stages itself.

> DEVELOPER

1) if the user asks to push --scope=bot and there is not .claude it's an error but push --scope=code it's ok

> AGENT

Got it — `--scope=bot` requires the bot repo to *exist* (workspace has `other-repo`, scope_to_repos already errors here); empty-but-existing `.claude` flows through normally and may produce a no-op push, same as `vc-x1 sync` does when up-to-date. `--scope=code` is always fine because the app side is the universal anchor.

That matches `scope_to_repos`'s existing semantics — clean. I'll wire push's scope resolution through that helper so the error message is consistent.

Carrying the other two open items at my own discretion:
- **cwd portability** — leave as-is for push (still requires cwd = workspace root). Separate cycle if we want it.
- **bot-only finalize-claude** — keep the FinalizeClaude stage in the bot-only path. It's the right tool for capturing trailing writes.

Proceeding. Bumping to 0.41.0-4.

254	pub struct PushState {
255	    /// State-file format version; must match `STATE_FORMAT_VERSION`
256	    /// to be considered readable.
257	    pub version: u32,
258	    /// The next stage to execute on resume.
259	    pub stage: Stage,
260	    /// Bookmark being advanced by this run (persisted so resume
261	    /// doesn't need `--bookmark` again).
262	    pub bookmark: String,
263	    /// ISO-8601 UTC timestamp captured when the state was first
264	    /// written. Informational — helps the user spot stale state.
265	    pub started_at: String,
266	    /// App-repo changeID captured at `message` stage (before
267	    /// `commit-app` runs). Stable across `jj commit` — becomes the
268	    /// chid of the just-committed change. Used when composing the
269	    /// `.claude` commit's ochid trailer. Added in 0.37.0-2.
270	    pub app_chid: Option<String>,
271	    /// `.claude` repo changeID used by the app-repo commit's ochid
272	    /// trailer. Either the pre-commit `@` chid (when `.claude` has
273	    /// pending changes — becomes `@-` after commit) or the current
274	    /// `@-` chid (when `.claude` is clean — stays stable). Added in
275	    /// 0.37.0-2.
276	    pub claude_chid: Option<String>,
277	    /// Whether `.claude`'s working copy had changes at `message`
278	    /// time. Decides whether `commit-claude` actually runs or
279	    /// skips. Added in 0.37.0-2.
280	    pub claude_had_changes: Option<bool>,
281	    /// `jj op` id of the app repo captured before `commit-app`. On
282	    /// failure in stages 4-6, `jj op restore` rewinds here. Added
283	    /// in 0.37.0-2.
284	    pub op_app: Option<String>,
285	    /// `jj op` id of `.claude` captured before `commit-app`. Same
286	    /// rollback target as `op_app`. Added in 0.37.0-2.
287	    pub op_claude: Option<String>,
288	    /// Composed commit title — persisted so resume doesn't need
289	    /// `--title` re-passed. Set during `message` stage from either
290	    /// `--title` or `$EDITOR`. Added in 0.37.0-4.
291	    pub title: Option<String>,
292	    /// Composed commit body (sans ochid trailer, which each commit
293	    /// stage appends). Persisted alongside title. Multi-line
294	    /// content is escaped for the flat-TOML save format (see
295	    /// `escape_multiline` / `unescape_multiline`). Added in
296	    /// 0.37.0-4.
297	    pub body: Option<String>,
298	}
299	
300	impl PushState {
301	    /// Build a fresh state for a new run.
302	    pub fn new_for(bookmark: &str) -> Self {
303	        PushState {
304	            version: STATE_FORMAT_VERSION,
305	            stage: Stage::first(),
306	            bookmark: bookmark.to_string(),
307	            started_at: Utc::now().to_rfc3339(),
308	            app_chid: None,
309	            claude_chid: None,
310	            claude_had_changes: None,
311	            op_app: None,
312	            op_claude: None,
313	            title: None,
314	            body: None,
315	        }
316	    }
317	
318	    /// Write the state to `path`, creating parent dirs as needed.
319	    ///
320	    /// Values are single-line and contain no `"` characters under
321	    /// normal operation (stage names are kebab-case, chids are
322	    /// ASCII, bookmark names don't contain quotes, timestamps are
323	    /// ASCII). A defensive escape pass replaces any stray `"` with
324	    /// `\"` so the file always parses with `toml_simple`. Optional
325	    /// fields are only emitted when set so older state files don't
326	    /// carry a wall of blank keys.
327	    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
328	        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
329	            fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
330	        }
331	        let mut content = String::new();
332	        content.push_str("# vc-x1 push state — managed file, do not edit by hand\n");
333	        content.push_str("[push-state]\n");
334	        content.push_str(&format!("version = {}\n", self.version));
335	        content.push_str(&format!("stage = \"{}\"\n", self.stage.as_str()));
336	        content.push_str(&format!("bookmark = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(&self.bookmark)));
337	        content.push_str(&format!(
338	            "started_at = \"{}\"\n",
339	            escape_toml(&self.started_at)
340	        ));
341	        if let Some(v) = &self.app_chid {
342	            content.push_str(&format!("app_chid = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
343	        }
344	        if let Some(v) = &self.claude_chid {
345	            content.push_str(&format!("claude_chid = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
346	        }
347	        if let Some(v) = self.claude_had_changes {
348	            content.push_str(&format!("claude_had_changes = {v}\n"));
349	        }
350	        if let Some(v) = &self.op_app {
351	            content.push_str(&format!("op_app = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
352	        }
353	        if let Some(v) = &self.op_claude {
354	            content.push_str(&format!("op_claude = \"{}\"\n", escape_toml(v)));
355	        }
356	        if let Some(v) = &self.title {
357	            content.push_str(&format!("title = \"{}\"\n", escape_multiline(v)));
358	        }
359	        if let Some(v) = &self.body {
360	            content.push_str(&format!("body = \"{}\"\n", escape_multiline(v)));
361	        }
362	        fs::write(path, content)?;
363	        debug!("push: wrote state to {}", path.display());
364	        Ok(())
365	    }
366	
367	    /// Load state from `path`, returning `Ok(None)` if the file is
368	    /// absent (a fresh run) and `Err` if the file exists but is
369	    /// unusable (stale format, missing required keys, unknown stage).
370	    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Option<Self>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
371	        if !path.exists() {
372	            return Ok(None);
373	        }
374	        let map = toml_load(path)?;
375	        let require = |k: &str| -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
376	            map.get(k)
377	                .cloned()
378	                .ok_or_else(|| format!("push state {}: missing key '{k}'", path.display()).into())
379	        };
380	        let version: u32 = require("push-state.version")?
381	            .parse()
382	            .map_err(|e| format!("push state {}: invalid version: {e}", path.display()))?;
383	        if version != STATE_FORMAT_VERSION {
384	            return Err(format!(
385	                "push state {}: unsupported format version {version} (expected {}); \
386	                 re-run with --restart to start fresh",
387	                path.display(),
388	                STATE_FORMAT_VERSION
389	            )
390	            .into());
391	        }
392	        let stage_str = require("push-state.stage")?;
393	        let stage = Stage::from_str(&stage_str)
394	            .ok_or_else(|| format!("push state {}: unknown stage '{stage_str}'", path.display()))?;
395	        let claude_had_changes = match map.get("push-state.claude_had_changes") {
396	            Some(s) => Some(s.parse::<bool>().map_err(|e| {
397	                format!(
398	                    "push state {}: invalid claude_had_changes: {e}",
399	                    path.display()
400	                )
401	            })?),
402	            None => None,
403	        };
404	        Ok(Some(PushState {
405	            version,
406	            stage,
407	            bookmark: require("push-state.bookmark")?,
408	            started_at: require("push-state.started_at")?,
409	            app_chid: map.get("push-state.app_chid").cloned(),
410	            claude_chid: map.get("push-state.claude_chid").cloned(),
411	            claude_had_changes,
412	            op_app: map.get("push-state.op_app").cloned(),
413	            op_claude: map.get("push-state.op_claude").cloned(),
414	            title: map.get("push-state.title").map(|s| unescape_multiline(s)),
415	            body: map.get("push-state.body").map(|s| unescape_multiline(s)),
416	        }))
417	    }
418	}
419	
420	/// Escape any `"` in a value so the single-line TOML strings we emit
421	/// stay parseable. `toml_simple` trims the surrounding quotes on read
422	/// but doesn't process escapes, so we just avoid characters that
423	/// would break the single-line form.

> AGENT

Sharp instinct. `--scope=single` does something the current vocabulary doesn't cleanly express — and on reflection I think the gap shows up retroactively in sync/init too, not just in push.

**What `single` would distinguish**

Today's `code` actually plays two different roles depending on context:
- In a dual-repo workspace, `code` = "the app side of the pair" (a *role*).
- In POR or a single-repo workspace, `code` resolves to cwd as a fallback — really meaning "this lone repo" (a *mode*).

`single` would split these honestly: roles (`code`, `bot`, `code,bot`) for dual-repo work; `single` for the no-dispatcher mode. Default-scope resolution becomes more honest too — `single` for POR / single-repo workspaces, `code,bot` for dual-repo. The current "POR happens to default to code which happens to resolve to cwd" works but is implicit.

**Friction points worth flagging now**

1. **Type shape.** `Scope(Vec<Side>)` with `Side ∈ {Code, Bot}` is clean. Adding `Single` to `Side` makes `vec![Code, Single]` representable but nonsense — needs validation. Probably better as a sum type: `enum Scope { Sides(Vec<Side>), Single }`. That's a small refactor.

2. **Sync's `-R`.** Sync today has `-R` (arbitrary repo set) mutually exclusive with `--scope`. With `single` available, the relationship needs deciding: does `single` *imply* a single `-R`? Stay both? Deprecate `-R` once `single` lands? Real call to make, not just code.

3. **Init's vocabulary.** `init --scope=code` today means "create a single-repo workspace". Honest rename: `init --scope=single` for single-repo, `init --scope=code,bot` for dual. That's a behavior change for users who already adopted `init --scope=code`. Possibly OK since init is bootstrap-only, but worth flagging.

4. **Per-command applicability.** `validate-desc --scope=single` is meaningless (validate needs another repo to validate against). So `single` isn't valid everywhere. The per-command rollout map in 0.41.0-3 needs updating to call out which commands accept which subset of scope values.

5. **What I've already partially started for 0.41.0-4** (Cargo.toml bump + push.rs imports + `--scope` flag on PushArgs) needs to be unwound or paused — push --scope shouldn't ship before `single` design lands.

**Recommended cycle restructure**

1. **0.41.0-4 (revised)** — pause the push code; revert Cargo.toml back, revert the push.rs partial changes. Notes-only step capturing `--scope=single` design: type shape, sync `-R` decision, init vocabulary call, per-command applicability matrix.
2. **0.41.0-5** — implement `single` in `scope.rs` + `common.rs` (refactor to sum-type `Scope`), retrofit sync to accept it, decide init's path (rename or leave).
3. **0.41.0-6** — push --scope (now consumes all three values).
4. **0.41.0-7** — finalize --scope.
5. **0.41.0** — close-out.

Adds two steps but each one stays reviewable. The alternative — folding the `single` design into a single big push step — is exactly the kind of bundled change the dev ladder exists to avoid.

Want me to revert the partial 0.41.0-4 changes and pivot to the notes-only design step? Or discuss further before committing the restructure?

> AGENT

Slowing down. Just thoughts on the meaning, no design conclusions.

**Your proposal in a sentence**: split `code|bot|code,bot` (roles requiring a workspace) from `single` (mode requiring no workspace). Default depends on whether `.vc-config.toml` exists. That's a cleaner mental model than today's "code happens to fall back to cwd in POR mode."

**Reactions**

1. **Pro: the role/mode split is honest.** Today's `code` is overloaded — it's a workspace role *and* an implicit fallback for POR. Your proposal makes a user think "do I have a workspace? then `code/bot/code,bot`. otherwise `single`." That's obvious in the way you want.

2. **"cwd means nothing" is *almost* right.** In dual-repo modes, cwd doesn't drive operation — workspace config does. But cwd still matters for *discovery*: `find_workspace_root` walks up from cwd to locate `.vc-config.toml::path = "/"`. If you're at `/tmp/random` with no enclosing workspace, no command can find one. So cwd is irrelevant *once the workspace is found*, but it's the entry point. Worth being precise about so the doc doesn't say "cwd doesn't matter" and then surprise someone running from a path that doesn't reach the config.

3. **The single-repo-workspace edge case needs a decision.** Today the [65] design recognizes three states: POR (no config), single-repo workspace (config with `path` but no `other-repo`), dual workspace (config with both). Under your proposal, what does `--scope=code` do in a single-repo workspace? Two options:
   - Treat single-repo workspace like POR: `code/bot/code,bot` all error, only `single` works.
   - Treat single-repo workspace as a degenerate dual: `code` is valid (resolves to `path`), `bot` errors.
   
   I'd lean (a) — drop the single-repo-workspace concept entirely. If you have `.vc-config.toml`, you have *both* roles. Otherwise you're POR. That keeps the rule binary: config exists ⇒ workspace ⇒ dual roles; no config ⇒ POR ⇒ `single`. Simpler.

4. **`--scope=single` target — your three options:**
   - `cwd`: clean as the implicit default.
   - `--repo <path>` (existing flag on sync, not on push): cleanest explicit form. Each command would gain `--repo <path>` if it doesn't have it.
   - `--scope=<path>`: I'd skip. Lets you write `--scope=/some/path` which mixes a path namespace into a value enum. Type-wise it's a sum (paths and enum values share one input), and you'd lose tab-completion of valid scope values. Uglier than the gain.
   
   So: `cwd` by default; `--repo <path>` for explicit override; don't overload `--scope` itself with paths.

**One thing your proposal implicitly settles**

Sync's existing `-R/--repo` (arbitrary multi-repo escape hatch) becomes `--repo` (single-repo target for `--scope=single`). That's a semantic narrowing — today `-R` accepts repeatable + comma-separated for arbitrary repo lists. Are you OK losing that, or do we keep it for the rare multi-repo workspace case? My guess: drop it. Multi-repo-beyond-pair was hypothetical anyway.

Want me to dig deeper on any of these, or step back further to a different question?

> AGENT

Got it on all three. Updating my mental model:

**Three workspace states stay**, each with its own valid scope set:

| State | `single` | `code` | `bot` | `code,bot` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| POR (no `.vc-config.toml`) | ✓ default | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| Single-repo workspace (`path`, no `other-repo`) | ✓ | ✓ default | ✗ | ✗ |
| Dual workspace (`path` + `other-repo`) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ default |

**`single` as an always-available escape hatch** — works in any state including dual. Says "ignore `.vc-config.toml` for this command." That's the explicit-error-recovery use case you described.

**`-R/--repo` is single-target** — drops the comma-separated + repeatable behavior. `--repo <PATH>` accepts one path. Pairs with `--scope=single` to override cwd.

**Things I'd want to settle next, in order**

1. **Coupling between `--repo` and `--scope=single`.** Three options:
   - (a) `--repo <PATH>` implies `--scope=single`. Concise but couples mode and target into one flag.
   - (b) `--scope=single` is required; `--repo` is an optional target override (default = cwd). Two flags, more explicit.
   - (c) Either path: `--scope=single` alone works (target = cwd); `--repo <PATH>` alone works (mode inferred); both together also work.
   
   I'd lean (b) for "obvious to the user" — `--scope=single` is always present so the mode is always visible in the invocation.

2. **Default-scope detection.** Three states means three different defaults. Easy to compute by reading `.vc-config.toml`'s presence and the `other-repo` field. Already mostly built (the helper I added in 0.41.0-2 handles two of three states; just needs the single-repo-workspace case to default to `code` rather than the current `code` fallback that also covers POR).

3. **Sync's existing `-R` back-compat.** Today `-R` accepts repeatable + comma-separated. Dropping that breaks any existing `vc-x1 sync -R . -R .claude` invocation (mine included — it's in CLAUDE.md examples). Two paths: (a) just break it, document in chores. (b) Add deprecation warning for one minor cycle, then break. Probably (a) since this work is pre-1.0 and the audience is small. Worth your call.

4. **What `--scope=bot` means in a single-repo workspace.** Single-repo workspace has no `other-repo`. `--scope=bot` should error with "no bot repo configured — drop --scope or use --scope=single." Same error helper as today.

Anything I'm misreading? Or want me to push further on the (a)/(b)/(c) coupling question?

> AGENT

Worth exploring. Pulling on it:

**What `--scope=<path>` does that the (b) two-flag form doesn't**

- One flag instead of two for the single-repo case.
- `--scope=.` directly states "this dir, ignore config" — no meta-keyword needed.
- Removes the "does `--repo` imply `--scope=single`?" coupling question entirely. There's no `--repo` to couple.

**What it costs**

1. **Value-namespace mixing.** `--scope` becomes a sum: kebab keywords (`code|bot|code,bot`) OR a path. Clap's `[possible values: ...]` in `--help` only shows the enum side; users have to read prose to learn paths work too. Discoverability hit.

2. **Disambiguation.** Bare `--scope=foo` — keyword or relative path `./foo`? Cleanest rule: bare names are always keywords; paths require a prefix (`./`, `/`, `~/`). So `--scope=foo` errors as "unknown keyword foo"; `--scope=./foo` is a path. Documentable but adds a wrinkle a user has to learn.

3. **`single` keyword goes away.** Under the new model, `--scope=.` *is* what `--scope=single` would have meant. Saves one keyword from the vocabulary, but `single` was the most discoverable form ("I want single-repo mode") — `--scope=.` is cuter but less obvious to a user reading `--help`.

4. **Internal type shape.** Goes from `Scope(Vec<Side>)` to a sum:
   ```
   enum Scope {
       Roles(Vec<Side>),     // code | bot | code,bot
       Single(PathBuf),      // explicit path
   }
   ```
   Not a problem, just an honest accounting.

**Middle ground worth considering**

Keep `single` as a keyword *and* allow paths under one `--scope` flag:
- `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` → roles
- `--scope=single` → single mode, target = cwd (the discoverable form)
- `--scope=<path>` → single mode, target = path (the explicit form)

That preserves the discoverable keyword for the common case while still letting power users write `--scope=/some/path` directly. Costs you nothing extra design-wise — just two equivalent ways to express the single-repo case.

**My lean**

Slight preference for the middle ground over the strict `--scope=<path>` only, because of point (3) — losing `single` from the help-listed values hurts the "obvious to the user" goal. But strict `--scope=<path>` is genuinely simpler and might be the right call once paths-as-scope feels natural. I don't think we have enough info to settle which yet — could see going either way.

Want me to push further on this, or step back to a different question?

> AGENT

Settled then. Quick recap to make sure we're aligned, plus the open items I see.

**Vocabulary (settled)**
- `--scope=code|bot|code,bot|bot,code|<path>`
- Keywords are bare names; anything starting with `./`, `/`, or `~/` is a path
- Type internally becomes a sum: `enum Scope { Roles(Vec<Side>), Single(PathBuf) }`
- No `single` keyword; `--scope=.` is the discoverable cwd form

**Implementation implication**

Clap's value-derive can't auto-handle this sum. We'll need a custom value parser (via `value_parser = parse_scope`) that:
- Returns `Roles(...)` for keyword values
- Returns `Single(expanded_path)` for prefixed paths (with `~/` expansion to mirror init's existing behavior)
- Errors with a helpful message on bare names that don't match the keyword set ("did you mean `./foo`?")

The retrofit touches everything that has `--scope` today: `init` (already shipped), `sync` (already shipped). Both currently use `Vec<Side>` — they'd need to migrate to the sum type. Behavior change for users who already adopted them, but no real users yet beyond us.

**Open items**

1. **Default-scope rules.** With three workspace states:
   - POR → `Single(cwd)`
   - Single-repo workspace (`path` only) → `Roles([Code])`
   - Dual workspace (`path` + `other-repo`) → `Roles([Code, Bot])`
   
   Confirm?

2. **Sync's existing `-R/--repo`.** Once `--scope=<path>` exists, `-R` is functionally redundant (`--scope=./foo` covers it). Drop entirely, or keep as a short alias? Lean drop — vocabulary uniformity beats one-letter convenience.

3. **Per-command applicability.** Some commands won't accept `<path>` meaningfully — e.g. `validate-desc` requires *both* repos by definition, so `Single(_)` doesn't apply. The 0.41.0-3 capture's per-command rollout map needs updating to call out which commands accept `Roles` only, `Single` only, or both.

4. **Cycle structure for what's left.** This is a bigger refactor than the original `--scope=single` thought. My read on the dev ladder ahead:
   - 0.41.0-4: notes-only design capture (revert the partial push code I started). Records the sum-type vocabulary, rules, and per-command matrix.
   - 0.41.0: close out 0.41.0 as "scope foundation + sync + design captured for the sum-type direction". Stop the cycle here.
   - 0.42.0-N: separate cycle for the sum-type refactor (Scope type + CLI parser + sync/init migration + push --scope + finalize --scope + close).
   
   Reasoning: 0.41.0's original premise was "wire `--scope` into push/finalize using the existing `Vec<Side>` shape". The refactor to a sum type changes the type model — that's a different scope of change and deserves its own cycle. Closing 0.41.0 now produces a clean checkpoint; reopening with 0.42.0 announces the deeper refactor honestly.

Thoughts on (1)–(4)? Especially (4) — the cycle restructure is a bigger reframe than the smaller in-cycle inserts I was proposing earlier.

1298	### 0.41.0-3: capture --scope-everywhere direction
1299	
1300	Notes-only step. Records the directional decisions that
1301	came out of the 0.41.0-2 close-out conversation
1302	(2026-04-26) so the rest of the cycle and the post-0.41.0
1303	follow-ups don't relitigate them.
1304	
1305	**Consistency principle (decided).** Every subcommand that
1306	operates on one or both repos gets `--scope=code|bot|code,
1307	bot`. No vocabulary forks across commands; `--scope` is the
1308	project-wide convention. Existing per-command flags
1309	(`-R/--repo`, `--repo`) stay as back-compat aliases where
1310	they already exist; new commands inherit `--scope` from day
1311	one.
1312	
1313	**Per-command rollout map.**
1314	
1315	- `init` — done (0.40.0). `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`.
1316	- `sync` — done (0.41.0-2). `--scope` + `-R` (mutually
1317	  exclusive); `-R` retained for arbitrary multi-repo.
1318	- `push` — 0.41.0-4. State machine becomes scope-aware;
1319	  single-side path skips `commit-claude` /

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-06.md",
  "old_string": "  In Progress reshaped — explanatory intro paragraph\n  shows the dev-ladder format, and the active entry is\n  rewritten as the bulleted ladder with\n  `(done)` / `(current)` markers.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "  In Progress reshaped — explanatory intro paragraph\n  shows the dev-ladder format, and the active entry is\n  rewritten as the bulleted ladder with\n  `(done)` / `(current)` markers.\n\n### 0.41.0-4: capture --scope sum-type vocabulary\n\nNotes-only step. Mid-implementation rethink — the\noriginal `-4` (push --scope on top of `Vec<Side>`)\nexposed a vocabulary gap that the user surfaced\n2026-04-26: `code` plays two distinct roles depending on\ncontext (a *role* in a dual workspace; an implicit\n*fallback to cwd* in POR). Conflating them muddies what\n\"obvious to the user\" looks like. Splitting them honestly\nis a type-model change, not a per-command implementation\ndetail — so the cycle pivots: capture the refined\nvocabulary here, defer all command implementations to\n0.42.0, and close 0.41.0 cleanly.\n\n**Settled vocabulary.**\n\n- `--scope` (short form `-s`) accepts:\n  - `code` — the app side of a dual-repo workspace.\n  - `bot` — the bot side of a dual-repo workspace.\n  - `code,bot` / `bot,code` — both (commutative; same\n    semantic, same default).\n  - `<path>` — single-repo mode; ignore `.vc-config.toml`,\n    operate on the given path. Path values must be\n    prefixed (`./`, `/`, `~/`) to disambiguate from the\n    keyword set. Tilde expansion mirrors init's existing\n    behavior on `--repo-local` / `--repo-remote`.\n- No discoverable `single` keyword. `--scope=.` is the\n  cwd form; `--scope=/abs/path` or `--scope=./rel` is the\n  explicit form. Bare names that aren't keywords error\n  with a \"did you mean `./foo`?\" hint.\n\n**Internal type model.**\n\n```rust\npub enum Scope {\n    Roles(Vec<Side>), // code | bot | code,bot\n    Single(PathBuf),  // explicit path, single-repo mode\n}\n```\n\nReplaces today's `Scope(pub Vec<Side>)`. The current\n`has_code` / `has_bot` / `is_code_only` etc. helpers all\nshift to operate on `Roles` only — `Single(_)` is a\ndistinct mode, not a one-element role list.\n\n**Default-scope rules (revised).**\n\n| Workspace state | `.vc-config.toml` shape | Default scope |\n|---|---|---|\n| Dual workspace | `path` + non-empty `other-repo` | `Roles([Code, Bot])` |\n| Single-repo workspace | `path`, no `other-repo` | `Roles([Code])` |\n| POR | (no file) | `Single(cwd)` |\n\nThe single-repo-workspace concept stays — it's a real,\ndistinct state with a non-trivial default (`Roles([Code])`\nnot `Single(...)`). `--scope=bot` errors there with\n\"no other-repo configured.\"\n\n**Per-command applicability matrix.**\n\n| Command | `Roles` accepted | `Single(_)` accepted |\n|---|---|---|\n| `init` | yes | no — init creates workspaces, not POR repos |\n| `sync` | yes | yes |\n| `push` | yes | yes |\n| `finalize` | yes (currently has `--repo`; migrates to `--scope`) | yes |\n| `clone` | yes | yes (single-repo clone target) |\n| `validate-desc` | yes | **error** — validate compares two repos by definition; `Single` is meaningless |\n| `fix-desc` | yes | **error** (same reason as validate-desc) |\n| `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` | yes | yes |\n| `symlink` | n/a | n/a |\n\n`Single` is *always* available as the explicit\n\"ignore-the-config\" escape hatch on commands where it\napplies, even inside a dual workspace — the user\nexplicitly bypasses the dispatcher. On commands where it\ncan't apply (validate-desc / fix-desc), errors with a\nspecific message rather than silently doing the wrong\nthing.\n\n**Flag-surface changes.**\n\n- Drop `-R` / `--repo` everywhere. Currently sync has it\n  (mutually exclusive with `--scope`); finalize has its\n  own `--repo` (single repo). Both fold into the new\n  `--scope=<path>` form.\n- `--scope` gets short form `-s` for ergonomics.\n- The path form covers what `-R` / `--repo` did before.\n  Multi-repo (sync's old `-R . -R .claude` style) is\n  deliberately not preserved — that capability had no\n  real users beyond manually emulating today's default.\n\n**Rationale for the cycle pivot.**\n\n- The 0.41.0-3 capture committed to \"consistency\n  everywhere\", which means push, finalize, and the\n  remaining commands all need to agree on the type\n  model. Implementing push on the soon-to-be-changed\n  type would be churn.\n- The sum-type refactor touches scope.rs, common.rs,\n  init, sync, plus push and finalize at the same time.\n  That's a 0.42.0-sized cycle, not a sub-step inside\n  0.41.0.\n- Closing 0.41.0 here produces a clean checkpoint: the\n  scope foundation (0.40.0 init) and one consumer\n  (0.41.0-2 sync) are shipped, the direction is captured\n  in 0.41.0-3 and 0.41.0-4. 0.42.0 opens with the type\n  refactor as its `-0` plan.\n\nEdits:\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to `0.41.0-4` (notes-only step).\n- `src/push.rs`: revert the partial scope work that was\n  in flight (`--scope` flag on `PushArgs`, scope-related\n  imports). Push stays unchanged on main; its\n  scope wiring lands in 0.42.0 against the new type.\n- `notes/chores-06.md`: cycle-step sketch updated (0.41.0-3\n  marked shipped; 0.41.0-4 redefined as the sum-type\n  capture; 0.41.0-5 dropped from the cycle); this\n  subsection added; legacy default-scope rules marked\n  superseded.\n- `notes/todo.md`: dev-ladder updated (0.41.0-3 → done;\n  0.41.0-4 current; 0.41.0-5 removed; 0.41.0 close-out\n  noted).\n\n# References"
}

25	## Todo
26	
27	A markdown list of tasks to do in the near future, ordered
28	highest-priority first. Keep entries brief — 1-3 lines.
29	Detailed motivation, safety requirements, and ordering belong
30	in `notes/chores-NN.md` design subsections; link via `[N]` ref.
31	
32	Items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. `; the
33	markdown renderer auto-numbers them, so reorder/insert without
34	renumbering. Reference by displayed number ("let's work on #3").
35	1. vc-x1 push: `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` flag. Applies
36	   the generalized convention; warn on scope/WC
37	   mismatch. [57],[60],[71]
38	1. vc-x1 clone: `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` flag. Parallel
39	   to `init --scope`; supports single-repo clone for
40	   `vc-template-x1`-shape remotes. Symmetric with init's
41	   surface so users don't have to relearn it. [60],[71]
42	1. vc-x1 validate-desc / fix-desc: `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`
43	   flag. Same vocabulary as elsewhere; `code` validates
44	   code's commits against bot, `bot` reverses the
45	   direction, `code,bot` does both (new default). [60],[71]
46	1. CommonArgs sweep — add `--scope` to `chid`/`desc`/
47	   `list`/`show` in one cycle (single shared `CommonArgs`
48	   change picks all four up). Vocabulary-only — `-R` /
49	   `--repo` stays as the equivalent today. Low priority;
50	   bundle when the consistency benefit is felt. [60],[71]
51	1. Help layout: force over-under everywhere. Apply
52	   `next_line_help(true)` at the root (or via the existing
53	   `cli_with_banner` walker) so every subcommand's `-h` /
54	   `--help` uses the same layout. Today clap auto-picks
55	   per-command based on the widest flag spec, so
56	   `sync -h` is two-column but `init -h` is over-under —
57	   visual inconsistency.

  - New `### 0.41.0-4: capture --scope sum-type vocabulary` subsection — settled vocabulary, internal type model, revised default-scope rules, per-command applicability matrix, flag-surface changes (`-s/--scope`, drop `-R`), and rationale for the cycle pivot.
- **`notes/todo.md`**:
  - In Progress dev-ladder updated: `0.41.0-3` done, `0.41.0-4` current; `0.41.0-5` removed; close-out note explains 0.42.0 takes over.

Proposed commit:

**Title**:
```
docs: capture --scope sum-type vocabulary (0.41.0-4)
```

**Body**:
```
Mid-implementation pivot. The original 0.41.0-4 (push --scope on
the existing Vec<Side> shape) surfaced a vocabulary gap — `code`
plays two distinct roles depending on context (a workspace role
vs an implicit cwd fallback in POR). Splitting them honestly is
a type-model change, not a per-command implementation detail —
so the cycle pivots: capture the refined vocabulary, defer all
command implementations to 0.42.0, and close 0.41.0 cleanly.

- Cargo.toml: bump to 0.41.0-4.
- src/push.rs: revert the partial scope work (imports +
  `--scope` flag on PushArgs). Push stays unchanged on main.
- notes/chores-06.md: [71] cycle-steps updated (0.41.0-3
  shipped; 0.41.0-4 redefined; 0.41.0-5 removed); legacy
  default-scope rules marked superseded; new ### 0.41.0-4
  subsection records vocabulary, sum-type model, revised
  defaults, per-command applicability matrix, flag-surface
  changes (-s/--scope, drop -R), and the cycle-pivot
  rationale.
- notes/todo.md: dev-ladder updated for the new shape;
  four --scope follow-up entries (push, clone, validate-desc
  /fix-desc, CommonArgs sweep) updated to the new vocabulary
  and pointed at the 0.42.0 cycle.
```

Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit and push.

The tradeoff: restarting at `[1]` keeps chores-07 self-contained and easier to read in isolation, but `todo.md`'s reference list is a single global namespace spanning all chores files (today: `[4]` → chores-01, `[71]` → chores-06, etc.) — if chores-07 also has its own `[1]`, citations from `todo.md` become ambiguous unless we either (a) prefix with the file (e.g. `[07-1]`) or (b) keep two parallel namespaces (file-local for cross-references inside a chores file, global in todo.md). Continuing from `[73]` preserves the current single-namespace convention with zero collisions.

My recommendation: continue from `[73]`+ unless you want to take the broader convention change (file-local refs everywhere, with file-prefixed cites in todo.md). The latter is cleaner long-term but is a sweep across all existing references, not just chores-07.

```
1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	 
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore.
19	
20	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
21	
22	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
23	and using `jj-lib`
24	```
25	
26	## Workflow and conventions
27	
28	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
29	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
30	
31	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
32	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
33	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
34	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
35	  stuck detection.
36	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
37	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
38	
39	## Todo format
40	
41	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
42	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
43	references.
44	
45	Todo items use lazy numbering — every entry begins with `1. ` and the
46	markdown renderer auto-numbers them. Reorder or insert items without
47	renumbering, and reference an entry by its displayed number ("let's
48	work on #3"). The Done section keeps `-` bullets — items aren't
49	referenced by number once completed.
50	
51	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
52	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
53	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
54	
55	Examples:
56	
57	# Todo
58	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
59	- Fix bug Y [1]
60	
61	# Done
62	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
63	
64	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
65	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
66	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
67	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/README.md",
  "old_string": "# Done\n- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]\n\n[1]: bugs.md#bug-y\n[2]: issues.md#issue-z\n[3]: fixes.md#fix-z",
  "new_string": "# Done\n- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]\n\n[1]: bugs.md#bug-y\n[2]: issues.md#issue-z\n[3]: fixes.md#fix-z\n\n## Reference numbering\n\nEvery note file (`todo.md`, `chores-NN.md`, `done.md`) keeps a\nfile-local `# References` section at the bottom. Reference numbers\nare scoped to that file — `[1]` in `chores-07.md` and `[1]` in\n`chores-01.md` are independent slots that may point at completely\ndifferent URLs. New chores files start their numbering at `[1]`.\n\n## Retiring Done entries\n\n`todo.md`'s `## Done` section is a rolling buffer of recently shipped\nwork, not a permanent log. Move entries into `done.md` at two natural\nbeats:\n\n- **Closing a ladder** — when the final `X.Y.Z` (no suffix) commit\n  ships, decide which prior entries are no longer needed for nearby\n  context and migrate them.\n- **Opening a new ladder** — at `X.Y.Z-0`, do the same sweep before\n  bumping the version.\n\nMigration mechanics:\n\n- Move the bullet itself from `todo.md > ## Done` to\n  `done.md` (preserving the original ref number).\n- Copy any references the moved entries cite into\n  `done.md`'s `# References` section (those refs are file-local,\n  so coexisting with `todo.md`'s namespace is fine).\n- Prune any references in `todo.md > # References` no longer cited\n  by anything in `## In Progress` / `## Todo` / `## Done`. This\n  frees the numbers for future reuse."
}
```

28	  `notes/chores-07.md`. Notes only.
29	- **0.42.0-1** — `scope.rs` sum type. Internal only — no
30	  CLI changes yet, no consumers updated. Existing
31	  `Scope(Vec<Side>)` → `enum Scope { Roles(Vec<Side>),
32	  Single(PathBuf) }`. Helpers (`has_code`, `has_bot`,
33	  `is_code_only`, `is_both`, `is_empty`) shift to operate
34	  on `Roles` only; `Single(_)` is a distinct mode. Tests
35	  follow.
36	- **0.42.0-2** — custom CLI value parser for `--scope` +
37	  retrofit `init`. Parser handles the keyword set
38	  (`code|bot|code,bot|bot,code`) and the prefixed-path
39	  form (`./...`, `/...`, `~/...`). Bare names that aren't
40	  keywords error with a "did you mean `./foo`?" hint.
41	  Init is the smallest consumer (it already takes
42	  `--scope=code|bot|code,bot`); migrating it first proves
43	  the parser end-to-end before sync's more complex
44	  resolution logic comes onto the new shape.
45	- **0.42.0-3** — retrofit `sync`. Drop `-R/--repo`. Add
46	  `-s` short form. Default-scope rules update to the
47	  three-state model from [2]: dual workspace →
48	  `Roles([Code, Bot])`, single-repo workspace →
49	  `Roles([Code])`, POR → `Single(cwd)`. `scope_to_repos`
50	  in `common.rs` updates to handle the `Single(_)`
51	  variant. Update sync's integration tests where they
52	  exercise the resolver chain.
53	- **0.42.0-4** — `push --scope`. State machine becomes
54	  scope-aware: each stage either runs or no-ops based on
55	  scope. `Single(<path>)` mode means single-repo push
56	  (no `commit-claude` / `bookmark-claude` /
57	  `finalize-claude`, no `ochid:` trailer). `Roles(...)`
58	  modes work as captured in chores-06 [1]. Persist scope
59	  in `PushState` so resumes use the same scope. Add
60	  integration tests for each scope shape.
61	- **0.42.0-5** — `finalize --scope`. Replace the existing
62	  `--repo` flag with `--scope` (`Roles` for the dual case,
63	  `Single(<path>)` for the single-repo case). `--repo`
64	  goes away; per the 0.41.0-3 capture this is the
65	  intentional break, not a deprecation.
66	- **0.42.0-6** — `clone --scope`. Parallel to init —
67	  bootstrap with the appropriate scope. Single-repo clone
68	  via the path form for `vc-template-x1`-shape remotes.
69	- **0.42.0-7** — Single(_) dogfood validation. Apply the
70	  full `sync → edit → push` flow against a fresh POR or
71	  single-repo fixture (likely the `vc-template-x1` repo
72	  itself, since it's the canonical single-repo target).
73	  Surface and fix anything the unit tests miss.
74	- **0.42.0 (final)** — cycle close-out. Drops the
75	  `-N` suffix; updates `notes/todo.md`'s In Progress and
76	  Done sections; chores-07 close-out subsection.
77	
78	**Deferred to later cycles (per [2]).**
79	
80	- `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` `--scope`. Read-side
81	  commands; `Single(_)` errors there (validate compares
82	  two repos by definition).
83	- `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` — CommonArgs sweep.
84	  All four pick up `--scope` via a shared change to
85	  `CommonArgs`; existing `-R/--repo` retires there too.
86	- `--message-file PATH` for push. Independent push
87	  feature; gates the CLAUDE.md refresh from CLAUDE2.
88	
89	**References.** [2] points back at chores-06's
90	`### 0.41.0-4: capture --scope sum-type vocabulary` for
91	the full vocabulary, type-model, and per-command
92	applicability matrix. Read that subsection first before
93	diving into any of the `-N` steps below.
94

98	[2]: /notes/chores-06.md#0410-4-capture---scope-sum-type-vocabulary

```
1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` wraps the parsed list; a missing flag (None at the arg
5	//!   layer) means "caller decides", typically via workspace-state
6	//!   default resolution.
7	//! - See `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope across commands
8	//!   (0.40.0)` for the full rationale and the vocabulary history.
9	
10	use clap::ValueEnum;
11	
12	/// One side of the workspace.
13	///
14	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
15	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
16	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
17	pub enum Side {
18	    Code,
19	    Bot,
20	}
21	
22	/// Parsed `--scope` value: the set of sides the command applies to.
23	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
24	pub struct Scope(pub Vec<Side>);
25	
26	impl Scope {
27	    /// True when the scope includes the code side.
28	    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {
29	        self.0.contains(&Side::Code)
30	    }
31	
32	    /// True when the scope includes the bot side.
33	    pub fn has_bot(&self) -> bool {
34	        self.0.contains(&Side::Bot)
35	    }
36	
37	    /// Exactly the code side — single-repo operation.
38	    pub fn is_code_only(&self) -> bool {
39	        self.has_code() && !self.has_bot()
40	    }
41	
42	    /// Exactly the bot side — single-repo operation on the bot.
43	    pub fn is_bot_only(&self) -> bool {
44	        !self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
45	    }
46	
47	    /// Both sides — dual-repo operation.
48	    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {
49	        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
50	    }
51	
52	    /// Neither side — empty scope, always an invalid input.
53	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
54	        self.0.is_empty()
55	    }
56	}
57	
58	#[cfg(test)]
59	mod tests {
60	    use super::*;
61	
62	    #[test]
63	    fn code_only() {
64	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code]);
65	        assert!(s.is_code_only());
66	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
67	        assert!(!s.is_both());
68	    }
69	
70	    #[test]
71	    fn bot_only() {
72	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Bot]);
73	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
74	        assert!(s.is_bot_only());
75	        assert!(!s.is_both());
76	    }
77	
78	    #[test]
79	    fn both_code_then_bot() {
80	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]);
81	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
82	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
83	        assert!(s.is_both());
84	    }
85	
86	    #[test]
87	    fn both_bot_then_code() {
88	        // Order doesn't matter — contains-based checks.
89	        let s = Scope(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code]);
90	        assert!(s.is_both());
91	    }
92	
93	    #[test]
94	    fn empty() {
95	        let s = Scope(vec![]);
96	        assert!(s.is_empty());
97	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
98	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
99	        assert!(!s.is_both());
100	    }
101	}
102	
```

644	/// Resolve the workspace's default `--scope`.
645	///
646	/// Reads `<workspace_root>/.vc-config.toml > [workspace] other-repo`:
647	///
648	/// - non-empty `other-repo` → `Scope([Code, Bot])`
649	/// - missing / empty `other-repo` → `Scope([Code])`
650	/// - `workspace_root = None` (POR) → `Scope([Code])`
651	pub fn default_scope(workspace_root: Option<&Path>) -> Scope {
652	    let Some(root) = workspace_root else {
653	        return Scope(vec![Side::Code]);
654	    };
655	    let cfg = match toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE)) {
656	        Ok(c) => c,
657	        Err(_) => return Scope(vec![Side::Code]),
658	    };
659	    match toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo") {
660	        Some(v) if !v.is_empty() => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
661	        _ => Scope(vec![Side::Code]),
662	    }
663	}
664	
665	/// Resolve a `Scope` to concrete repo paths.
666	///
667	/// - `Side::Code` → `workspace_root` (or cwd's `.` when `workspace_root` is None).
668	/// - `Side::Bot` → `workspace_root.join(other-repo)`.
669	///
670	/// Errors when `Side::Bot` is requested but the workspace doesn't define
671	/// one (POR or single-repo workspace).
672	pub fn scope_to_repos(
673	    scope: &Scope,
674	    workspace_root: Option<&Path>,
675	) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
676	    let mut repos = Vec::new();
677	    for side in &scope.0 {
678	        match side {
679	            Side::Code => repos.push(
680	                workspace_root
681	                    .map(Path::to_path_buf)
682	                    .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(".")),
683	            ),
684	            Side::Bot => {
685	                let root = workspace_root.ok_or(
686	                    "--scope=bot: not in a vc-x1 workspace (no .vc-config.toml with path = \"/\") — drop --scope or use --scope=code",
687	                )?;
688	                let cfg = toml_simple::toml_load(&root.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
689	                let other = toml_simple::toml_get(&cfg, "workspace.other-repo")

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/scope.rs",
  "content": "//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.\n//!\n//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.\n//! - `Scope` is a sum type:\n//!   - `Roles(Vec<Side>)` — dual-repo workspace; the parsed\n//!     keyword form (`code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`).\n//!   - `Single(PathBuf)` — explicit single-repo mode; ignores\n//!     `.vc-config.toml` and operates on the one repo at the\n//!     given path. Wired up incrementally (parser lands in\n//!     0.42.0-2, consumers in later steps).\n//! - Helpers (`has_code`, `is_both`, etc.) reflect the `Roles`\n//!   arm only; on `Single(_)` they all return `false` /\n//!   `is_empty()` returns `false`. Callers that must\n//!   distinguish modes match on the enum directly.\n//! - See `notes/chores-07.md > --scope sum-type refactor (0.42.0)`\n//!   for the migration plan, and `notes/chores-06.md > 0.41.0-4`\n//!   for the vocabulary capture.\n\nuse clap::ValueEnum;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\n/// One side of a dual-repo workspace.\n///\n/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.\n/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).\n#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]\npub enum Side {\n    Code,\n    Bot,\n}\n\n/// Parsed `--scope` value.\n///\n/// - `Roles(_)` — dual-repo workspace; the requested side set.\n/// - `Single(_)` — explicit single-repo mode at the given path.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Scope {\n    Roles(Vec<Side>),\n    Single(PathBuf),\n}\n\nimpl Scope {\n    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the code side.\n    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {\n        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.contains(&Side::Code))\n    }\n\n    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the bot side.\n    pub fn has_bot(&self) -> bool {\n        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.contains(&Side::Bot))\n    }\n\n    /// Roles-only with exactly the code side — single-side dual-repo op.\n    pub fn is_code_only(&self) -> bool {\n        self.has_code() && !self.has_bot()\n    }\n\n    /// Roles-only with exactly the bot side — single-side dual-repo op.\n    pub fn is_bot_only(&self) -> bool {\n        !self.has_code() && self.has_bot()\n    }\n\n    /// Roles arm including both sides — full dual-repo op.\n    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {\n        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()\n    }\n\n    /// Roles arm with an empty side list — always invalid input.\n    /// `Single(_)` returns `false` (it carries a path, not a side list).\n    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.is_empty())\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn code_only() {\n        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]);\n        assert!(s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bot_only() {\n        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot]);\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn both_code_then_bot() {\n        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]);\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(s.is_both());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn both_bot_then_code() {\n        // Order doesn't matter — contains-based checks.\n        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code]);\n        assert!(s.is_both());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn empty_roles() {\n        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![]);\n        assert!(s.is_empty());\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn single_helpers_all_false() {\n        // `Single(_)` is a distinct mode; the Roles-shaped helpers\n        // all report false / not-empty so callers that match on\n        // helpers naturally fall through to mode-aware logic.\n        let s = Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"/some/repo\"));\n        assert!(!s.has_code());\n        assert!(!s.has_bot());\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n        assert!(!s.is_empty());\n    }\n}\n"
}

100	/// Precedence: explicit `-R` → `--scope` → workspace-default scope.
101	/// See [`SyncArgs`] for the full contract.
102	fn resolve_args_to_repos(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
103	    if !args.repos.is_empty() {
104	        return Ok(split_repos(&args.repos));
105	    }
106	    let workspace_root = find_workspace_root();
107	    let scope = match &args.scope {
108	        Some(sides) if sides.is_empty() => return Err("--scope: value is empty".into()),
109	        Some(sides) => Scope(sides.clone()),
110	        None => default_scope(workspace_root.as_deref()),
111	    };
112	    scope_to_repos(&scope, workspace_root.as_deref())
113	}

595	/// - Validate flag mutual-exclusion.
596	/// - Resolve URLs and paths; derive NAME from URL if not given.
597	/// - Pick the provisioner.
598	/// - Errors on any ambiguous or incomplete flag combination.
599	pub(crate) fn plan_init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<InitPlan, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
600	    // --- Resolve scope first: default (None) → dual; empty Vec is
601	    // invalid; `bot` alone is meaningless at init time. ---
602	    let scope = match &args.scope {
603	        None => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
604	        Some(sides) => {
605	            let s = Scope(sides.clone());
606	            if s.is_empty() {
607	                return Err("--scope: value is empty".into());
608	            }
609	            if s.is_bot_only() {
610	                return Err(
611	                    "--scope=bot is meaningless at init time — use --scope=code for single-repo or --scope=code,bot (default) for dual-repo"
612	                        .into(),
613	                );
614	            }
615	            s
616	        }
617	    };
618

> AGENT

Work complete for 0.42.0-1.

**Cargo.toml** — `0.42.0-0` → `0.42.0-1`.

**src/scope.rs** — full rewrite: `enum Scope { Roles(Vec<Side>), Single(PathBuf) }`. Helpers (`has_code`, `has_bot`, `is_code_only`, `is_bot_only`, `is_both`, `is_empty`) implemented with `matches!` so they reflect the `Roles` arm and return `false`/not-empty on `Single(_)`. `#[allow(dead_code)]` on the `Single` variant covers the staging window. Added `single_helpers_all_false` test; existing tests migrated to `Scope::Roles(...)`.

**src/common.rs** — `default_scope` returns `Scope::Roles(_)`. `scope_to_repos` matches on the enum and returns an explicit `0.42.0-3`-staging error on `Single(_)`. New test `scope_to_repos_single_errors_until_0420_3` pins that contract. Existing tests migrated to `Scope::Roles(...)`.

**src/sync.rs** — `resolve_args_to_repos` builds `Scope::Roles(sides.clone())`. Integration-test constructors migrated.

**src/init.rs** — `plan_init` constructs `Scope::Roles(...)` for default + explicit-flag paths. Helper call sites untouched.

**notes/chores-07.md** — added `### 0.42.0-1: scope.rs sum type` subsection.

**notes/todo.md** — In Progress: `-0 (done)`, `-1 (current)`.

Pre-commit clean: `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings`, 257 tests pass (255 baseline + 2 new), `cargo install --locked`, `vc-x1 --version` → `0.42.0-1`.

Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body.

> DEVELOPER

Why use "sum type" why not just say Scope is an enum or similar

> AGENT

Fair point — "sum type" is type-theory jargon; "enum" is what Rust developers actually say. The original framing came from describing the *shift* (one shape → tagged union), but "Scope becomes an enum with two variants" carries the same information without the decoder ring.

Currently appears in:
- `notes/chores-07.md` — section title `## --scope sum-type refactor (0.42.0)` + the -1 subsection prose + the closing reference paragraph
- `notes/chores-06.md` — `### 0.41.0-4: capture --scope sum-type vocabulary`
- `notes/todo.md` — In Progress ladder line, plus my just-pushed close-out commit body
- `Cargo.lock`/commit history (immutable)

10	pub struct InitArgs {
11	    /// Project name (directory and GitHub repo name).

13	    /// - Optional with `--repo-remote` (derived from URL's last
14	    ///   path segment, trailing `.git` stripped).

45	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 5)]
46	    pub push_retries: u32,
47	
48	    /// Seconds between push retries [default: 3]
49	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 3)]
50	    pub push_retry_delay: u64,
51	
52	    /// Seed repos from template directories.
53	    ///
54	    /// Value is `CODE[,BOT]`. Default bot path is `<CODE>.claude`
55	    /// (file-name concat, not path join — templates are siblings).
56	    ///
57	    /// - With `--scope=code`: only `CODE` is used; passing `,BOT`
58	    ///   is fatal (no session side to seed).
59	    /// - Non-hidden contents copied recursively; hidden entries
60	    ///   (names starting with `.`) are skipped — init writes its
61	    ///   own hidden files.
62	    /// - If a copied tree has a `README.md`, its first line is
63	    ///   rewritten to `# <repo-name>`.
64	    #[arg(long, value_name = "CODE[,BOT]", verbatim_doc_comment)]
65	    pub use_template: Option<String>,

67	    /// Fixture mode — fully self-contained local workspace.

73	    /// - Bare origins at `<PARENT>/remote-code.git` and
74	    ///   `<PARENT>/remote-claude.git`.
75	    /// - No `gh`, no network.

77	    /// - Exclusive with `--repo-remote`, `--dir`, `--owner`, `--private`.
78	    #[arg(long, value_name = "PARENT", verbatim_doc_comment)]
79	    pub repo_local: Option<String>,

85	    /// - GitHub URLs → `gh repo create` (same as default).
86	    /// - Other URLs → caller pre-creates the remote; init only pushes.
87	    /// - Supports `~/…`, `$VAR`, `${VAR}` in path-shaped values.
88	    /// - Exclusive with `--repo-local`, `--owner`, `--private`.

108	/// Run a command with retries, sleeping between attempts.
109	fn run_retry(
110	    cmd: &str,
111	    args: &[&str],
112	    cwd: &Path,
113	    retries: u32,
114	    delay_secs: u64,
115	) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
116	    let mut last_err = String::new();
117	    for attempt in 1..=retries {
118	        match run(cmd, args, cwd) {
119	            Ok(out) => {
120	                if attempt > 1 {
121	                    debug!("succeeded after {attempt} attempts");
122	                }
123	                return Ok(out);
124	            }
125	            Err(e) => {
126	                last_err = e.to_string();
127	                if attempt < retries {
128	                    debug!("attempt {attempt}/{retries} failed: {last_err}");
129	                    debug!("retrying in {delay_secs}s...");

374	/// Expand `~` and `$VAR` / `${VAR}` substitutions in a user string.
375	///
376	/// - `~` and `~/…` resolve via `HOME`.
377	/// - Unset env vars are a fatal error (silent empty substitution
378	///   would mask typos).
379	/// - Bare `$` with no identifier after stays literal.
380	pub(crate) fn expand_vars(s: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
381	    let home_expanded = if s == "~" {
382	        std::env::var("HOME").map_err(|_| "HOME is not set; cannot expand '~'")?
383	    } else if let Some(rest) = s.strip_prefix("~/") {
384	        let home = std::env::var("HOME").map_err(|_| "HOME is not set; cannot expand '~/'")?;
385	        format!("{home}/{rest}")
386	    } else {
387	        s.to_string()
388	    };
389	
390	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(home_expanded.len());
391	    let mut chars = home_expanded.chars().peekable();
392	    while let Some(c) = chars.next() {
393	        if c != '$' {
394	            out.push(c);
395	            continue;
396	        }
397	        let (name, braced) = if chars.peek() == Some(&'{') {
398	            chars.next(); // OK: peeked '{' above, next() must yield it
399	            let mut name = String::new();

{

  "old_string": "impl Scope {\n    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the code side.\n    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {",
  "new_string": "/// Parse a `--scope` value string.\n///\n/// Accepted forms:\n///\n/// - Keyword: exactly one of `code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`\n///   → `Scope::Roles(...)`. Order is preserved.\n/// - Path: starts with `./`, `../`, `/`, `~/`, or is the bare `~`\n///   → `Scope::Single(PathBuf)`. The raw string is stored; tilde\n///   and `$VAR` expansion happens at the consumer (see\n///   `init::expand_vars`).\n///\n/// Anything else — including a bare unprefixed name like `foo`, an\n/// empty string, or an unrecognized keyword combination — is an\n/// error. The error message hints at the path-prefix requirement\n/// so users see the disambiguation rule when they mistype.\npub fn parse_scope(s: &str) -> Result<Scope, String> {\n    if s == \"~\"\n        || s.starts_with(\"./\")\n        || s.starts_with(\"../\")\n        || s.starts_with('/')\n        || s.starts_with(\"~/\")\n    {\n        return Ok(Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(s)));\n    }\n    match s {\n        \"code\" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])),\n        \"bot\" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot])),\n        \"code,bot\" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])),\n        \"bot,code\" => Ok(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code])),\n        \"\" => Err(\"--scope: value is empty\".into()),\n        other => Err(format!(\n            \"--scope: '{other}' is not a recognized form. \\\n             Expected one of `code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`, \\\n             or a path (`./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`). \\\n             Bare names without `./` are reserved for keywords; \\\n             prefix paths with `./` to disambiguate.\"\n        )),\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Scope {\n    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the code side.\n    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {"
}

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn single_helpers_all_false() {\n        // `Single(_)` is a distinct mode; the Roles-shaped helpers\n        // all report false / not-empty so callers that match on\n        // helpers naturally fall through to mode-aware logic.\n        let s = Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"/some/repo\"));\n        assert!(!s.has_code());\n        assert!(!s.has_bot());\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n        assert!(!s.is_empty());\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn single_helpers_all_false() {\n        // `Single(_)` is a distinct mode; the Roles-shaped helpers\n        // all report false / not-empty so callers that match on\n        // helpers naturally fall through to mode-aware logic.\n        let s = Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"/some/repo\"));\n        assert!(!s.has_code());\n        assert!(!s.has_bot());\n        assert!(!s.is_code_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());\n        assert!(!s.is_both());\n        assert!(!s.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    // ---------- parse_scope ----------\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_keyword_code() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_scope(\"code\").unwrap(), Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_keyword_bot() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_scope(\"bot\").unwrap(), Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot]));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_keyword_code_bot_preserves_order() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"code,bot\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_keyword_bot_code_preserves_order() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"bot,code\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot, Side::Code])\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_path_dotslash() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"./foo\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"./foo\"))\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_path_dotdotslash() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"../sibling\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"../sibling\"))\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_path_absolute() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"/abs/path\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"/abs/path\"))\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_path_tilde_slash() {\n        // Stored raw; the consumer expands with `expand_vars`.\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"~/work/x\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"~/work/x\"))\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_path_bare_tilde() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            parse_scope(\"~\").unwrap(),\n            Scope::Single(PathBuf::from(\"~\"))\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_bare_name_errors_with_hint() {\n        let err = parse_scope(\"foo\").unwrap_err();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"'foo'\"), \"got: {err}\");\n        assert!(err.contains(\"./X\") || err.contains(\"./\"), \"got: {err}\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_empty_errors() {\n        let err = parse_scope(\"\").unwrap_err();\n        assert!(err.contains(\"empty\"), \"got: {err}\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_unknown_keyword_combo_errors() {\n        // Duplicates and out-of-set combos are rejected; only the\n        // exact four keyword forms are accepted.\n        assert!(parse_scope(\"code,code\").is_err());\n        assert!(parse_scope(\"code,bot,code\").is_err());\n        assert!(parse_scope(\"bot,bot\").is_err());\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "    let scope = match &args.scope {\n        None => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),\n        Some(sides) => {\n            let s = Scope::Roles(sides.clone());\n            if s.is_empty() {\n                return Err(\"--scope: value is empty\".into());\n            }\n            if s.is_bot_only() {\n                return Err(\n                    \"--scope=bot is meaningless at init time — use --scope=code for single-repo or --scope=code,bot (default) for dual-repo\"\n                        .into(),\n                );\n            }\n            s\n        }\n    };",
  "new_string": "    let scope = match &args.scope {\n        None => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),\n        Some(s @ Scope::Roles(_)) => {\n            if s.is_bot_only() {\n                return Err(\n                    \"--scope=bot is meaningless at init time — use --scope=code for single-repo or --scope=code,bot (default) for dual-repo\"\n                        .into(),\n                );\n            }\n            s.clone()\n        }\n        Some(Scope::Single(p)) => {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"--scope={}: path-form scope is meaningless at init time — use --repo-local <PATH> for a local fixture or --repo-remote <URL> for an existing remote\",\n                p.display()\n            )\n            .into());\n        }\n    };"
}
